# Changelog

All notable changes are recorded here, following [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/)
and [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/). The release workflow refuses a tag whose version
has no section below.

## [Unreleased]

### Fixed
- Events: `HttpForwarder` treats a `3xx` it does not follow (a cross-origin redirect, or one urllib will not follow for a POST) as a configuration fault (`MisconfiguredError`, the event stays pending until the URL is fixed) instead of a permanent refusal that marked the event `rejected`; the forwarder contract in docs/events.md says so.
- Health and restart correctness (review 2, F19, F20, F36). `compose_app` health fails on an empty `docker compose ps` (it used to loop over nothing and pass) and when a service of `compose_app_spec` has no container, and reads both the one-object-per-line and the single-array output formats. `vllm_server` restarts an instance whose API key file was rewritten (the engines read `EnvironmentFile` only at start, so a rotated key left them on the old one and the gateway got 401s), and retiring an instance removes its API key file and cache directory. `wazuh_agent` health reads the agent's `ossec.conf` (at `<home>/etc/ossec.conf`, the home being three levels above `wazuh_agent_state_file`) and fails when no `<address>` in it is the registry's manager (the manager is handed to the package at the first install only; addresses inside XML comments do not count). `compose_app` health accepts only `Health: healthy`, so a container without a healthcheck (drifted from the spec) fails. Retiring a `vllm_server` instance removes its key file and cache only when the instance name is a valid slug. Clearing `vllm_server_api_key_ref` leaves the old key files on disk (follow-up). **Breaking for overlay sites:** `compose_app` health now requires `compose_app_spec`; `wazuh_agent` health now requires `wazuh_agent_manager_id`, so an agent enrolled with a manager other than the registry's fails health until it is reinstalled or the registry is corrected.
- The `lynis` health hook fails when the last audit exited non-zero (it read the exit status and ignored it), when `report.dat` is missing or older than the last run by more than `lynis_max_run_seconds` (new, default 3600; the scan now removes the previous report before auditing), and when `dpkg-verify.json` is missing or older than the last run (a failed sweep leaves the old result in place). The broker filing is named `<YYYY-MM-DD>-lynis-<host>.md`: the old `lynis-<host>.md` never matched the example findings lanes' `filename_pattern`, so the broker refused every filing and `|| true` hid it (the refusal is now printed to stderr). **Breaking for overlay sites:** a findings lane whose `filename_pattern` does not accept the date-prefixed name refuses the lynis filing; an audit that exits non-zero now fails health.
- The `clamav` role stats the guest's freshclam configuration instead of testing the controller (`is file`), so clearing `clamav_private_mirror` removes the `PrivateMirror` line on the guest, and a changed line restarts `clamav-freshclam` through a new handler (freshclam reads its configuration only at start). The scan script already records a clean exit when none of the configured paths exist (`ansible-a-l--practice--6`): that is the documented "nothing to scan" case, left as is.
- Approvals (review 2, F16; ADR-0034 decision 4, amended 2026-11-06): the person is named by one claim, the IdP's `preferred_username` (`homelabnext.edge_identity.USERNAME_CLAIM`), across the hub, the auth-proxy contract and the SSH CA. The status hub's private tier and Approvals page read `X-Auth-Request-Preferred-Username` (the reference auth proxy's copy of the claim under `--set-xauthrequest`, now the declared `IdentityHeaders.username`), not `X-Auth-Request-User`, which under the documented flags carries the proxy's user-id claim (an email, or `sub`): every approve and deny through the documented setup was `403` (not a canonical approver subject), so no gated broker action could be approved. The generated edge snippet copies the header too (it already carried the mark, so the client-sent copy is still stripped in every spelling), the generated realm sets `editUsernameAllowed: false` explicitly and maps the username into `preferred_username` on every client (a `username` protocol mapper), so the claim no longer hangs on the optional `profile` scope a proxy's `--scope` may drop, the lab-in-a-box broker scenario's edge sends the reference proxy's header set (built from `IDENTITY_HEADERS`, the user-id header email-shaped), and a test drives an approve with the reference proxy's headers through the config the hub really loads. **Breaking for overlay sites:** an edge that does not use the generated `hnx_forward_auth` snippet must copy `X-Auth-Request-Preferred-Username` (a hub behind one that copies only `X-Auth-Request-User` sees no identity and answers `403`); `hnx ssh-ca` refuses an `oidc.user_claim` other than `preferred_username`; `agents.yml` `private.identity_header: X-Auth-Request-User` is still taken, with a warning, and not obeyed; `IdentityHeaders` takes a required `username` header; a non-default IdP must carry the username in `preferred_username`, and an overlay must not enable self-registration or an identity-brokering provider that lets a user choose their username. The frozen `status-agents` 1.4 schema's `identity_header` description still names `X-Auth-Request-User` until the next schema version (a developer follow-up in `docs/status.md`).
- Status hub: `ingest` keeps the stored reading when a pushed observation is older than it (a late or replayed event used to replace a newer state); an equal or newer one still replaces it, and the source still counts as heard from. The OpenProject webhook receiver answers 500 when the feed fails on a delivery instead of dropping the connection.
- Docs: the OpenProject change feed and webhook receiver, and push-event status sources (`StatusHub.ingest_event`, `status.observed`), are marked stage 2, library only: no command, unit or publisher runs them yet. The wiring is tracked as developer follow-ups in `docs/projects.md` and `docs/status.md`.
- The `trivy` role's CycloneDX SBOM run takes the same options as the scan (`--severity`, `--skip-db-update`, `--db-repository`), so an offline guest or a mirror is honoured on that line too; the test asserts the full command line.
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the `trivy` role pins `trivy_version` to `0.76.1` (tagged 2026-10-21, past the 14-day cooldown), installs exactly that package and always holds it; an empty `trivy_version` (the repository's newest, unheld) is now refused for every profile, and `tools/pin_freshness.py` tracks the pin.
- Novelty docs (`docs/novelty.md`) list `burst` among the statuses `corroborate` groups as signals, as the code does, and ADR-0036 decision 3 says a burst is a signal only for an entity quiet while learning: an entity noisy in both windows is reported once as `high_cardinality`, not as a signal (behaviour unchanged, now documented and pinned by a test). A doc test holds the documented signal set to `corroborate.SIGNALS`.
- `hnx invariants run --notify-cmd` is refused (usage error, exit 2) without `--notify-ceiling`; it used to page at a default `red` ceiling, an undeclared reader (ADR-0033 decision 6, clarification amended 2026-11-06). The stdout pager keeps `red`. **Breaking for overlay sites:** an invariants timer that passes `--notify-cmd` with no `--notify-ceiling` must add the flag (`red` keeps today's behaviour). Only the flag declares the ceiling: `run` takes no registry, so the registry notify row is not consulted. The check also fires without `--state`.
- Registry (`homelabnext.registry.model.semantic_problems`): a public route needs auth. **Breaking for overlay sites:** a `services.yml` row with a `public_hostname` and no `auth` (the schema default `none`) or `auth: none` is refused with `a public route needs auth`, the message a module's rows already get (`modules/rules.py`); the edge used to publish it with no forward auth, so the app was open to anyone who reached the edge. `auth: mtls` on a public row is refused too: the edge terminates TLS and adds no client-certificate check, so it was never enforced. A module's public rows meet the same rule (`modules/rules.py` now refuses `auth: mtls` there too, so `hnx modules` lint and `hnx registry validate` agree). `auth: own` and `auth: idp` still pass (ADR-0034, note on decision 3; `own` is an unchecked claim a reviewer verifies against the app). Zone CIDRs that overlap (the same family) are refused (`zone <b>: cidr ... overlaps zone <a>`), and duplicate host addresses are compared as addresses, so two spellings of one IPv6 address are caught; an IPv4-mapped IPv6 zone CIDR (inside `::ffff:0:0/96`) or host address is refused, as it names an IPv4 address a second time and got past both checks. The minimal example's `edge-proxy` row (the front door itself) no longer has a `public_hostname`, which published a login-less site proxying the edge to itself. Before upgrading an overlay, run `hnx registry validate` on its `services.yml` and give each reported row `auth: idp` (with `roles`) or `auth: own`, or drop its `public_hostname`.
- Lake readers sign in to the query engine (ADR-0033 decision 6; `docs/modules/tlp.md#engine-sign-in`). A reader used to be named only by its `X-Trino-User` header, so the engine's row filters held only for honest clients, and the lake adapter could not send a credential. **Breaking for overlay sites:** `homelabnext.conform.adapters.lake.make_lake` refuses a `lake-query` endpoint that is not loopback unless it is given `credential=`, a secret reference (`secret:<name>`, conventionally `reader_secret(user)`, the secret `lake-query/readers/<login>`) to the reader's password in the engine's per-reader password file; there is no override. With it the engine client sends HTTP Basic for the reader's login (`trino_http.engine_login`: the reader id's first `:` as `.`, which the engine maps back to the reader id; `trino_http.password_auth`) on every request, the `nextUri` reads included; `catalog_credential=` sends the catalog a bearer token, `ca_file=` names a private CA, and both clients then send through `credential_opener` (no redirect, no proxy). `TrinoHTTP` takes `opener=`. `hnx novelty --domain` signs in as `analyzer:novelty` with `secret:lake-query/readers/analyzer.novelty` (`HNX_SECRET_LAKE_QUERY_READERS_ANALYZER_NOVELTY`; `--credential`, `--catalog-credential`, `--ca-file`), and the swap rehearsal takes `--user` (default `hnx-conform`) and `--credential`. A credential goes over https only, except to loopback, even with `allow_plaintext=True`, and a catalog token a header cannot carry (a trailing newline) is refused naming its secret, never quoting it. **Breaking for overlay sites:** `lake-access.json` now carries `impersonation` denying everyone the right to act as another user, so a signed-in reader cannot name a `red` reader in its user header and read that reader's rows. The loopback lab-in-a-box is unchanged. The engine's own set-up (password authenticator, password file, a user-mapping rules file with an identity fallback for `hnx-conform`, no impersonation, a catalog that authenticates and vends storage credentials to the engine only) is an operator step in `TODO.md` §4.
- Reader-to-actor handoff (`homelabnext.agent.handoff`, ADR-0033 decisions 4 and 6): the reader's output now keeps its TLP label. **Breaking for overlay sites:** `declaration_problems` refuses a handoff whose actor's receive ceiling (`readers.actor_ceiling`: the lower of what its `data_tier` lets it receive and its declared `tlp_ceiling`, `clear` when that is not a canonical label, so a `cloud-allowed` actor declaring `red`, `TLP:RED` or a non-label never reads as receiving `red`) is below what the reader may hold (`readers.held_label`: its receive ceiling, `red` when it states neither), and a reader or actor whose `tlp_ceiling` is not a canonical label agreeing with its `data_tier` (`readers.tlp_ceiling_problems`), so a `red`-ceiling reader can no longer hand off to a `clear` (`cloud-allowed`) actor; `accept` takes two new required keywords, `reader_profile=` and `actor_profile=` (ids must be the handoff's), labels the output with the strictest of the reader's held label, the pack's label (`pack_label`) and any TLP marker in the output, and refuses an output above the actor's ceiling and either profile's unsound `tlp_ceiling`. `Accepted` carries the label (`label`), and `build_request` sets it on the broker request as `tlp`. `broker/request` 1.1 adds the optional `tlp` (canonical labels only), and `request_digest` binds it when present, so a request without one keeps its digest and an approval does not survive a lowered label. A red reader's output used to reach a clear actor unlabelled. The missing per-request `model_tlp.admit` call site and what it waits on are recorded as a developer follow-up in `docs/modules/egress.md`, with a second one for the unenforced request `tlp` (a handler that sends params out of the lab must apply `egress_check` to it, a missing `tlp` reading as `red`).
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** a module manifest whose `lifecycle.remove.audit` names a plane that is neither a registered `hnx decommission` plane nor `contributions` is now refused by the loader (it loaded and silently audited less than declared).
- The module `licence` probe (warn at 30 days, page at 7, red on expiry) is marked "specified, not implemented" in `docs/modules.md`, the manifest schema and the follow-up list: nothing reads `licence.expires`.
- `hnx new schema` writes the layout X-03 checks (`examples/<name>.valid.json`, `negative/<name>.*.json`, a version-pinned `$id`), and its verify runs the X-03 check. `hnx new` names are matched in full (a trailing newline is refused), and `hnx new role` outside a checkout fails cleanly before writing files.
- The decommission `secret-names` plane no longer lists a sibling guest's secrets for deletion (key `app` matched `app_core_*`): a name that also belongs to another known guest (snapshot `guests`, or a `host_vars` file) is reported `AMBIGUOUS` for review.
  The most specific key wins: a target's own secrets (`app-core`'s `app_core_*` with a sibling guest `app`) stay firm remnants, `host_vars/<guest>/*.yml` names the guest, and a non-list snapshot `guests` is refused.
- The `contributions` audit plane is marked "specified, not implemented" in the manifest schema, the swap flow and the follow-up list; the loader still accepts the name.
- Agent context packs (`homelabnext.agent.pack`): **Breaking for overlay sites:** a `source: input` section declared `tier: stable` is refused by `assemble_pack`, `session_problems` (so `start_session`) and `manifest_problems` under the `readers` rule (so `hnx agent lint`); a stable section is neither nonce-fenced nor escaped, so input there could forge an `instructions [stable]` section. Custom fence delimiters are folded with `fence_key` before a line is compared with them, so input cannot slip past escaping with a full-width, space-led, zero-width-laced or accented spelling of the manifest's delimiter; `fence_key` now folds with NFKD, so a precomposed accented letter and its decomposed spelling read the same, and trailing whitespace in a delimiter is ignored when matching. **Breaking for overlay sites:** a manifest or prompt-template delimiter that holds a line break, or reads as fewer than 4 visible characters, is refused (`pack.delimiter_problems`: by `assemble_pack`, `session_problems`, the `readers` lint rule and the prompt-template lint); `hnx agent lint` reports both refusals under `readers` and again under `sessions` for each profile the manifest names. Pre-gate outputs are now checked before use: each must match `agent/pregate_output` (an unknown status fails), its `gate` must be the id it is filed under, and with `max_age_s` its `observed_at` must be no older than that and no more than 300 s ahead, at the clock `assemble_pack(..., now=)` (a callable returning an aware datetime, else `SpecError`; default the current UTC time), which `start_session(..., now=)` passes through and `hnx agent pack --now <RFC 3339 time>` sets, so a recorded pack can be reproduced. An output that fails a check is not rendered: a required gate's defers the pack, an optional gate's is listed in `omitted`. A stale pre-gate file still saying `ok` used to give a ready pack. No schema change.
- `hnx guests`: `load_keys` refuses a `--keys` value that holds private key material or is not a single-line OpenSSH public key (the message never echoes the value); `render --target cloudinit --out` refuses ssh key names the keys file does not resolve instead of writing `<ssh-key:NAME>` into user-data, and `validate --keys` checks the names. The stdout render keeps the placeholder. A keys-file YAML error reports only its position (PyYAML quotes the source, which may be a pasted key), key names must match `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]{1,64}` and are unique, the comment after a key is printable ASCII, and the name check covers only the VMs cloud-init renders. Breaking for overlay sites: a keys file with non-public-key values, or a cloud-init guest naming an absent key, now fails.
- Tests: the real-tool checks may not skip silently in CI. A new `real-tools` job installs nftables and dnsmasq, allows unprivileged user namespaces, and runs the `nft -c` checks of the rendered firewall (`tests/network`, the `nftables_host` role refusal) and `dnsmasq --test`, then the root-only user-switching test (`setpriv`) as root, all with `HNX_REQUIRE_TOOLS=nft,dnsmasq,unshare,setpriv,ansible-playbook`, so a missing or unusable tool fails instead of skipping. The probes moved into `tests/suite_policy.py` (`require_nft_check`, `require_dnsmasq`, `require_root_setpriv`; `require_tool` takes a `search_path`), and `test_suite_policy` rejects a new local probe-and-skip (a `which` lookup, `shutil.which` or imported, followed by a skip, a `skipif` or an alias of either, or an answer of False, None or a bare `return`, including a module-level probe variable used by a `skipif`; or a skip that reads the uid or euid; the older ones are a shrink-only baseline). `find_nft_check()` is the non-skipping lookup the role test uses for its stand-in, so the two nft probes cannot drift, and an unusable user namespace or a non-root setpriv run is reported as that, not as a missing tool. `TODO.md` asks the operator to require `real-tools` on `main`.

### Changed
- ADR-0026 (proposed) is amended with the operator's stage-2 decisions: the controller cuts over after stage 3 (the agent-runner contract and its provider), not after stage 2, behind an internal runner seam under `orchestrator/internal/` that a private refactor proves first, with the private controller frozen from stage 2's merge and only security fixes backported; neutral wire names (`session_id`, neutral config keys, metric prefix and event source) with a one-release overlap, refused conflicting names, a config and a database migration and a checked, protected backup before every live run; no HTTP passthrough (an unknown `agent_key` is always refused, non-agent callers use the gateway); the lessons and runbooks readers on this repository's registers, with `match` an optional, bounded field of an additive lesson schema 2.2 that lands with the code; and `go.yaml.in/yaml/v3` as the YAML library, so the module gains a `go.sum` while the gateway's packages stay standard library only.
- `tools/experimental.yml` is ratcheted against the base: CI's `trusted-gates` job runs the base's `tools/experimental.py ratchet --base` (the `schemas` job runs the change's own copy with `--ci-base` as quick feedback), which fails when the list adds a component, or a `tests:` or `code:` path not equal to or under one the base lists, or a gate, invariant or conformance capability the base does not list, or a key the base's rules refuse. Removing entries, moving them between components and editing the other keys stay free. It also fails when the change's own `tools/experimental.py` assigns `LIST` or `MARKER` other than the base's (which list the test marker reads), and a base without a list counts every entry as growth. Growth needs the operator's `experimental-growth` pull-request label and passes only on the run that applying the label triggers (`HNX_EXPERIMENTAL_GROWTH`), so the label approves that head and not a later push; a push to `main` was gated at its pull request. CI now also runs on `labeled` and `unlabeled`. Listing a component's tests and code took them out of every required job and the coverage floor, so a change could silence a red security test by listing it, with no test file in its diff. Not breaking for overlay sites (the list is this repository's own); a pull request that grows the list now needs the label.
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the broker bounds successful calls, not only refusals (review 2, F14; ADR-0017 note of 2026-11-06). Broker manifest 1.9 (additive) adds `quotas.calls_per_minute`, **on by default at 60**: the calls, submits, Code Mode token operations and owner operations (`status`, and `detail` through it, `run`, `cancel`) one actor may have admitted in any 60 s (a sliding window, per broker process); one past it is refused `call-rate-exceeded` (outcome `denied`, audited), which the socket's `refusal_limit` counts against the peer (a sandbox's, after its token was admitted, is bounded by the token's `max_calls` instead), so a loop of granted calls, or of `ok` status reads, ends `rate-limited` as a loop of refusals does. An agent (or a Code Mode execution) that made more than 60 operations a minute is now refused until its window slides; raise `calls_per_minute` in the manifest for it (an agent running Code Mode needs at least `codemode.max_calls` times `codemode.max_live_tokens_per_actor`, 200 with the defaults, or its concurrent executions are refused first). 1.9 also adds `retention`: a run directory (`runs/<request>/`) last written more than `runs_days` ago (default 30) and an audit record, with its `decisions/` entry, stamped more than `audit_days` ago (default 400; `null` keeps the trail for ever) are removed at start and at most once an hour (on a background thread, never on a caller's request), never those of a request still pending or running, and no audit record at all while every one is past retention (a clock stepped forward), so audit records older than 400 days are now deleted unless the manifest says otherwise. Each run's private copy of its handler is removed as soon as the handler has ended (the pinned digest names its bytes). Before, every `ok` call left a run directory with a handler copy and up to 1 MiB each of stdout and stderr, plus two audit records, and nothing pruned either, so an agent looping a read action it was granted could fill the state disk, after which every action of every agent and every approval was refused `audit-unavailable`. `docs/broker.md` no longer claims a looping agent cannot fill the disk: the rate and the retention bound how fast state grows, and the state filesystem must still be sized for them and watched. `examples/broker/manifest.production.yml` states both.
- `tools/experimental.yml` is ratcheted against the base: CI's `trusted-gates` job runs the base's `tools/experimental.py ratchet --base` (the `schemas` job runs the change's own copy with `--ci-base` as quick feedback), which fails when the list adds a component, or a `tests:` or `code:` path not equal to or under one the base lists, or a gate, invariant or conformance capability the base does not list, or a key the base's rules refuse. Removing entries, moving them between components and editing the other keys stay free. It also fails when the change's own `tools/experimental.py` assigns `LIST` or `MARKER` other than the base's (which list the test marker reads), and a base without a list counts every entry as growth. Growth needs the operator's `experimental-growth` pull-request label and passes only on the run that applying the label triggers (`HNX_EXPERIMENTAL_GROWTH`), so the label approves that head and not a later push; a push to `main` was gated at its pull request. CI now also runs on `labeled` and `unlabeled`. Listing a component's tests and code took them out of every required job and the coverage floor, so a change could silence a red security test by listing it, with no test file in its diff. Not breaking for overlay sites (the list is this repository's own); a pull request that grows the list now needs the label. The `ratchet` command ships first and the two CI steps (with the `labeled` and `unlabeled` triggers) follow in the next change, because `trusted-gates` runs the base's copy.
- ADR-0035 (coordinator memory, proposed) amended to fit ADR-0037 and G-03/G-04, by the operator's decision: the warm and cold tiers are live `warm-*`, `rollup-*` and `exc-*` keys in `fleet-ledger`, not retired keys, because ADR-0037 compacts a retired platform key to a tombstone without its value and ages tombstones out oldest first whatever their class; so ADR-0037 needs no per-scope opt-out, and its decision 17 now also covers ADR-0035's decisions 3 and 7. The coordinator is two profiles joined by a G-04 handoff: a reader (`reads_untrusted: true`, declared) that owns the ledger and opens other profiles' reports in levels, with no broker grant; and an actor (`reads_untrusted: false`) that holds the broker grants, files the proposals and journal notes from the reader's constrained output only, and reads nothing else. A reopen retires the item's live warm and exception keys in the same call (an evicted item that reopened and was evicted again used to `add` a live `exc-*` key, which the store refuses, stalling the fold every cycle); exception keys count against the cold cap, and past it the oldest is folded into its rollup as a counted exception; nominations are capped per cycle; cited ledger keys and report digests must be ones rendered into the reader's pack; and `request_id`s are derived from the accepted report's digest so a replayed handoff files nothing new. Text only; nothing of ADR-0035 is built yet.
- Detections lint: workflow packs (`workflows/packs/**`) are agent-facing files, so `detections lint-ids` and invariant X-06 (one shared `lint_agent_ids` over one `find_agent_files`) now fail a pack that cites a `det:` id missing from the det map. The shipped packs cited three undefined ids; `new-critical-alert` now uses `det:mass-file-rename-ransom-extension` and `det:ssh-authorized-key-added`, and `phishing-report` triggers on `$event.source` equal to `svc:mail-gateway` (a user report is not a detection rule) instead of the never-matching `det:user-reported-phish` (the pack now acts only when the reported-message lookup finds the alert). **Breaking for overlay sites:** an overlay pack citing a `det:` id its det map lacks now fails `make detections` and X-06.
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the `config_leak_scan` role (control `secrets-from-store`) scans the files a git work tree ignores, and its health fails on a file it could not scan. It ran `tools/leak_scan.py`, which inside a work tree scans only what `git ls-files` lists; etckeeper makes `/etc` one, and its `.gitignore` hides `*.dpkg-old`, `*.dpkg-dist` and the like, so a credential left in `/etc/<app>/<app>.conf.dpkg-old` by a package upgrade was never read and health reported clean. `leak_scan` has a new `--walk` mode (walk the directories, never ask git; `.git` is still never scanned), which the role passes; the repository's own CI invocation keeps the git listing. The role also dropped `--allow-skip '*'`, which silently excluded unreadable and oversized files as well as binaries: binaries are still skipped, every other unscanned file is listed under `unscanned` in the report and fails health unless a glob in the new `config_leak_scan_unscanned_accepted` (default empty) names it. A guest whose scan paths hold a file over 5 MB, or a file or directory the scan cannot read, now fails health until it is moved, fixed or accepted; overlay sites that define the `secrets-from-store` control themselves must add `unscanned_accepted` to its `settings:` list (as `examples/minimal/guests/_baselines.yml` now does) before a baseline's `with:` can set it. Under `--walk` the scanner prunes only `.git` (not `venv`, `build`, `dist`, `.tox`, `node_modules` or caches, so a secret in `/srv/venv/creds` is found). In every mode the scanner never opens a socket, FIFO or device (it reports them as special files and the role drops them like binaries; a FIFO used to hang the scan) and escapes control characters in the paths it prints (a newline in a directory name could forge a line that read as an accepted host-key path, hiding a real hit). Where it walks the directories (under `--walk`, or where there is no git work tree) it reports a directory it cannot list instead of passing over it (`os.walk` without an error handler made a scan below an unreadable directory print clean); `tools/private_leak_check.py` reports such a directory as an `unreadable` finding in both its modes. The role checks its parse against the scanner's own hit and unscanned counts, and does not count an unscanned file whose name ends in `:<digits>` as a hit.
- `make constraints` cooldown exemptions are dated (`CONSTRAINTS_FRESH=pkg=YYYY-MM-DD`, admitting that package's releases up to that date, not today's); `ruff`, `pyarrow` and `certifi`, which the cutoff already covers, are dropped; the target fails with an error when GNU `date -d` yields no cutoff; `tests/packaging/test_constraints_cooldown.py` fails once an exemption is older than 14 days or dated in the future (a future date would waive the cooldown for good; the test turns red for the current 2026-10-24 entries from 2026-11-08, the intended expiry) and checks the Makefile's 14 days equal dependabot's cooldown.
- `hnx status serve` reuses one built document while its input files (state file, activity store and its `-wal`) are unchanged, for at most 15 seconds (`status.serve.DocumentMemo`), instead of rebuilding it and loading every activity event on every request; a write or a retention purge rebuilds it.
- One writer of inbox notes, `homelabnext/records/inbox.py` (`stamp`, `decided_clash`, `write_inbox`): the agent library (`Records`) and the broker's `records-inbox` lanes both stamp and write a note through it, and `tools/shared_helpers_lint.py` has a `records` rule refusing a file write into an inbox elsewhere (ADR-0003, ADR-0010).
- `homelabnext/broker/service.py` is split along its privilege boundaries behind the unchanged `BrokerService` facade, as pure moves (no behaviour change): `broker/requests.py` (call, submit, run, cancel, what a caller may see), `broker/approvals.py` (approve, deny, the pending listings, the `decision.requested` event), `broker/paging.py` (approval pages, lane-quota notices, the notifier), `broker/codemode_tokens.py` (Code Mode tokens; the token `flock` unchanged) and `broker/common.py` (the shared reasons and defaults, all still importable from `broker.service`); `tests/broker/test_broker_service_parts.py` holds each boundary to its module.
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the `open_webui` role turns off tool servers, plugins, workspace Tools and Functions on every start, and `open_webui_extra_env` is now an allowlist (issue #239). Each setting was read in the pinned v0.12.3 source. The role now fixes `ENABLE_PLUGINS=false`, `ENABLE_TOOLS=false`, `ENABLE_FUNCTIONS=false`, `ENABLE_TOOL_SERVERS=false` and `ENABLE_PIP_INSTALL_FRONTMATTER_REQUIREMENTS=false` (`backend/open_webui/env.py` lines 1194-1202). `ENABLE_PLUGINS` is the master switch: in 0.12.3 the other three are each ANDed with it, so it alone turns them off. The other three are fixed too, so a release that decouples them does not turn one back on. All four are read only from the environment, so an admin cannot turn them back on in Admin Settings, even until the next restart. An admin can still save tool and terminal server connections there (`routers/configs.py` lines 245 and 336), but they are never contacted: the verify, proxy, terminal and OAuth routes answer 403 (`main.py`, `routers/configs.py`, `routers/terminals.py`), and workspace Tools and Functions, which run admin-supplied Python in the container, refuse to load (`utils/plugin.py`). `OFFLINE_MODE=true` already skipped pip-installing their frontmatter requirements; the pip switch is belt and braces. Two admin-only routes are not gated by any setting and are left open: `POST /api/v1/tools/load/url` and `POST /api/v1/functions/load/url` still fetch any URL an admin gives them and return the body (nothing fetched is run). The role cannot deny them, since it runs only the container and the front door's routes are site configuration; the host egress rule bounds them, and a site's front door may deny both paths. A site that used tool servers, Tools or Functions loses them. `open_webui_extra_env` used to accept any upper-case name the reserved pattern did not match; it now accepts only the reviewed UI and behaviour names in `open_webui_allowed_env` (`WEBUI_NAME`, `DEFAULT_LOCALE`, `DEFAULT_MODELS`, `DEFAULT_PINNED_MODELS`, `MODEL_ORDER_LIST`, `DEFAULT_PROMPT_SUGGESTIONS`, `WEBUI_BANNERS`, `PENDING_USER_OVERLAY_TITLE`, `PENDING_USER_OVERLAY_CONTENT`, `RESPONSE_WATERMARK`, `SHOW_ADMIN_DETAILS`, `ENABLE_MESSAGE_RATING`, `ENABLE_ADMIN_EXPORT`, `ENABLE_ADMIN_CHAT_ACCESS`, `TASK_MODEL`, `TASK_MODEL_EXTERNAL`, `ENABLE_TITLE_GENERATION`, `ENABLE_TAGS_GENERATION`, `ENABLE_FOLLOW_UP_GENERATION`, `ENABLE_AUTOCOMPLETE_GENERATION`, `GLOBAL_LOG_LEVEL`), and still refuses any name the reserved pattern matches or the role fixes. The pattern now also covers the names that called out but passed it: `TERMINAL_SERVER*`; `STORAGE_PROVIDER`, `S3_*`, `GCS_*`, `AZURE_STORAGE_*`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS*`; `CONTENT_EXTRACTION_ENGINE`, `TIKA_*`, `DOCLING_*`, `MINERU_*`; `WEBHOOK_URL`, `ENABLE_USER_WEBHOOKS`; `PIP_*`, `ENABLE_PIP_INSTALL*`; `CUSTOM_NAME` (it fetches a name and logo from upstream's API at start, `config.py` line 199); `VECTOR_DB`, `QDRANT_*`, `MILVUS_*`, `CHROMA_*`, `ELASTICSEARCH_*`, `OPENSEARCH_*`, `PGVECTOR_*`, `PINECONE_*`; `YACY_*`, `YANDEX_*`; `OPENID_*`, `OAUTH_*`, `*_CLIENT_SECRET`; `DATABASE_*`, `SCIM_*`, `MFA_*`; `WEBUI_AUTH*`, `WEBUI_URL`, `CORS_ALLOW*` and every `*_COOKIE_*` setting; and `ENABLE_PLUGINS`, `ENABLE_TOOL*` and `ENABLE_FUNCTIONS`. A test holds them apart: no allowed name matches the pattern or is fixed. A fixed name such as `ENABLE_SIGNUP` was silently overridden before; it is now refused. An overlay that set any name outside the allowlist (for example `ENABLE_CHANNELS`, `DEFAULT_USER_ROLE` or `JWT_EXPIRES_IN`) now fails validation, which names the refused keys. `GLOBAL_LOG_LEVEL` stays allowed but only as `INFO`, `WARNING`, `ERROR` or `CRITICAL` (`DEBUG` logs whole chat requests and replies); values are ordinary Ansible input, templated like any other variable (the overlay is trusted and the role cannot see a value's text before templating), so put no secret lookup in `open_webui_extra_env`: a value there lands in `docker-compose.yml` and in the unauthenticated `/api/config`; a harmless name is added to the allowlist after it is read in the pinned release. New optional input `open_webui_public_url` (`https://host[:port]`, a lower-case host, punycode for a non-ASCII name, no IPv6 literal, no path, a port from 1 to 65535 but never `:443`, which would break the websocket's exact origin check): when set it is `WEBUI_URL` and the only `CORS_ALLOW_ORIGIN` (upstream's default is `*`, with credentials allowed, so Starlette echoes any origin; `config.py` line 2179); empty keeps that default. Once it is set, any other origin (a tunnel, a second host name) loses the websocket. The host egress firewall stays the backstop (docs/model-serving.md).
- The lab-in-a-box notify service is ntfy v2.33.1 (was v2.32.0; `binwiederhier/ntfy:v2.33.1` pinned by its multi-arch index digest `sha256:29bc9522…`, read from Docker Hub with an anonymous token and checked against the index body's own sha256: a Docker manifest list for linux amd64, arm64, arm/v6 and arm/v7). v2.33.1 (2026-10-23) is the newest 2.33.x past the 14-day cooldown; v2.34 and later are inside it. v2.33.0 had an amd64 "Illegal instruction" crash on CPUs without x86-64-v3 (AVX2/BMI2), fixed in 2.33.1 by returning to Ubuntu 24.04 builds. Security fixes in 2.33: built with Go 1.27.3 (the Go 1.27.2 `crypto/tls`, `net/http` and `html/template` fixes, and a 1.27.3 `crypto/x509` fix rejecting chains through the compromised eMudhra intermediate CAs); phone number verification now requires Twilio to confirm the code as approved; subscriptions ignore duplicate topics and are limited to 150. The example registries' `notify` `version` follows (2.32 -> 2.33), with the schema copy, generator goldens, keyring and docs/systems/notify.md regenerated. Nothing the conformance adapter and offline fake use changed between 2.32.0 and 2.33.1 (publish headers and their limits, `GET /<topic>/json?poll=1&since=all`, `/v1/health`), so their text moves to 2.33; CI's `lab-in-a-box` job ran `hnx conform run notify` against 2.33.1 (9 checks pass). The 150-topic cap applies only to comma-separated multi-topic subscriptions, which nothing here uses (`NtfySender` topics forbid commas).
- The lab-in-a-box notify service is ntfy v2.32.0 (was v2.11.0; `binwiederhier/ntfy:v2.32.0` pinned by its multi-arch index digest `sha256:59250e1d…`, read from Docker Hub with an anonymous token and checked against the index body's own sha256: a Docker manifest list for linux amd64, arm64, arm/v6 and arm/v7). v2.32.0 (2026-10-18, image built the same day) is the newest release past the 14-day cooldown; v2.33.0 (2026-10-23) to v2.37.2 are inside it. The image's licence label is unchanged (Apache-2.0, GPL-2.0; `tools/licences.yml` unchanged). The example registries' `notify` `version` follows (2.11 -> 2.32), with the schema copy, generator goldens, keyring and docs/systems/notify.md regenerated. The upstream release notes from v2.12.0 to v2.32.0 (`docs/releases.md` at the v2.32.0 tag) were read against this repository. Security fixes in that range: an SSRF through the web push endpoint allow-list (2.22, GHSA-w9hq-5jg7-q4j7), script execution from an SVG preview in the web app's publish dialog (2.23, GHSA-j8hr-p342-xrmh), ACL rules matching topics case-insensitively on SQLite (2.24), tokens and IDs from a clock-seeded PRNG (2.25, now `crypto/rand`), CPU and memory denial of service through message templates (2.26, 2.27), and secrets in the config hash served to the web app (2.27). What applies here: since 2.28 the server refuses a title over 1,024 bytes (HTTP 400, code 40057) and tags over 512 bytes in all (40058), measured after trimming and after decoding an RFC 2047 encoded word; `NtfySender` now shortens a longer title to 1,024 bytes ending `...` (so a long title no longer gets its page refused); its headers now also drop every control character (an HTTP server refuses a header carrying one) and break `=?`, so the server never decodes an encoded word past the counted length or back into a line break and refuses oversized tags itself before sending, and the conformance adapter records that its hex key tag shares that budget, so a dedupe key over 246 UTF-8 bytes cannot pass through it. A recovery adds `recovered` to its problem's tags, so a problem's own tags should stay within 502 bytes or its recovery is refused (no producer comes close). v2.33.0 (built with Go 1.27.3's `net/http` and `crypto/tls` fixes) clears the cooldown on 2026-11-06 and is a separate change (superseded by the 2.33.1 entry above). The offline fake (`conformance/fakes/ntfy_api.py`) refuses both as 2.32 does and answers `/v1/health` with 2.31's `role` field. Non-ASCII titles were already lossy (the sender sends latin-1 header bytes, which ntfy replaces as invalid UTF-8); 2.32 replaces a run of them with one `U+FFFD` where 2.11 replaced each byte, and the adapter's finding now says so. Nothing else applies: the lab runs `serve` with `NTFY_BASE_URL` and `NTFY_LISTEN_HTTP` only (no auth, cache or attachment file, so 2.31.1's message-cache schema change, which needs a manual step to downgrade, touches no lab state); the compose healthcheck still matches (`/v1/health` answers `{"healthy":true,"role":"active"}`, and a 503 only for an experimental cluster node); the image still runs as root with entrypoint `ntfy`; 2.23's topic-creation limit (100 burst, 1/min) and 2.28's poll replay budget (500 MB/day by default) are far above what the adapter's per-target topic and polls use. Checked against the pinned image run as the compose file runs it (hardened, `NET_BIND_SERVICE`, 96m; about 13 MiB used, `ntfy version 2.32.0`): the healthcheck command, `hnx conform run notify` with the lab's target (9 checks pass), the 1,024/1,025-byte title and 512/513-byte tag edges through `NtfySender` (and v2.11.0 accepting all four), and, on a second instance with `auth-default-access: deny-all`, a user, an ACL and a token, `NtfySender` and the adapter's poll authenticating with `Bearer` (anonymous poll refused 403). The full stack (`make lab-smoke`) was not run here; CI's `lab-in-a-box` job runs it.
- The lab's LLM gateway is LiteLLM v1.106.3 (was v1.106.2; `litellm/litellm-database:v1.106.3` pinned by its multi-arch OCI index digest `sha256:f631537b…`, read from Docker Hub with an anonymous token and checked against the index body's own sha256; amd64 and arm64, each with a build attestation). 1.106.3 (PyPI 2026-10-24 09:14 UTC, image tag pushed 09:27 UTC) is the newest release past the 14-day cooldown; 1.106.4 (2026-10-25) to 1.106.7 are inside it. The amd64 image's 22 layers were downloaded with an anonymous token and each checked against its digest: there is no `cook.pth`, `cook.py` or top-level `sitecustomize.py` (the three `sitecustomize.py` files are inside the ddtrace and opentelemetry bootstrap packages); the only `.pth` files are coloredlogs', google-cloud-aiplatform's namespace file and the `_virtualenv.pth` that uv writes when it creates the venv, as in 1.106.2. PyPI lists no advisory against 1.106.3, and it is not yanked. Upstream changes, from a diff of the two manylinux wheels: the Python source differs only in three files of the opt-in OTel V2 integration, adapted to OpenTelemetry 1.45.1, which the `proxy-runtime` extra now pins (was 1.33.1; grpcio 1.84.1, was 1.78.0). The Rust bridge is rebuilt with the same interface (`_native.pyi` unchanged), the admin UI is rebuilt, and the `boto3` and `certifi` floors rise. The proxy, router, budget and limit code, auth, `schema.prisma` and `litellm-proxy-extras` (0.4.108 in both) are unchanged. So the generated config, `provision`, the 401 and 429 answers and `/spend/logs/v2` behave as in 1.106.2, and there are no new migrations (213 in both). The lab configures no OTel callback. Rehearsed with the PyPI releases in scratch venvs (`litellm[proxy]` plus prisma 0.11.0, as the image has it) on distro postgres 16.15, not the lab's 18.6. No Docker daemon was available, so the image itself was not run. On a fresh database 1.106.3 applied all 213 migrations ("All migrations have been successfully applied."). In place, on a database that 1.106.2 had initialised and used, it applied none, and the keys 1.106.2 had created still served. Both times, `provision` created and then updated the lab's keys and the orchestrator example's telemetry reader, and `hnx conform run inference --target compose/lab-in-a-box/targets/inference-gateway.yml` passed (12 executed, 12 skipped for undeclared features). The reader's key got 403 on `/v1/chat/completions`, `/chat/completions`, `/key/generate`, `/key/list`, `/user/info` and `/v1/models`, even with a servable model on it, and 200 on `/spend/logs/v2`; a wrong key got 401. Memory did not change between the two versions under the same calls: about 585 MiB RSS after a warm start and 743 MiB after a cold one, for both. A cold start (no bytecode cached) is what every new container does, since the image ships no `.pyc`. With the image's Prisma settings (`PRISMA_OFFLINE_MODE`, the node CLI run directly) and the cgroup capped at 896 MiB, a cold start ran the same sequence without reaching the limit (peak 843 MiB on 1.106.2, and 844 MiB in place and 849 MiB fresh on 1.106.3, page cache included). `mem_limit` stays 896m; the compose comment's 604 MB was measured on 1.106.2 and is close to the warm figure. The `llm-gateway-keys` container was not built for the rehearsal; its `provision` command ran from the checkout instead.
- The lab's LLM gateway is LiteLLM v1.106.2 (was v1.103.7; `litellm/litellm-database:v1.106.2` pinned by its multi-arch OCI index digest `sha256:35f2cec3…`, read from Docker Hub with an anonymous token and checked against the index body's own sha256; amd64 and arm64). 1.106.2 (PyPI and the image tag, both 2026-10-21) is the newest release past the 14-day cooldown. It is also the last release before the compromised 1.107.0 (PyPI wheels and Docker tags, published later on 2026-10-21 and since removed). This tag was pushed at 10:47 UTC, before the compromised tags appeared (about 11:46 UTC), and its digest matches none of the published compromised digests (none was published for Docker Hub's `litellm-database`); `.github/dependabot.yml` ignores `v1.107.0` so it is never proposed. The image has no `cook.pth`, `cook.py` or top-level `sitecustomize.py`; its only `.pth` files are coloredlogs', google-cloud-aiplatform's namespace file and virtualenv's. PyPI lists no advisory against either version. Upstream changes that bear on the lab: the proxy refuses to start with an unset, empty or publicly known (`sk-1234`) master key (the lab's master key is required by compose, and the example value is neither); it exits when database setup or a migration fails (`ENFORCE_PRISMA_MIGRATION_CHECK=false` is ignored); and an exhausted key, user or team budget answers 422 (was 429). The lab sets budgets only per deployment, and in 1.106.2's source those still filter deployments (the budget limiter's "No deployments available - crossed budget", which the proxy answers with 429) rather than raise the budget error. A deployment budget was not exhausted live. The schema change is additive, with 29 more migrations (213, was 184). Rehearsed on postgres 18.6: on a fresh volume, all 213 migrations applied ("All migrations have been successfully applied."); in place on a volume 1.103.7 had initialised and used, the 29 new ones applied, and the keys 1.103.7 had created still served. Both times, `provision` created and then updated the lab's keys and the orchestrator example's telemetry reader (its user and route-confined key), and `hnx conform run inference --target compose/lab-in-a-box/targets/inference-gateway.yml` passed (12 executed, 5 skipped for undeclared features). A key with `allowed_routes: ["/spend/logs/v2"]` gets 403 on `/v1/chat/completions`, `/chat/completions`, `/key/generate`, `/key/list`, `/user/info` and `/v1/models`, even with a servable model on it, and a wrong key gets 401. `llm-gateway`'s `mem_limit` is now 896m (was 768m): 1.106.2 measured 604 MB RSS and a 692 MB cgroup peak in the rehearsal, against 528 and 621 for 1.103.7 under the same calls. With that change, the gateway profile's total is 960 MB and the gateway-only path's total is 1280 MB (compose header and `docs/llm-gateway.md`). The `llm-gateway-keys` container was not built for the rehearsal; its `provision` command ran from the checkout instead.
- The `open_webui` role pins Open WebUI v0.12.3 (was v0.11.4; `ghcr.io/open-webui/open-webui:v0.12.3` pinned by its multi-arch index digest `sha256:a537bbe1…`, read from ghcr with an anonymous token and checked against the index body's own sha256). 0.12.3 (2026-10-16) is the newest release past the 14-day cooldown; 0.12.4 (2026-10-25), 0.12.5 (2026-10-29) and 0.13.0 (2026-11-03) are inside it; 0.12.4 and 0.12.5 each open with an upstream security advisory whose fixes are not listed, and clear the cooldown about 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-12, a separate change. 0.12.0 carries a security advisory (access-control fixes, some not yet enumerated upstream) and 0.12.0 and 0.12.1 carry database migrations, which run on first start (upstream asks for a backup first: run the role's `backup` before the bump; an empty data directory migrates to head and answers `/health`, checked with the 0.12.3 PyPI release, not the image). Every setting the role fixes is still read under the same name. 0.12.1 adds external apps (channel bots at an HTTPS address that Open WebUI keeps a live connection to, off by default): the role sets `ENABLE_APPS=false` on every start (an admin can still turn Apps on in Admin Settings until the next restart, as `ENABLE_PERSISTENT_CONFIG=false` resets it; the host egress firewall is the backstop), and `open_webui_extra_env` refuses `ENABLE_APPS` and `APP_*` as it refuses the other settings that call out (0.12.0's Realtime voice settings, `AUDIO_REALTIME_*`, were already refused as `AUDIO_*`).
- The `vllm_server` role pins vLLM 0.32.0 (was 0.31.0; `main` still refuses any other installed version). 0.32.0 (PyPI, 2026-10-18) is the newest release past the 14-day cooldown; 0.33.0 (2026-11-02) is inside it. It fixes the advisories PyPI lists against 0.31.0 with 0.32.0 as the fixed version, among them a CORS default of `*` on the OpenAI-compatible server (now no origins; vLLM is reached only by the gateway here, which for `zone` exposure the host firewall enforces), request chat templates rendered with no time bound (now 30 s, `VLLM_CHAT_TEMPLATE_RENDER_TIMEOUT`; there is still no output-size bound), and the `/v1/responses` endpoint skipping the `--trust-request-chat-template` gate. Read against the 0.31.0 and 0.32.0 sdists (sha256 equal to PyPI's): no `EngineArgs` or frontend field was removed or renamed, so every flag the unit sets and every name in `ALLOWED_ARGS` that 0.31.0 accepts is still there; the usage-stats switches the unit sets (`VLLM_NO_USAGE_STATS`, `DO_NOT_TRACK`), `VLLM_CACHE_ROOT`, `VLLM_API_KEY` and `--scheduling-policy priority` are unchanged, and `vllm --version` still prints the bare version. The unit, the allowlist and the serving plan are unchanged. Three allowlisted names (`swap-space`, `calculate-kv-scales`, `max-num-partial-prefills`) are in neither release's source (the docs of both releases say `--swap-space` was removed), and `tokenizer-mode: slow` is no tokenizer mode either release registers, so an instance carrying one would fail at start under both; taking them off narrows the `model_serving` schema's `args` names, a major change under ADR-0001, and is left to its own change. Requires-Python is now `>=3.11,<3.15` (0.31.0 accepted 3.10), so the virtualenv needs Python 3.11 or newer; torch stays 2.13.0. Not run here: vLLM itself (needs a GPU), `vllm serve --help=all` and the inference conformance suite against a 0.32.0 install.
- The lab-in-a-box events service is NATS 2.15.1 (was 2.10.29; `nats:2.15.1-alpine` pinned by its multi-arch index digest, read from Docker Hub with an anonymous token and checked against the index body's own sha256), and CI's `NATS_SERVER_VERSION` v2.15.1 with its module sum (sum.golang.org's `h1:` as hex, equal to `go mod download`'s). 2.15.1 (2026-10-09) is the newest release past the 14-day cooldown; 2.15.2 and 2.14.9 (2026-10-26) are inside it. The licence is still Apache-2.0 (its LICENSE byte-identical to 2.10.29's). The example registry's `events` `version` follows (2.10.29 -> 2.15.1), with its schema copy, generator goldens, keyring and docs/systems page regenerated. What 2.11 to 2.15 change for this repository: the bridge's NATS user is granted its acks in both of ADR-15's reply-subject forms, each token for token as the consumer subscribes to it: v1 `$JS.ACK.<stream>.<durable>.*.*.*.*.*` (narrowed from `...<durable>.>`) and v2 `$JS.ACK._.<account hash>.<stream>.<durable>.*.*.*.*.>` (no JetStream domain is configured; the hash is the server's `getHash` of the account name, which `tests/compose` recomputes). nats-server 2.15.1 sends v1; `js_ack_fc_v2` (v2) is enabled in 2.16, flag removed in 2.17 (upstream main), and a grant of one form alone refuses every ack in the other (seen on a 2.15.1 server with the flag on: `Publish Violation`, each delivery redelivered toward `max_deliver`). A wildcard over the domain or hash token (`$JS.ACK.*.*.<stream>.<durable>.>`) is not used: it also matches another consumer's nine-token v1 ack subject, which takes `+NXT`. The docs' ACL excerpts (broker.md, stackstorm.md) follow, and docs/stackstorm.md says what to grant and why; nats.conf is a reviewed `allow:` path of the product-name lint (its grants must name the bridge's durables). The broker scenario's `bus-acl` step (`compose/lab-in-a-box/broker_e2e.py`) now also reads `decision.returned` through the bridge's durable as the bridge's user, acks it and reads the consumer back (nothing pending, ack floor past it), so the lab-in-a-box job exercises a real bridge ack on every NATS bump; its unit test runs it over the wire fake in both ack forms and fails it when the ACL grants only the other form. JetStream API requests are decoded strictly since 2.12 (an unknown field is an `invalid JSON` 10025 error, not a warning), and the offline JetStream wire fake (`conformance/fakes/jetstream_wire.py`) now refuses an unknown field of a `STREAM.CREATE`, `CONSUMER.CREATE` or `STREAM.MSG.GET` request as 2.15.1 does (its field lists read from the server source; `strict=False` is the old behaviour) and can hand out v2 ack subjects (`ack_format="v2"`), and the events suite passes through the adapter in both formats; the events container gets `GOMEMLIMIT: 96MiB`, below its 128m `mem_limit`, since 2.12's filestore caches are given back under the Go runtime's memory limit, which Go does not derive from the cgroup. Nothing else applies here: the lab config uses no removed or renamed option, no server, cluster or gateway name with spaces (refused since 2.11), no TLS, MQTT, websocket or leafnode; `/healthz` (the compose healthcheck) is unchanged; 2.11's ingest buffer limits (`max_buffered_msgs`/`max_buffered_size`) and 2.15's default of 1,000 consumers per stream are far above the lab's use; the adapter, the broker relay and the bridge talk to the server only through their own `$JS.API` JSON and need nothing newer from nats-py (2.16.0, the newest release, unchanged). An existing `events-data` store upgrades in place (a 2.10.29 store holding the stream, a durable and an acked delivery, then 2.15.1 on it: the messages, the durable's ack floor and the dedup window carry over; NATS rebuilds the stream state files if a store is ever taken back to 2.10); a graceful stop now exits 0 (was 1), which the lab's `restart: "no"` does not read. Checked: `nats-server -t` on the lab config with nats-server v2.15.1 built by `go install` with Go 1.26.10 (as CI builds it), and the pinned image run as the compose file runs it (hardened, 128m, the lab config; `/healthz` ok, `Git: [b2fd3ef]`, the v2.15.1 tag's commit) with `hnx conform run events` and the lab's target against it (17 checks pass, no `Invalid JetStream request` warning in its log) and the bridge user's acks accepted; on the built server, the final config takes the bridge's acks with `js_ack_fc_v2` off and on, and the old v1-only grant refuses them with it on. The full stack (`make lab-smoke`, `make lab-broker`) was not run here; CI's `lab-in-a-box` job runs it. 2.15.2 (security fixes, among them bounds on stream-state recovery; built with Go 1.26.10) clears the cooldown about 2026-11-09 and is a separate change.
- CI's `GO_VERSION` is Go 1.27.3 (was 1.26.10). 1.27.3 (2026-10-19) is the newest Go release past the 14-day cooldown, read from the Go toolchain proxy (`proxy.golang.org/golang.org/toolchain`). It builds the external tools CI installs from source (actionlint v1.7.12, nats-server v2.15.1; their go.mod lines, 1.25.0 and 1.26.0, are below it, so `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` accepts them) and runs the Python tools' Go tests; it will run `fix_has_test`'s Go proofs once a Go module exists (no `go.mod` yet, so none changes). What 1.27 changes here: `go test`'s default vet set gains `stdversion` (a standard-library API newer than a module's go line fails the test), and seven GODEBUG settings are removed (`asynctimerchan`, `gotypesalias`, `tls10server`, `tlsrsakex`, `tls3des`, `tlsunsafeekm`, `x509keypairleaf`), none of which the repository, nats-server or actionlint sets. Checked with a 1.27.3 toolchain: both tools build from their pinned module sums with matching `go version -m` build info, `nats-server -t` accepts the lab config, actionlint is clean on the repository, and the Go tool tests pass with `HNX_REQUIRE_GO=1`.
- The lab-in-a-box events service is NATS 2.15.1 (was 2.10.29; `nats:2.15.1-alpine` pinned by its multi-arch index digest, read from Docker Hub with an anonymous token and checked against the index body's own sha256), and CI's `NATS_SERVER_VERSION` v2.15.1 with its module sum (sum.golang.org's `h1:` as hex, equal to `go mod download`'s). 2.15.1 (2026-10-09) is the newest release past the 14-day cooldown; 2.15.2 and 2.14.9 (2026-10-26) are inside it. The licence is still Apache-2.0 (its LICENSE byte-identical to 2.10.29's). The example registry's `events` `version` follows (2.10.29 -> 2.15.1), with its schema copy, generator goldens, keyring and docs/systems page regenerated. What 2.11 to 2.15 change for this repository: the bridge's NATS user is granted its acks in both of ADR-15's reply-subject forms, each token for token as the consumer subscribes to it: v1 `$JS.ACK.<stream>.<durable>.*.*.*.*.*` (narrowed from `...<durable>.>`) and v2 `$JS.ACK._.<account hash>.<stream>.<durable>.*.*.*.*.>` (no JetStream domain is configured; the hash is the server's `getHash` of the account name, which `tests/compose` recomputes). nats-server 2.15.1 sends v1; `js_ack_fc_v2` (v2) is enabled in 2.16, flag removed in 2.17 (upstream main), and a grant of one form alone refuses every ack in the other (seen on a 2.15.1 server with the flag on: `Publish Violation`, each delivery redelivered toward `max_deliver`). A wildcard over the domain or hash token (`$JS.ACK.*.*.<stream>.<durable>.>`) is not used: it also matches another consumer's nine-token v1 ack subject, which takes `+NXT`. The docs' ACL excerpts (broker.md, stackstorm.md) follow, and docs/stackstorm.md says what to grant and why; nats.conf is a reviewed `allow:` path of the product-name lint (its grants must name the bridge's durables). The broker scenario's `bus-acl` step (`compose/lab-in-a-box/broker_e2e.py`) now also reads `decision.returned` through the bridge's durable as the bridge's user, acks it and reads the consumer back (nothing pending, ack floor past it), so the lab-in-a-box job exercises a real bridge ack on every NATS bump; its unit test runs it over the wire fake in both ack forms and fails it when the ACL grants only the other form. JetStream API requests are decoded strictly since 2.12 (an unknown field is an `invalid JSON` 10025 error, not a warning), and the offline JetStream wire fake (`conformance/fakes/jetstream_wire.py`) now refuses an unknown field of a `STREAM.CREATE`, `CONSUMER.CREATE` or `STREAM.MSG.GET` request as 2.15.1 does (its field lists read from the server source; `strict=False` is the old behaviour) and can hand out v2 ack subjects (`ack_format="v2"`), and the events suite passes through the adapter in both formats; the events container gets `GOMEMLIMIT: 96MiB`, below its 128m `mem_limit`, since 2.12's filestore caches are given back under the Go runtime's memory limit, which Go does not derive from the cgroup. Nothing else applies here: the lab config uses no removed or renamed option, no server, cluster or gateway name with spaces (refused since 2.11), no TLS, MQTT, websocket or leafnode; `/healthz` (the compose healthcheck) is unchanged; 2.11's ingest buffer limits (`max_buffered_msgs`/`max_buffered_size`) and 2.15's default of 1,000 consumers per stream are far above the lab's use; the adapter, the broker relay and the bridge talk to the server only through their own `$JS.API` JSON and need nothing newer from nats-py (2.16.0, the newest release, unchanged). An existing `events-data` store upgrades in place (a 2.10.29 store holding the stream, a durable and an acked delivery, then 2.15.1 on it: the messages, the durable's ack floor and the dedup window carry over; NATS rebuilds the stream state files if a store is ever taken back to 2.10); a graceful stop now exits 0 (was 1), which the lab's `restart: "no"` does not read. Checked: `nats-server -t` on the lab config with nats-server v2.15.1 built by `go install` with Go 1.26.10 (as CI then built it; re-checked with a 1.27.3 build), and the pinned image run as the compose file runs it (hardened, 128m, the lab config; `/healthz` ok, `Git: [b2fd3ef]`, the v2.15.1 tag's commit) with `hnx conform run events` and the lab's target against it (17 checks pass, no `Invalid JetStream request` warning in its log) and the bridge user's acks accepted; on the built server, the final config takes the bridge's acks with `js_ack_fc_v2` off and on, and the old v1-only grant refuses them with it on. The full stack (`make lab-smoke`, `make lab-broker`) was not run here; CI's `lab-in-a-box` job runs it. 2.15.2 (security fixes, among them bounds on stream-state recovery; built with Go 1.26.10) clears the cooldown about 2026-11-09 and is a separate change.
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the lab-in-a-box database is PostgreSQL 18.6 (was 16.15; `postgres:18.6-alpine` pinned by its multi-arch index digest `sha256:10a5c69a…`, read from Docker Hub with an anonymous token and checked as an OCI image index for amd64, arm64 and six more platforms). 18.6 is the newest 18.x (tag `REL_18_6`, stamped 2026-08-11), past the 14-day cooldown; official images are rebuilt, and this digest was pushed 2026-10-26. The 18 image moves `PGDATA` to `/var/lib/postgresql/18/docker` and its `VOLUME` to `/var/lib/postgresql`, so the `postgres-data` volume now mounts at `/var/lib/postgresql` (was `/var/lib/postgresql/data`); `tests/compose` checks the mount against the image's major and that `PGDATA` is not overridden (with negatives for the old mount and an override). A major upgrade does not convert data: the 18 entrypoint exits with an error on a volume a 16 server initialised (or on a mount at the old path), after creating an empty `18/docker` directory in it. Rehearsed: a volume the 16.15 image initialised, mounted at `/var/lib/postgresql`, makes the 18.6 container exit 1 naming `/var/lib/postgresql` (the old cluster sits at the volume's root), leaving an empty `18/docker` beside it. A fresh lab needs nothing. A lab that keeps its volume recreates it (`docker compose down -v`; the catalog, LiteLLM's keys and the `litellm` role are rebuilt by `catalog-migrate`, the init script and `llm-gateway-keys`). A site with data to keep dumps it with the 16 image (`pg_dumpall`) and restores into a fresh 18 volume, or runs `pg_upgrade` (both binaries; the new single mount at `/var/lib/postgresql` allows `pg_upgrade --link` once the old cluster is moved into its own `16/docker`), then moves its own mount to `/var/lib/postgresql`. `pg_upgrade` needs both clusters to agree on data checksums: a cluster the 16 image made has them off and 18's `initdb` turns them on, so initialise the 18 cluster with `POSTGRES_INITDB_ARGS=--no-data-checksums` (or run `pg_checksums --enable` on the stopped 16 cluster first); the dump and restore route is unaffected. Clients: Lakekeeper v0.14.1 runs its own tests against Postgres 15 and 18; LiteLLM v1.103.7 (its Prisma CLI and engines are 5.4.2) states no 18 support of its own (its compose example uses 16), so it was rehearsed on a fresh 18.6 volume: `--profile gateway up --wait llm-gateway llm-gateway-keys` came up healthy, `prisma migrate deploy` applied all 184 migrations into schema `litellm` ("All migrations have been successfully applied."), `llm-gateway-keys` created its three keys, and `hnx conform run inference --target compose/lab-in-a-box/targets/inference-gateway.yml` passed (12 executed, 5 skipped for features the target does not declare); every lab role already authenticates with SCRAM (the image's default since 14), so 18's deprecation of MD5 passwords changes nothing here; nothing in `homelabnext` connects to Postgres. The init script, environment and server flags are unchanged; 18's `initdb` enables data checksums by default. The healthcheck now probes over TCP (`pg_isready -h` the container's loopback address): the entrypoint's init server listens on the socket only, so the socket probe could report postgres healthy while `/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d` was still running. The example registry's records-db `version` follows the pin (18.6), with its schema copy, generator goldens, keyring and docs/systems page regenerated; the PostgreSQL licence is unchanged (`tools/licences.yml`).
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the lab-in-a-box uptime service is Uptime Kuma 2.5.6 (was 1.23.17; `louislam/uptime-kuma:2.5.6-slim-rootless` pinned by its multi-arch index digest, read from Docker Hub with an anonymous token and checked against the index body's own sha256; `uptime` and `uptime-init` share the one pin). 2.5.6 (2026-10-09) is the newest release past the 14-day cooldown; 2.5.7 to 2.5.9 (2026-10-23 to 2026-10-31) are inside it. Licence re-read for the major bump: MIT, the `LICENSE` file byte-identical between 1.23.17 and 2.5.6 (`tools/licences.yml` unchanged). The upstream [migration from v1 to v2](https://github.com/louislam/uptime-kuma/wiki/Migration-From-v1-To-v2) and the 2.x sources read against this repository: the image is the **slim rootless** variant (2.x's full image adds an embedded MariaDB and Chromium the lab does not use; rootless starts as the image's `node` user, which owns `/app/data`), so `uptime` drops 1.x's `cap_add: [CHOWN, SETGID, SETUID]` (2.x's entrypoint no longer chowns and switches user) and both services run with `cap_drop: [ALL]` alone; `UPTIME_KUMA_DB_TYPE=sqlite` selects SQLite, since a 2.x data directory with neither `db-config.json` nor a 1.x `kuma.db` otherwise serves a "setup database" page and holds the server; that page also answers `/api/entry-page` (with `type: setup-database`), so the compose health check now requires `type: entryPage` as well as a 200. The setup page is unauthenticated, so a fresh 2.x reachable before it is configured lets anyone pick its database: the example registry's probe (which a setup page also passes) carries a comment and docs/systems/uptime.md a failure-mode note that a deployment must set `UPTIME_KUMA_DB_TYPE` or ship `db-config.json`. `smoke.sh` prints a one-line hint about a kept root-owned 1.x volume when `uptime` is not healthy after `up --wait` fails. Kuma still tries `sudo service nscd start` and logs that it failed (no-new-privileges). The provisioning script moves to `config/uptime/provision.js` (the slot's name, so the product-name baseline shrinks rather than grows with the new `UPTIME_KUMA_DB_TYPE` line) and keeps the same socket.io events, which 2.x kept, but its payloads follow 2.x: a monitor carries `conditions` and `rabbitmqNodes` (`monitor.conditions` is NOT NULL; the 1.23 payload failed `add` with a constraint error against a real 2.5.6), and the status page config carries 2.x's `StatusPage.toJSON()` fields (`analyticsType: null`, since `saveStatusPage` rejects an undefined one as "Invalid analytics type", `showOnlyLastHeartbeat`, `autoRefreshInterval`, `analyticsId`, `analyticsScriptUrl`, `rssTitle`; `googleAnalyticsId` is gone). The fake Kuma in `tests/compose` refuses both. The status hub's `poll-uptime-kuma` collector reads 2.x's `incidents` list as well as 1.x's single `incident`, and names each pinned incident `incident-<id>` after Kuma's incident id in both shapes (it was `incident` for 1.x's one), so an item stays the same incident while others come and go and across the upgrade; a monitor whose name equals an incident item no longer merges with it (both are suffixed with a digest of their labels). Monitors and heartbeats kept their shape, and an unknown slug is now a 404 (docs/status.md). New fixtures `kuma-2-status-page.json` and `kuma-2-heartbeat.json` were recorded from the provisioned lab page on a real 2.5.6 (the 1.23.16 recordings stay), and the lab smoke's fixture server serves them. The example registry's `uptime` `version`, its schema copy, the generator goldens, the keyring and docs/systems/uptime.md follow. A fresh lab needs nothing; a kept 1.x `uptime-data` volume is owned by root (1.x ran as root here), which the rootless image cannot write, and would be migrated one way: `docker compose down -v` first, and a site running its own Kuma 1.x follows the upstream migration guide (back up `data/`, badge `:duration` limits, LiquidJS e-mail templates, JSON backup/restore removed).
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the lab-in-a-box metrics service is Prometheus v3.15.1 (was v2.55.1). The image is `prom/prometheus:v3.15.1`, pinned by its multi-arch index digest. That digest was read from Docker Hub with an anonymous token and checked against the sha256 of the index body. v3.15.1 (2026-10-21) is the newest release past the 14-day cooldown; v3.16.0 is still a release candidate. The example registry's `metrics` `version` follows (2.55.1 -> 3.15.1), and its schema copy, the generator goldens, the keyring and docs/systems/metrics.md are regenerated with it. The licence is still Apache-2.0 (the v3.15.1 `LICENSE`, unchanged in `tools/licences.yml`). Each 3.0 migration item was checked against this repository:
  - Retention: the lab's 2-hour retention moves from the deprecated `--storage.tsdb.retention.time` flag to `storage.tsdb.retention.time` in `config/prometheus/prometheus.yml`.
  - Generated scrape config: the `prometheus` generator's `scrape_configs.yml` is unchanged apart from the version label. Prometheus 3 fails a scrape whose `Content-Type` is missing or unknown, where 2.x read it as the text format. The lab scrapes only itself (verified); each endpoint in the example registry is expected to be fine because it is served by a Prometheus client library that sets a supported type (Lakekeeper's `metrics-exporter-prometheus` sends `text/plain`, read in its source; the others are inferred from the library each product uses, not tested live). So no `fallback_scrape_protocol` is generated. A generated job is "do not edit", so a site cannot add a fallback to it by hand. When a target needs one, the plan is an optional, pattern-validated registry field `metrics.fallback_protocol` copied into that job, so the protocol name lives in site data rather than core code. Until then, a site keeps such a target out of the generated file and writes the job in its own scrape config (docs/architecture.md).
  - Unaffected:
    - The lab's rules (`vector(1)`, `vector(0) > 0`) and the status sources' `up` query use no `holt_winters`, no integer `le`/`quantile` matcher and no range or subquery that left-open selectors change.
    - Nothing configures an Alertmanager, so the v1 API removal does not apply.
    - Nothing sets `scrape_classic_histograms` or a removed feature flag.
    - The self-scrape target names its port.
    - The image is still the busybox variant, running as `nobody`, so the `wget` healthcheck stands.
  - The status hub's `poll-prometheus` collector reads 3.x unchanged. v3.15.1's `web/api/v1` `Response`, `QueryData`, `Alert` and sample JSON fields are the same as 2.54.1's. The recorded 2.54.1 fixtures stay. The query and alerts tests now also run each fixture in a 3.x shape (`infos`/`warnings`, a UTF-8 label name, a normalised `le`, `keepFiringSince`), and both shapes map the same way.
  - `tests/compose/test_lab_in_a_box.py` now fails on a 2.x image or setting in the lab's metrics service:
    - a removed or deprecated flag, given as `--flag=value` or `--flag value`;
    - an `--enable-feature` name that 3.x no longer reads (the 3.0 defaults and removals, and later no-ops; `--enable-feature` itself stays, since `double_exponential_smoothing` needs it);
    - retention set outside the config file;
    - `scrape_classic_histograms`;
    - an Alertmanager on the v1 API;
    - `holt_winters`;
    - an integer `le`/`quantile` matcher in any PromQL quoting, including YAML-escaped `\"1\"`.
  - No Ansible role installs Prometheus or an exporter, so no role default or checksum moves.

  docs/architecture.md lists what 3.0 changes for a production Prometheus: UTF-8 names, `le`/`quantile` normalisation, left-open range selectors, `double_exponential_smoothing`, removed flags and the Alertmanager v1 API. A fresh lab needs nothing. A kept 2.x TSDB opens in 3.x, but after that it can be downgraded only to 2.55.
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the lab-in-a-box query engine is Trino 484 (was 470; `trinodb/trino:484` pinned by its multi-arch index digest, read from Docker Hub; released 2026-10-15, past the 14-day cooldown; Apache-2.0, the LICENSE unchanged). Trino now needs JDK 25 (the image ships 25.0.4). The 484 image is built on Red Hat's hardened core runtime (Project Hummingbird `hi/core-runtime`, under the Red Hat UBI end-user licence terms, as the UBI 9 micro base of 470 was), which has no `grep`, so the `lake-query` healthcheck (was `curl | grep` in a shell) runs the image's own `/usr/lib/trino/bin/health-check`; and the image no longer has the `run-trino` wrapper that passed the container hostname as the node id, so `config/trino/node.properties` sets `node.id=${ENV:HOSTNAME}` (a node id must match `[A-Za-z0-9][_A-Za-z0-9-]*`, so an overlay that gives `lake-query` a dotted `hostname:` fails at start). The catalog turns on the native S3 file system as `fs.s3.enabled` (`fs.native-s3.enabled` is a deprecated alias; the legacy S3 file system was removed in 481). `jvm.config` needs no JDK option: Trino's launcher (airlift launcher 321) appends a set per JDK major (21 to 28) after `jvm.config`, for JDK 25 `-XX:+EnableDynamicAgentLoading`, `--add-modules=jdk.incubator.vector`, `--sun-misc-unsafe-memory-access=allow` (which 476 requires only for the BigQuery and Snowflake connectors), `--enable-native-access=ALL-UNNAMED` and `-XX:+UseCompactObjectHeaders` (and `-XX:-RewriteBytecodes` on arm64). The lab's `jvm.config` gains the image default `-XX:-OmitStackTraceInFastThrow`. The swap rehearsal's candidate follows the pin (484.0), and `tools/tests/test_pin_consistency.py` holds it to the compose tag. The lab mounts `config/trino/` as the whole `/etc/trino` (the image's own etc holds only its default config, sample catalogs, a comment-only `log.properties` and a cache-manager file that only its default config names), and `tests/compose` checks the healthcheck, the HTTP port the image's health check reads, the node id, that the directory holds only the four expected kinds of file (a `secrets.toml` there would replace the resolver behind `${ENV:...}`) and that no removed or defunct property is set. No property, endpoint, header or SQL statement the repository's adapters use changed (`TrinoHTTP`'s `/v1/statement` protocol and `X-Trino-*` headers, `information_schema` queries, `CREATE`/`INSERT ... SELECT ... FROM (VALUES ...)`). A site carrying its own Trino configuration or queries checks the 471-484 release notes for: removed or defunct properties (`task.statistics-cpu-timer-enabled`, `enable-large-dynamic-filters`, `dynamic-filtering.small*`, the `hive.s3.*` legacy file system, catalog-level `fs.cache.*` settings, `iceberg.extended-statistics.enabled`), catalogs present on every node (477), decimal arithmetic precision (477), `char`/`varchar` coercion (482), typed bounds in the Iceberg `$files` table (482), and in 484 JSON values that keep numeric types and member order, UTC timestamps in `$snapshots`/`$history`, case-preserved user names in `GRANT`, and interval semantics; the redesigned Web UI is now at `/ui` (docs/architecture.md). The lab keeps no data across the upgrade: a fresh lab needs nothing.
- The example registries' `lake-query` version follows the lab's Trino pin: 484.0 in `examples/services.yml` (was 450.0) and `examples/lab-topology/services.yml` (was 460.0), with its schema copy, the generator goldens, the swap test scenario and its goldens, the keyring and docs/systems regenerated. The published swap-record schema examples stay the 1.0 documents they are.
- The lab-in-a-box lake catalog is Lakekeeper v0.14.1 (was v0.9.5; `quay.io/lakekeeper/catalog:v0.14.1` pinned by its multi-arch index digest, read from quay; v0.14.2 is inside the 14-day cooldown), for `catalog-migrate` and `lake-catalog` alike; the example registry's lake-catalog `version` follows (0.5.2 -> 0.14.1), with its schema copy, generator goldens, keyring and docs/systems page regenerated (the negatives and other fixtures that copy the registry keep their own versions, as before). Since 0.11 the catalog vends a temporary credential by default to a client that names no access-delegation mode, where it can; `lab_init.py` now creates the warehouse with `remote-signing-enabled: true` stated beside `sts-enabled: false` and an access-key credential, so no credential is vended and clients get remote signing (as on 0.9) or nothing, and Trino keeps `vended-credentials-enabled=false`. `tests/compose/test_lab_init_catalog.py` runs the catalog step against a loopback fake of Lakekeeper 0.14's management API (bootstrap once, the warehouse payload, idempotent reruns, loud failures) and pins the delegation rule. The loopback lake fake now serves the warehouse `prefix` under `defaults`, where Lakekeeper has always served it (0.9 too; the fake had it under `overrides`, which was wrong); `RestCatalog` reads either, and the census test's catalog fake keeps `overrides` on purpose for the other branch. docs/architecture.md notes the 0.13 storage-layout change for new namespaces and the 0.14 OIDC settings a production catalog must meet. A fresh lab needs nothing; a kept catalog database is migrated forward by `catalog-migrate` (one way).
- The lab-in-a-box secrets service is OpenBao 2.7.1 (was 2.1.1; `openbao/openbao:2.7.1` pinned by its multi-arch index digest, read from Docker Hub with an anonymous token and checked against the index body's own sha256). 2.7.1 (2026-10-01) is the newest release past the 14-day cooldown; 2.7.2 and 2.6.5 (2026-10-26) are inside it. The example registry's `secrets` `version`, its schema copy, the generator goldens, the keyring, docs/systems/secrets.md and the adapter's pinned line (and so docs/capabilities/secrets.md) follow. Licence re-read for the minor bump: MPL-2.0, the `LICENSE` file byte-identical between v2.1.1 and v2.7.1 (`tools/licences.yml` unchanged). Upstream changes 2.2 to 2.7 read against this repository: the lab runs `server -dev`, in memory, so the storage changes (legacy barrier entries and direct Shamir keys dropped in 2.2, the `file` backend removed in 2.7) and the seal, plugin and LDAP/Kerberos/RADIUS removals (2.7) do not apply; the dev flags and `BAO_DEV_ROOT_TOKEN_ID`/`BAO_DEV_LISTEN_ADDRESS` are unchanged, and the service now passes `-dev-no-store-token` as the image's own default has since 2.2; dev mode still mounts `secret/` as KV v2; the healthcheck and smoke read only `/v1/sys/health`'s status (2.6 dropped the `performance_standby` and `last_wal` fields), and the image is still Alpine, so the `wget` healthcheck stands.
