The API the `secrets` adapter, the conformance fake, the `hnx_secret` lookup, `vault-pass-client.py` and `hnx secrets seed` use (KV v2 data/metadata, `sys/mounts`, ACL policies, `auth/token/create`, `lookup-accessor`, `revoke-accessor`, `revoke-self`, AppRole login) is unchanged: the token store's accessor handlers and the KV v2 metadata delete answer as in 2.1 (`invalid accessor` 400, 204 for a missing path); 2.5.4 removed only the legacy `sys/renew`, `sys/revoke*` lease endpoints (GHSA-v8v8-cm84-m686), which nothing here calls. `hnx ssh-ca` output needs no change: its SSH roles list principals literally (2.6 rejects commas only in templated `allowed_users`/`allowed_domains`), every field it writes still exists on the SSH and OIDC roles, and its OIDC roles use the default client callback (2.5's confirmation prompt is for `callback_mode=direct`). **For overlay sites:** docs/private-overlay.md now rotates unseal keys with `bao operator rotate-keys` (the authenticated `sys/rotate/root/*` endpoints): since 2.5 a listener refuses the unauthenticated `sys/rekey/*` endpoints behind the deprecated `bao operator rekey` (`disable_unauthed_rekey_endpoints` defaults to `true`). Upgrading a production OpenBao is the overlay's own change; 2.6 starts the container as the `openbao` user and 2.7 drops the image's `VOLUME`s, so a server-mode container must mount its data directory explicitly.
- The lab-in-a-box `secrets` (OpenBao) container now runs with the same `no-new-privileges` and `cap_drop: [ALL]` as the others, and without `cap_add: [IPC_LOCK]`: the 2.7.1 image starts as the `openbao` user (`USER openbao`, read from the image config), so its entrypoint neither chowns its directories nor switches user, and OpenBao has not used mlock since 2.0. `tests/compose` has no hardening exception left; docs/architecture.md says so.
- Lab-in-a-box images refreshed within their current release line (each still `tag@sha256:` of the multi-arch index, digests read from Docker Hub; each upstream release past the 14-day cooldown, though an official image can be rebuilt later, e.g. the postgres 16.15-alpine digest was pushed 2026-10-26): postgres 16.4-alpine -> 16.15-alpine, nats 2.10.22-alpine -> 2.10.29-alpine (and CI's `NATS_SERVER_VERSION` v2.10.29 with its module sum), prom/prometheus v2.54.1 -> v2.55.1 (a minor bump; the last 2.x), louislam/uptime-kuma 1.23.16 -> 1.23.17, openbao/openbao 2.1.0 -> 2.1.1, litellm/litellm-database v1.103.3 -> v1.103.7, and the broker and fakes images' base python:3.12-slim-bookworm -> python:3.12.15-slim-bookworm (3.12.16 is inside the cooldown). The example registry's `version` for records-db, events, secrets, metrics and uptime follows the pins (events had drifted to 2.10.20), with its schema copy, generator goldens, keyring and docs/systems pages regenerated. Major and 0.x-minor upgrades (postgres 18, nats 2.11+, prometheus 3, uptime-kuma 2, openbao 2.7, trino, lakekeeper, ntfy, LiteLLM minors, open-webui) are separate changes.
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the lab-in-a-box object store is Garage v2.5.0 (was v1.0.1; `dxflrs/garage:v2.5.0` pinned by its multi-arch index digest). `compose/lab-in-a-box/fakes/lab_init.py` now speaks Garage's admin API v2 (`/v2/GetClusterStatus`, `GetNodeInfo`, `GetClusterLayout`, `UpdateClusterLayout`, `ApplyClusterLayout`, `GetKeyInfo`, `ImportKey`, `UpdateKey`, `GetBucketInfo`, `CreateBucket`, `AllowBucketKey`): v2 dropped most of the v1 admin API and refuses the v1 calls whose semantics changed (`GET /v1/status`, `POST /v1/layout`: `400 InvalidRequest`, "v1/ endpoint is no longer supported"), so the v1 initialiser cannot assign a layout. A failed key lookup now stops the run instead of attempting an import. `tests/compose/test_lab_init.py` runs it against a loopback fake shaped from Garage's `garage-admin-v2.json` and its admin handlers. Garage v2 never re-imports a key id it has held, so changing `LAB_S3_ACCESS_KEY`/`LAB_S3_SECRET_KEY` on a kept volume needs `docker compose down -v`. A fresh lab needs nothing; a site that keeps a Garage v1 cluster's volumes, or calls the admin API from its own tooling, follows the upstream [migration to v2](https://garagehq.deuxfleurs.fr/documentation/working-documents/migration-2/) (`garage repair --all-nodes --yes tables`, a metadata snapshot, stop every v1 node, start them all on v2; `replication_mode` becomes `replication_factor`) and moves its admin calls to `/v2/` (docs/architecture.md).
- `compose/lab-in-a-box/smoke.sh` prints, for a capability whose conformance run fails, the run's verdict line and each failed, untested or erroring check with its evidence (at most 20 lines of 300 characters, credential values masked), so the job log names the check; the full log stays in the report directory.
- `tools/licence_check.py` also requires a reviewed licence for the base image of every image a compose stack builds (a Dockerfile's `FROM` under `compose/`, other than an earlier stage or `scratch`): `python` (PSF-2.0, the lab broker and fakes images' base) is listed. `tools/licences.yml`, the dependabot entries for images and CONTRIBUTING say that a minor or major image bump re-reads the image's licence, which the gate does not do for a listed image.
- Dependabot also watches the lab-in-a-box compose images (`docker-compose`, patch updates grouped, a minor or major alone; the locally built `hnx-lab-*` images ignored), the base image of the lab's broker and fakes images (`docker`, one group so both keep one pin), the `.pre-commit-config.yaml` hook revisions (`pre-commit`; not ruff's, which moves with the constraints through `make constraints`) and the example private overlay's workflows (in the one github-actions entry, so a grouped update moves an action everywhere at once). The `gomod` entry for `/orchestrator` and `/orchestrator/tools`, which do not exist, is gone; `tests/packaging/test_orchestrator_tree.py` still fails when a Go module lands without one. `tests/packaging/test_dependabot.py` holds every entry to the pip entry's weekly schedule and 14-day cooldown, fails on a listed directory that does not exist or holds nothing the ecosystem reads, and on a tracked workflow tree, action, compose file or Dockerfile that no entry watches.
- CI tools and actions bumped to the newest release past the 14-day cooldown, each SHA from `git ls-remote` on the tag and each checksum from the upstream's own record: `GO_VERSION` 1.26.10 (the newest 1.26 patch; Go 1.27 is a separate change); actionlint v1.7.12 (module sum checked against sum.golang.org); gitleaks 8.30.1 (`gitleaks_8.30.1_checksums.txt`); github/codeql-action v4.39.1; pypa/gh-action-pypi-publish v1.14.3; pre-commit-hooks v6.0.0 (it drops `check-byte-order-marker` and `fix-encoding-pragma`, neither of which is used). Held back by the cooldown: actions/setup-python v7.1.0, tailscale/github-action v4.3.0/v4.4.0, zizmor 1.32.0, ruff 0.17.2/0.17.3 and caddy 2.11.8.
- docs/modules.md (2,000 lines) is split: the page keeps the overview (why, words, layout, the manifest at a glance, the three kinds) and a table naming the reference page under `docs/modules/` that holds each section (slots, contributions, data, egress, TLP, supply chain, selection, network scope, loader, loader at deploy time, census, swap, product-name lint, follow-ups). `tools/tests/test_doc_facts.py` caps every module page at 250 lines and checks that each `docs/modules.md, "Section"` citation in the tree names a heading or a paragraph lead on one of them (it found `"Model roles"` in `homelabnext/modules/rules.py`, now `"Model-role ceilings"`); anchor links into moved sections point at their new pages.
- One schema-registry loader: `homelabnext.schema_registry` (`schema_registry`, the new `registry_of` and `validator`) is the only place a `referencing` registry is built. The agent, broker-workflow, decision-lint, event-envelope, register, status, status-agents, swap-record, MCP and X-03 validators and `tools/schema_check.py` used about a dozen hand-rolled copies (each its own directory walk, some over a subset of the schema families); they now all resolve `$ref`s through it. `tools/shared_helpers_lint.py` gains rule `schema`: a call to `referencing.Registry()`, `Resource.from_contents` or a specification's `create_resource` outside `homelabnext/schema_registry.py` fails the lint (checklist M-03).
- Reading the lake at `red` is never a default (ADR-0033 decision 6): `homelabnext.lake.ceiling.reader_for(lake, reader, tlp_readers, schemas)` gives a consumer its `CeilingReader` at the ceiling the reader's `tlp_readers` entry declares and refuses a reader with no declaration (or two, or a non-canonical ceiling); a `red` `CeilingReader` needs a `RedGrant(holder, why)` (`reader_for` makes it from a `red` declaration's `why`), and `raw_query`/`raw_named` with a grant are the one raw read path outside `homelabnext/lake/`. The swap backend and rehearsal and the parity checks read through it under their own grants, and `tests/lake/test_lake_read_boundary.py` fails on any other module that imports the lake library and calls a client's `query` or `named` itself (the conformance harness, whose subject is that method, is exempt). Migration: `CeilingReader(lake, Label.RED, schemas)` now needs `grant=RedGrant(...)`.
- Single sign-on goes through an OIDC IdP behind a neutral contract (ADR-0034, accepted): Keycloak is the default `idp` and an auth proxy (registry capability `auth-proxy`, oauth2-proxy by default) is the edge's forward auth. The `caddy` generator's `hnx_forward_auth` snippet proxies the auth proxy's `/oauth2/*` paths, asks its `/oauth2/auth` check, copies `X-Auth-Request-User`, `-Groups` and `-Email` and redirects a 401 to `/oauth2/sign_in`; every public site strips `*auth-request*` and `*auth_request*` at site level (the IdP's own site too), and a public `auth: idp` service with no `auth-proxy` service fails generation instead of being published without a login. The `idp_blueprint` generator is replaced by `idp_realm` (`idp/realm.json`: a confidential OIDC client per `auth: idp` service and one for the auth proxy, default client scopes trimmed to `basic`, PKCE, the groups and client roles of the registry's `roles`, a groups and a roles claim, a browser flow that requires the OTP form, REQUIRED and with no alternative beside it, and the `CONFIGURE_TOTP` required action enabled as a default action; client secrets are secret-store references, never values). The example registry runs Keycloak 26.8.1 and oauth2-proxy 7.15.5 and marks every human-facing service `auth: idp` or a justified `auth: own`.
- The identity headers are one declaration, `homelabnext.edge_identity.IDENTITY_HEADERS` (user, groups, email, the mark every identity header carries, the groups separator `,`): the Caddy generator, `tools/caddy_lint.py` (F-23) and the status hub read it and nothing else. The lint judges the declared set (`caddy_lint.use_identity` for another; most of its tests now run against a non-default set) and keeps failing closed: a mark of several words needs one delete per `-`/`_` spelling of it.
- The status hub reads its viewer and approver groups from its registry row's `roles` (`viewer`, `approver`), the one group-to-role table the realm is rendered from (status agents schema 1.4: `private.viewer_group` and `approvals.approver_group` are only for a row without `roles`, and refused with it); a request needs one of the viewer (or approver) groups, and the groups header is split on `,`.
- v0 cuts (operator decision, 2026-10-31): StackStorm is *experimental* and Code Mode, the strategy stack and the home-built projects store are *frozen*; none is deleted, and each is out of the blocking set: `make check` no longer runs `workflows st2 check` (from `make workflows`), `codemode gen --check` and `golden codemode --check` (from `make gates` and CI's schemas job), `make strategy` or `make projects`; `make test` and CI's test job select `-m "not live and not experimental"`; `make invariants` (CI's invariants job) skips R-09 and P-08, and `hnx invariants run --skip` is repeatable (each adds to the set; it used to keep only the last). Their gates and tests still run in `make experimental` and CI's new `experimental` job, which is allowed to fail and needed by no other job. Code Mode stays at CM-6 (CM-7 and CM-8 not built; ADR-0031 reads "frozen (2026-10-31): resumes after the core lab runs"); projects and tasks live in OpenProject only, the Markdown store kept as the `projects` suite's reference target because the hub and the graph read it; the lessons, runbooks and invariants registers stay blocking. docs/build-order.md and docs/architecture.md name the v0 slice (registry and aliases, OpenBao, the lake, Sigma detections, the broker, notify, the status hub, SSO through Keycloak), and docs/workflows.md says that the playbook engine and the orchestration controller do different jobs (when a pack runs; agent-session scheduling) instead of implying the controller runs packs.
- ADR and doc drift closed: ADR-0017 decision 4 names `allow_agent_approvers` and how it is held to the decision table; ADR-0027 lists `provider_for` as not done; ADR-0028 counts the front-matter summaries copied into the indexes among the prose generators read, with their drift check; ADR-0033 decision 13 says an analyzer with no declared maximum is sent nothing (not read as a `red` maximum); docs/adr/README.md names the reserved ADR-0026 (`tools/tests/test_adr_log.py` holds it to `RESERVED`); the glossary separates the tiers, the capability sets and `cap:` ids, and adds module, slot, provider, TLP label and ceiling and lane, and drops a "boards" register that does not exist; architecture.md describes the lab bus's per-user accounts, aligns principle 2 with ADR-0027 and gives the README's adoption order; compute-model.md says `nftables_host` writes `ip6` rules for a zone with `subnet6`; pyproject's and interfaces.md's notes on the `records` and `decide` extras say they are unused.
- ADR-0012 (amended) says where hypervisor code lives: the guest model's render targets in `homelabnext/compute/gen/`, a rebuild aid and not a swap seam; nothing outside `homelabnext/compute/` imports a target except the loader reading the node mirror's unit name (`tests/compute/test_render_targets_confined.py`). docs/compute-model.md no longer calls a second target "swapping the hypervisor stack".
- ADR-0011 (amended) scopes the copyleft rule: refused for linked or vendored code, allowed for separately run tools and deployed services, which `NOTICE` records (ansible-core, the lab-in-a-box object store image). CI's dependency review also denies SSPL-1.0 and exempts ansible-core, the one separately run tool; `tools/tests/test_licence_policy.py` holds the deny list to the ADR.
- ADR-0001 (amended) and docs/interfaces.md describe schema majors as they are bumped: in place, the earlier major kept frozen under `schemas/_frozen/`, stored documents migrated, no dual-write window; `tools/schema_check.py` checks additivity within a major only. The ADR names the `x-draft` and `x-defs-only` markers and the append-only baselines, and records that the table-name lint of decision 2 is not built.
- M-04 names its declared exception: a role's host scan that alerts on its own host's state is a `kind: job` listed in docs/scripts.md (`needrestart-check`, `lynis-dpkg-verify`); `tests/scripts/test_host_scan_alerts_declared.py` fails on a timer-run script that alerts and is not listed.
- `--notify-ceiling` has no default: `hnx broker serve` and `hnx status collect` refuse `--notify-topic` without it (`red` for a channel only the operators read), as ADR-0033 decision 6 refuses a reader without a declared TLP ceiling (docs/broker.md, docs/status.md). `notify.ceiling_from_flag(None)` now raises instead of returning `red`. Add `--notify-ceiling red` to existing units to keep their behaviour.
- The TLP backfill plan labels a source's old rows `red` unless a human records that its items never carried labels of their own (`backfill.Source(..., items_carried_no_labels=True)`, `hnx modules tlp-backfill --items-carried-no-labels ROUTE`; ADR-0033 decision 5; homelabnext/lake/README.md). It used to give a source that does not honour item labels its declared label, though an item's own stricter label was never stored for those rows.
- `tlp_egress.analyzers_for` refuses (`ValueError`, naming the analyzer) a declared maximum that is not a canonical label, instead of silently sending that analyzer nothing; ADR-0033 decision 1 now says stored values and authored tables are canonical and the lenient spellings are for carried labels.
- ADR-0009 (amended) says what is on the bus and who publishes to it: three event classes (reaction triggers, per-item records, decision events), each event type's class listed in docs/events.md ("What goes on the bus"), and two sanctioned publish paths, the forwarder and the broker's outbox relay through the `EventBus` protocol, which is the swap boundary. `tests/events/test_bus_publish_paths.py` fails on a type with no class and on a module that publishes through an `EventBus` without being named in the ADR. Checklist T-01 now asks that every bus event type has a subject and a consumer, not that all events flow through the bus; ADR-0013 decision 4, docs/architecture.md and docs/broker.md agree; ADR-0033's Consequences state that only lake rows carry labels so far, so events, records, broker results and context documents read `red`.
- `make detections` and CI run `detections validate-techniques`.
- `hnx workflows validate`'s `deploy` rule also refuses a pin of a pack that cannot run (`homelabnext.workflows.validate.deployable_problems`): an action whose handler is not in the tree pinned by its `.handler.json`, or an event trigger that pins no producer. These are the StackStorm render's `st2-handler` and `st2-source`, so "deployable" means one thing on every path; `handler_problem` moved to `validate` (still importable from `st2`). `workflows/deploy.yml` no longer pins the reference packs `case-opened-notify`, `new-critical-alert` and `phishing-report`, whose handlers are site code (docs/workflows.md, Deploying). docs/stackstorm.md states that `admit` remembers an event before its run, so a run that fails after admit is not redelivered.
- ADR-0033 (TLP labels on data, a TLP ceiling on every reader) is accepted.
- `hnx custom plan` writes plan schema `hnx.customization.plan/2`: every entry of the resolved view (`role_vars.<role>.<var>`, `hooks`, `template_overlays`, `profiles.<name>`) is a list of `{id, hosts, ...}`, so two customizations the lint accepts on disjoint hosts keep their own hosts instead of collapsing to one role-wide value (and profile vars are no longer merged across disjoint hosts) (docs/customization.md).
- The X-09 invariant resolves customizations through the same `loader.resolve_settings` as `hnx custom` (the tree's `customizations/`, `HNX_CUSTOMIZATIONS_CONFIG`'s `customizations_dir` and `enable`, `HNX_CUSTOMIZATIONS_DIR`) and lints with the enabled set, so the two gates check the same customizations; `hnx custom` now also reads `<root>/customizations`.
- `hnx invariants run` sends its pages through `notify.CeilingSender` at `--notify-ceiling LABEL` (default `red`), and `--notify-cmd` is a notify sender: the runner's pages are a checked egress point (ADR-0033 decision 13; docs/modules.md). Its state file is written with `homelabnext.state.atomic_write_text`.
- The invariants register's early F-01, F-02 and F-03 tombstones move to `retired_legacy:`, which `check-lost` counts and `check-dupes` does not, so each checklist row can get its criterion under its own id (ADR-0019, "Scope").
- `hnx decommission` exits 100 when nothing could be measured (`UNMEASURED`), per the repository's exit-code contract; 1 stays for remnants.
- ADR-0010 and ADR-0003 describe the records write paths as they are: the library and the broker's `records-inbox` lane, sharing validation, rendering and naming; `records` is enforced by lane ownership, not by `shared_helpers_lint`.
- Docs agree with the code: `tools/coverage_floor.py` `UNITS` is the one list of security-critical units (its docstring, CONTRIBUTING rule 9 and the CI comment no longer keep shorter copies that left out the TLP units); CONTRIBUTING says pre-commit runs a subset of CI's checks, that `tools/tls_verify.allow.yml` is created on first need, and how far the Dependabot DCO exemption reaches (the CI comment too); docs/systems.md drops a stale page count; generators/README.md names `tests/generators/test_gen_manifest.py`.
- `hnx agent lint` checks each context manifest against the profiles it names (rule `manifests-fit-their-profiles`, run with the other two): context manifest 1.2 adds an optional `profiles` list, `start_session` refuses a profile a manifest that names any does not name, and the lint reports for every named pair what `start_session` would refuse (`session_problems`: persona, an actor fed raw input, tools beyond the profile's MCP grants, memory scopes beyond its own) and a named profile no linted file has; a manifest that names none is a note. The persona check moved into `session_problems`. The example manifests name `profile:reader` (docs/agent.md).
- The broker tiers and the grant lookup have one definition, `homelabnext.grants` (`TIERS`, `TIER_RANK`, `grant_for`, `tier_exceeds`): the broker's evaluator, the profile compiler's ceiling check, the reader-to-actor handoff, the access block and keyring, and `hnx workflows validate` read it instead of four copies of the tier ranking and three grant lookups (`homelabnext.broker.TIER_RANK` and `homelabnext.agent.TIER_RANK` are the same object). `tools/shared_helpers_lint.py` gains rule `tiers` (a dict literal ranking the four tiers anywhere else), and `tools/coverage_floor.py` measures `homelabnext/grants.py`.
- `homelabnext.mcp.servers.Catalog` and `CatalogError` are renamed `ServerCatalog` and `ServerCatalogError`: `homelabnext.mcp.catalog` has its own, unrelated `Catalog` and `CatalogError`, so `except CatalogError` caught one or the other depending on the import. The old names still resolve for one release and warn (`DeprecationWarning`).
- A `data_tier` maps to a TLP label in one place, `homelabnext.registry.model_tlp` (`DATA_TIERS`, `tier_label`, `receive_ceiling`, beside `send_ceiling` and `data_tier_of`): the reader rule's `server_label`, the MCP catalog's `record_label` and the gateway's tier list used to spell it out themselves. `tests/models/test_data_tier_one_mapping.py` fails on a module that maps a tier literal to a `Label` or spells the tiers out anywhere else.
- The conformance capabilities of frozen components (`codemode-executor`, `codemode-runner`) are out of the blocking set (`projects` stays blocking: the home-built store is frozen, but the same suite holds the OpenProject adapter, the lab's one projects backend, to the contract), as `tools/experimental.yml` says those components are: `homelabnext.conform.EXPERIMENTAL` lists them (held equal to the components' new `conform:` lists by `tools/tests/test_experimental.py`), `hnx conform report` leaves them out unless named with the new repeatable `--capability` and records them under `experimental_skipped`, so `make conform-check` (now sixteen capabilities) and invariant X-10 no longer fail on a frozen suite, and `make experimental` runs `hnx conform report --capability` for each. `hnx conform list` still prints the eighteen (docs/conformance.md, docs/architecture.md, docs/codemode.md).
- Raw-endpoint detection is package code, `homelabnext/endpoints_scan.py`, the one copy: `tools/no_raw_endpoints.py` is its command line (same output and exit codes, and it still exposes `scan_text`, `scan_tree` and `load_allowlist`), and the customization lint and the agent home check (A-14) call the module. `homelabnext/endpoint_gate.py`, which loaded the checkout tool in-process, is removed with the reduced detection each caller fell back to outside a checkout, so an installed package now checks what CI checks (a `host:port` outside a URL was missed by the lint's fallback) and `hnx gen gateway homes` no longer warns that its scan is reduced. The path-glob allowlist file the endpoint, TLS-verify, shared-helpers and workstation-path gates read is one reader, `homelabnext.allowlist.entries` (same messages and exit codes; the shared-helpers lint now names a missing `reason` before an unknown `rule`).

### Added
- The controller's core starts to join `orchestrator/` (ADR-0026 stage 2, PR 2.1; docs/orchestration-controller.md, "The controller's core in this repository"): the leaf packages `internal/safefs` (reads, lists and writes under one root with no symbolic link, hard link, mount crossing or swapped component able to redirect them; a root on a network or FUSE filesystem refused unless `SAFEFS_ALLOW_REMOTE_FS=1`), `internal/pathscope` (scope checks on path strings; a tree's zones, `denied_roots`, `findings_root`, `shared_write` and `reserved_names`, are configuration with no defaults, and no zone name is hard-coded), `internal/metrics` (the text exposition writer, neutral prefix `hnx_orchestrator_`) and `internal/events` (the CloudEvents envelope and durable outbox sender, default source `svc:orchestrator`), each rewritten for this repository without the private history. Nothing emits the new names yet; the one-release overlap with the private controller's `llmorch_` prefix and `svc:llm-orchestrator` source, and the consumers to move, are recorded for the cutover after stage 3. The events tests validate what the package builds against `schemas/events` in place, with a test-only validator checked against the schemas' own examples and negatives. Two corrections over the private copy: the derived event id escapes non-ASCII text as `homelabnext.events.envelope.derive_event_id` does (the two disagreed on such input), and a forwarder answer that blames the configuration (`401`, `403`, `404`, `421`, a redirect) keeps a durable event pending and retried, as the forwarder contract says, instead of dropping it; an event built with no data carries an empty object, which the envelope requires. The module's first third-party dependency, `golang.org/x/sys` v0.47.0 (released 2026-06-30, BSD-3-Clause, now `linked` in `tools/licences.yml`), and its first `go.sum`; `TestGatewayDependsOnlyOnTheStandardLibrary` keeps the gateway's packages standard library only. `orchestrator/skips.yml` registers the two bind-mount tests as root-only.
- The orchestrator joins this repository (ADR-0026, proposed; the number reserved for it): `orchestrator/`, the first Go module (module path `github.com/homelab-next/homelab-next/orchestrator`, Go 1.27.3, standard library only), holds the lab's own LLM gateway of ADR-0038 (`cmd/gateway`, `internal/gateway`, installed as `llm-gateway`), brought in without its private history and rewritten for this repository (documentation addresses, no lab names, comments citing ADR-0038's decisions). Its TLP admission test reads `conformance/fixtures/tlp_admit_vectors.json` in place, so the Go and Python implementations judge one set of vectors. Its linters' module `orchestrator/tools/go.mod` pins staticcheck 0.8.2 and govulncheck 1.9.0, which CI's `go` job now runs; `tools/licences.yml` gains a `go` section, and `make licence-check` reads every `go.mod`'s requirements against it. CI's new `gateway-conformance` job builds the gateway and runs the `inference` suite against it with the fake inference server, in a network namespace with only loopback and under no_new_privs (`tools/gateway_conformance.sh`, which refuses to start when either port is taken); every check must pass, and `go-ok` now requires the job (`tools/tests/test_gateway_conformance.py` holds the job and the script's ports and models to the conformance spec). Dependabot watches both Go modules (`gomod`). [docs/gateway.md](docs/gateway.md) documents the binary, and an example hardened systemd unit (`orchestrator/deploy/systemd/llm-gateway.service`, no host named) ships with it. ADR-0038 is amended (its acceptance run is this in-repository job; the cross-repository pin goes away), and so is ADR-0011 decision 1 (operator-owned generic code may come in fresh, without its history, through the gates). ADR-0026 also decides, for later pull requests: the agent-runner contract is public core, in an ADR of its own, with the agent runtime as one provider module; MCP servers become modules with catalog records, public or overlay per module; a module's Go code lives at `modules/<id>/go/` as its own Go module and imports only a public SDK module (`orchestrator/mcpkit`, pinned by version from the overlay); and the staged order, each component cutting over as soon as it can run the lab's work (the gateway as soon as it lands, the controller after stage 3), until the private repository is archived.
- Acceptance of the lab's own LLM gateway (ADR-0038 decision 7, proposed; amended 2026-11-07 with the gateway's departures R15 to R18; docs/llm-gateway.md, "Running the suite against the lab's own gateway"). The `inference` suite gains the `tlp-admit` feature (`TlpAdmitGateway`, `LabelledClient`): seven checks that send `X-HNX-TLP` over HTTP (no field to a role that leaves the lab is a 403 `unlabelled`, never a fallback; a label within the ceiling is served in every accepted spelling; `amber` is refused; the admin key's `clear` is judged at its `red`; an empty field and two fields read as `red`, `above-ceiling`; a value that is no label (`bogus`, a non-ASCII look-alike) reads as `red`; a fallback refused at admission is skipped, which `fallback-refused-at-admission-skipped` proves from the call's turn record), with four mutants. `hnx gen gateway render --conformance [--conformance-host HOST]` (a loopback host) is the test-only render: a spec's `conformance.cloud_base_url` (refused without the flag) points cloud deployments at the fake inference server, the render writes `gateway/` alone with `"conformance_only": true`, and its audit requires that where a lab render's refuses it. The conformance spec `compose/lab-in-a-box/config/llm-gateway-conformance/` (a test-only role table: a role that leaves the lab, a failing role whose first fallback is refused by its token limit, clear- and red-ceiling keys, the throttle key, the telemetry reader) and its committed render, held by `make gateway-check`; the target `compose/lab-in-a-box/targets/inference-own-gateway.yml` with `streaming`, `embeddings`, `gateway`, `tlp-ceiling`, `tlp-admit` and `turn-records`. The turn source `homelabnext.conform.adapters.gateway_usage:make` reads the own gateway's `GET /v1/usage` with the telemetry reader's key (paging by time with turn-id de-duplication at the 10000 cap; `model` is the serving deployment; status and error code from the HTTP status; cache figures null when unreported; an aggregated refusal of `count` n becomes n turns; an aggregate that is a 2xx or spends, a page over the cap, a read over `MAX_RECORDS * max_pages` turns and a key whose value is the admin key's are refused). The test-only render refuses a cloud backend without `conformance.cloud_base_url`. `OpenAICompat.chat_fields` sends a chat with extra header fields, each a field of its own, and `InferenceError.code` carries the error envelope's code.
- The `inference` suite's embeddings checks call the target's `embed_model` (default: `model`). The lab-in-a-box role table (`schemas/platform/examples/model_roles.valid.json`) gains the `embedder` role (`example-embed-8d`, served by the fake inference server), the LiteLLM target sets `embed_model: embedder`, and the rendered lab gateway (`litellm/`, `gateway/`) and `gateway-models-match-roles` include it.
- The product-neutral LLM gateway config (ADR-0038 decision 1, proposed; docs/llm-gateway.md, "The neutral gateway config"): `hnx gen gateway render` also writes `gateway/gateway.json` beside `litellm/`, to the new draft schema `platform/gateway_config` 1.0 (`homelabnext.generators.llm_gateway.render_neutral`), from the same role table, profiles and spec: each role's kind, context limit, chain-inclusive TLP ceiling and `leaves_lab` (`role_ceiling`, `cloud_roles`), its deployments in binding order (protocol `openai` or `anthropic`, base URL without `/v1`, the real provider API for a cloud one), fallbacks and whole (unsplit) limits; each key's environment name, owner, data tier, sending ceiling, roles and limits; the admin and telemetry-reader environment names, never a value. Its generated-by line and digest live in the sibling `gateway/.hnx-generated` (JSON carries no comment, and the contract allows no extra key), which also records the file's sha256. A spec the contract cannot express (a cloud provider other than openai or anthropic, an embedding role on anthropic, a role `max_cost` without `cost_per_1k_tokens`) still renders `litellm/`, notes why in `litellm/config.yaml` and on stderr, and an earlier `gateway/gateway.json` is removed (`--check`: stale). The audit (`--check` and every render) reads the file: schema-valid, locality (no local-only or agent role, nor its fallbacks, on a cloud deployment), cloud base URLs, role ceilings and `leaves_lab` equal to the spec's, keys equal to the spec's (tier, ceiling read as `red` when missing or unknown, no widened roles), no key above a reachable role's ceiling, no `local-only` key reaching a cloud or `leaves_lab` role, one credential per environment name, every key listing the fallbacks of each role it lists, and every field the spec decides equal to the spec's (roles, deployments, fallbacks, limits, cost, key environments, owners, roles and limits, admin and reader environments; counts as JSON integers), so a self-consistent hand edit fails even with its marker's sha256 rewritten; a stale `gateway/gateway.json` is removed with or without its marker. The role table now refuses a fallback of another kind (chat/embedding), which the gateway refuses at load. Dotfiles under the data trees (the examples' `.hnx-generated`) are no longer packaged, so the git and sdist wheels match. The three committed gateway dirs are regenerated (the LiteLLM config's digest line changes; nothing else in `litellm/`).
- Agent memory is judged by replay (ADR-0037 PR 5, decision 16): `tests/agent/replay/fixture.yml` is a history of reports and packs (times, sources, labels, a write through memory, an unchecked platform write, a test verifier's stamp and an operator retraction through `MemoryAdmin` partway through) with golden questions placed in it, and `tests/agent/replay/golden.yml` their exact answers: a scope `as_of` T, a key's history, `dependents(S)` cited and exposed (one hop through memory each way), what `retract(S)` retires (by a uid, and by a knowledge item's graph id) and the refusal of later reports citing or rendering S (by the pack's inputs, by its knowledge names, or through a marked memory revision) and the drop of a later unchecked platform write citing it (its closure), the unsourced (the store's meaning: the last `add` or `replace` named no source), unchecked and stale entries (all stale but the one the test verifier confirmed, and that one too once its budget has passed), and what a reader at a lowered ceiling sees withheld. `tests/agent/test_agent_memory_replay.py` replays it into a fresh store on `InMemoryBackend` and `FileBackend` (its deny file read from the operators file with `deny_path_from`) and prints a unified diff per answer that differs, reading the goldens so that a time of day such as `10:12` stays a string whether quoted or not; a test proves on both backends that making `dependents` stop following `mem:` hops, making staleness ignore its budget, or turning off the name-based deny check fails it. It runs in `make test` and `make check`; `make memory-replay` runs it alone. Test and docs only; no behaviour changes.
- Agent memory staleness (ADR-0037 PR 4, decision 15, with its 2026-11-06 amendment): a model reasserts a fact, only a verifier verifies it. `MemoryStore` applies `op: verify` (in `report_result` 1.2 since PR 2, refused by the store until now): it takes no value, needs a live key, bumps `rev`, adds its sources and leaves the value unchanged, and it must cite at least one source the pack rendered that is not a `mem:` revision (`homelabnext.agent.report.verify_problems`, checked by `validate_report` and the store; an unchecked platform write refuses it). `add`, `replace` and `verify` set the entry's `reasserted_at`. `verified_at` (on the record and the entry) is set only by a `homelabnext.agent.memory.Verifier` (a protocol, `verify(VerifyRequest) -> bool`; none ships) passed as `MemoryStore(verifier=)`, which is asked about every `add`, `replace` and `verify` of an accepted batch (never a refused one: the record cap, the byte cap, with the bytes its stamps can add reserved, and the exposure budget are checked first; a write that fails after the verifier was asked (I/O) is not a refusal) inside the transaction, with the record's label (`VerifyRequest.tlp`) so it can keep to its egress ceiling; an unconfirmed `replace` clears it, and a `verify` the verifier answers `False` is recorded `refuted` and clears it too (one it cannot answer keeps the earlier stamp). `read(..., stale_after=)` and `as_of(..., stale_after=)` add `stale: {key: {reasserted_at, verified_at?}}` for entries whose `verified_at` is absent, older than the budget or later than the judging time (a forward clock fault that a restamp corrected never reads as fresh), so until a verifier ships every entry is stale; a budget past the calendar's start marks only unverified entries stale rather than raising. `memory_block_input(..., stale_after=)` marks entries `stale` with their stamps, and `assemble_pack` records them in the pack's memory block (reported; the renderer decides rank; an entry with no `verified_at` marked not stale is refused). Compaction and the fold carry both stamps on both backends. The `report_result` 1.2 description is updated; no schema change.
- Agent memory is retractable (ADR-0037 PR 3, decisions 11 to 14, 17 and 18, with the 2026-11-06 amendments to decisions 9 and 12 to 14). **Breaking for callers and implementers of `MemoryBackend`, and for readers of exposures, closures and the log:**
  - `MemoryBackend.transact(owner, fn)` now calls `fn(current, deny)` with a copy of the deny state (the deny log and the denied-reach index), read under the deny state's own lock, which is taken first (lock order: deny, then memory); a backend also needs `owners()`, `load_deny()` and `transact_deny(fn)` (append-only, refused otherwise). The deny state is not in the memory state file: `FileBackend(path, deny_path=)` keeps it in its own `DenyFile` (`deny_path` required: the operators file's `deny_file`, read with `memory.deny_path_from`, so the runtime and the operator use one path), read under a shared `flock`, and `InMemoryBackend` in process. Locks are taken without blocking and retried for at most 5 s, then refused (`DenyFileProblem`). A deny file without its lock is still read. `MemoryAdmin` stamps the state file with the deny path and its length (`@deny-stamp`), and the runtime refuses writes, and pages (new core pair `AGENT_MEMORY_DENY`), when its deny file is missing, another path, shorter, or writable by the runtime itself (anywhere up its directory chain); `load_deny` and `denied(backend, notifier=)` refuse and page the same way, so new packs never read an empty deny set from the wrong path. Owners starting `@` are refused.
  - Run as root (an operator under sudo), every `FileBackend` state-file operation runs with the effective uid, gid and groups of the state file's owner, so root never opens, locks or renames a name the runtime controls (a planted lock link is refused), and what it writes stays the runtime's; a linked state file is refused.
  - A record's `closure` now holds each source's platform-issued aliases (a uid brings its digest, a lesson's digest its uid and graph id) and a cited `mem:` revision's own closure, so it reaches the 256 cap sooner: 16 uid sources count 32.
  - A pack exposure is `{rendered, memory, pairs}`: `memory` lists the `mem:` revisions the pack's memory block showed and `pairs` each rendered item's platform-issued names (indexes into `rendered`, graph ids as themselves); pairs count toward `exposure_bytes`.
  - The log gains operator records: `retire` records with `reason`, `by` and `by_sudo` (record field list), and two event ops that change no entry, `purge` and `restamp` (`EVENT_OPS`, beside `aged`). A purged value's digests become a random `purged:<hex>` token.
  - `as_of` also returns `purged`, the keys whose value at `t` an operator purged.
  - `validate_report` refuses a report whose `profile` is not the profile its pack was assembled for.
  - Pack hashes do not change: a pack gains `memory` only when assembled with `memory=`, and `retracted` only when a deny set left something out.
- The pack's memory block: `assemble_pack(..., memory=, profile=)` takes `memory_block_input(store, profile, scopes)` (the profile's own live entries within its ceiling) and records `memory: [{scope, key, rev, value_digest, tlp}]`, whose labels count toward the pack's. A `mem:` source is valid only when it names an entry of the block; one naming another owner is refused before any lookup with the same message as an unshown revision, and no update may cite any revision of its own key.
- `homelabnext.memory_admin.MemoryAdmin(backend)`, the operator's tool, outside `homelabnext.agent` (a test resolves every import spelling in the agent package, the broker and orchestration, and checks `sys.modules` in a subprocess). It writes only through a public operator seam of `homelabnext.agent.memory` (`stored_of`, `state_records`, `last_record`, `log_clock`, `operator_retire`, `owner_key`).
  - `dependents` is the dry run. Its alias set comes from platform-issued pairings only and never crosses from a digest to another uid: a digest paired with several uids is listed under `ambiguous` and joins only with `include_alias`, and a lone digest gets a warning. It lists the cited and exposed records across owners (a `mem:` source's own revision included, the walk repeated until a pass finds nothing new, truncated ranges listed) and a confirm digest that covers the settings.
  - `retract` writes the deny records first (with `revision`, `include_exposed` and `include_alias`), then the denied-reach marks, then retires the cited (and, with `include_exposed`, the exposed) entries owner by owner. A rerun with the same settings completes it without `confirm`, other settings need a fresh one, a concurrent lift stops it with `MemoryAdminError`, and retracting twice changes nothing.
  - `lift` makes names and marks stop counting; it restores nothing.
  - `purge` works by value digest (across owners with `"*"`) or by `<scope>/<key>@<rev>`. It retires a current revision, blanks the value everywhere and replaces its digests with a random token; only the dry run shows the digest, and a rerun appends nothing.
  - `restamp` is the clock-skew recovery of decision 3: the dry run lists from -> to, and applying it needs `expect_now` within the skew allowance.
  - Every method takes an `OperatorPrincipal`, built only by `hnx agent memory` from the invoking OS user and re-checked against the process's uids on every call.
  - The operators file (`/etc/homelabnext/memory-operators.yml`; `--operators` only for root) is trusted only through root ownership: it and every directory above it must be owned by uid 0 and writable by no group or other, and it is opened with `O_NOFOLLOW` and parsed from the `fstat`-ed descriptor. It refuses the agent runtime's and broker's uids, the broker manifest's callers, approval relays, operators and Code Mode peers, and the state file's owner. The runtime must not run as root. `SUDO_USER` is recorded as advisory `by_sudo`.
  - A `VerifiedProfile` or a string raises. `MemoryAdmin` may write `fleet-ledger` and any other platform scope (ADR-0035 decision 10, amended).
- `hnx agent memory {dependents,retract,lift,purge,restamp} --state FILE [--operators FILE]`: operator only, a dry run unless `--apply`; the deny file is the operators file's `deny_file`; the state file's path must hold no link and be owned only by root or the runtime, and a corrupt state file is refused (exit 2), never quarantined; the state file's uid and gid are pinned at the start, a second hard link is refused, only the state file's own directory may be the runtime's, and the state lock is waited on for at most 5 s (`StateStore(lock_timeout=)`, `file_lock(timeout=)`, `StateLockBusy`; exit 2); exit 2 for an unreadable operators, state or deny file.
- Writes re-check the deny set inside their transaction:
  - A pack that rendered a denied knowledge name or a denied input item, or whose memory block shows a marked revision, refuses the write whole. So does a checked batch with an update whose closure reaches a denied name or cites a marked revision.
  - An unchecked platform batch drops such an update, reports it in `dropped` and pages (`AGENT_MEMORY_DROPPED`).
  - `denied(backend)` gives the active `DenySet` (`homelabnext.agent.deny`; `marked` is indexed by key) for `select_knowledge(..., retracted=)` (exclusion reason `retracted`) and `assemble_pack(..., retracted=)`.
  - The assembler denies an input item by uid, or by digest only under a `revision=this` retraction.
  - A section declaring a denied input item is left out whole. A required one is quarantined (listed in `omitted` and `retracted.quarantined`, and paged by the caller with `pack.quarantine_page`, new core pair `AGENT_PACK_QUARANTINED`); the pack goes ahead without it (it used to be deferred).
  - A denied knowledge item is dropped (and no longer listed as included). A memory entry that is marked, or whose closure reaches a denied name or cites a marked revision, is left out of the block.
- `hnx broker validate` refuses every action in the `memory.*` namespace but the notes lane's `memory.write` (`memory.retract`, `lift`, `purge`, `dependents`, `restamp` and any other), in manifest actions and handler documents, and any handler that names `homelabnext.memory_admin`.
- `homelabnext.agent.report.served_report(report, reader=, author=)` (`author` the validated author; a report whose `profile` is not it is refused): a report served to a profile other than its author carries `memory_updates` as `{count: n}` (ADR-0037 decision 18); ADR-0035's renderer and the context store's level reads by digest must call it once they serve reports.
- The coverage floor (Q-07) also covers `homelabnext/memory_admin` and `homelabnext/agent/deny.py`, measured in one run with the other agent units over `tests/agent` and `tests/memory_admin`, so `tests/agent` is traced once. The memory invariants fuzz runs 80 steps per seed on the in-memory backend and 40 on the file backend (was 120 on both), with retract and lift events, keeping `tests/agent` inside its coverage budget.
- Agent memory is sourced and labelled (ADR-0037 PR 2, decisions 7 to 10, with the 2026-11-06 amendments to decisions 6, 7, 8 and 10 and follow-up 6). **Breaking for callers of `MemoryStore` and `assemble_pack`:**
  - `apply_report` needs the pack the report ran on (`pack=`, `pack_hash=`), and that pack must be bound to the reporting profile (`assemble_pack(..., profile=)`, which `start_session` passes; the pack records `profile: {id, digest}`).
  - `apply_updates` needs such a pack, or `unchecked=True`, which writes only the profile's `platform_scopes`.
  - Writes return only what they touched, filtered by the writer's ceiling: `{revs, unsourced, withheld, dropped}`, never the entries; `read` returns `{entries, withheld}` like `history` and `as_of`.
  - Every memory read is `red` until packs carry labels, so a profile whose ceiling is below `red` reads its whole memory as withheld.
  - Every pack hash changes: packs gain `provenance` and `profile`.
  - `inputs["items"]` is now validated, and refused unless `resolved_uids` is a `ResolvedUids` made by `resolve_input_uids` from a records or lake reader's stored rows; `hnx agent pack` refuses `items`.
  - `tlp` on an input section is refused (by `context_manifest` 1.3 and the assembler).
- `report_result` 1.2 (additive) adds `sources` on a memory update (at most 16 unique, each a `sha256:` digest, a UUIDv7 record uid or `mem:<profile id>/<scope>/<key>@<rev>`, anchored as in `ids/common`) and `verify` to `op`, which the store refuses until PR 4; `validate_report` and the store refuse more than 64 `memory_updates` or 128 `cites`.
- The pack's `provenance` block records only what it rendered (a trimmed section counts as rendered): each knowledge item's id, record uid, digests and `tlp` (its node's declared label read as stored, `red` when absent), each input item `{uid, digest, tlp}` (digest and label from the reader's stored row, a caller's label only raising it), each section's `tlp` (pre-gates `red`, static sections the manifest's `tlp` from `context_manifest` 1.3, input sections their items' and any TLP marker in their text) and the pack's. A uid paired with two digests, a digest with two uids, or a pack over 1,024 rendered identifiers (`rendered_count`) is refused at assembly.
- Every source must equal an identifier the pack rendered: text and budget-cut items never are, and every `mem:` source is refused with one message until PR 3. A record's `tlp` is the strictest of the pack's, its sources' and the entry's current label; unchecked platform writes are `red`. Reads take the ceiling from the `VerifiedProfile` and report entries above it only as a count; `history` answers a withheld key and a missing one alike, `as_of` says `aged_out` only within the ceiling, and refusals name positions, never keys.
- Each checked batch stores its pack's exposure once per owner, dropped when no record points at it; each record stores its closure (at most 256: a checked batch past it is refused, an unchecked platform update past it is dropped, reported and paged once per crossing through the new core pair `AGENT_MEMORY_DROPPED`); a range record keeps its `effective` sources beside the union and at most 256 value digests and pack ids (`truncated`). Profile schema 1.7 (additive) adds `memory_limits.bytes`, a per-scope byte cap over records only (default 2,000 bytes per record of the cap), and `memory_limits.exposure_bytes`, the owner's exposure budget (default 50,000,000 bytes); both refuse and page as the record cap does.
- Service registry schema 2.8 (additive): an egress service's row (`notify`, `events`, `backup`, `webhook`) may declare `tlp_ceiling`, the TLP ceiling of what it receives (ADR-0033 decision 6), refused on any other row; `hnx broker serve` and `hnx status collect` read their notify channel's ceiling from the row (`notify.ceiling.channel_ceiling`) and refuse a `--notify-ceiling` that disagrees with it, so the ceiling is declared once per receiver instead of restated per invocation.
- `hnx status views check --preview public|private` prints the document a tier is shown, built from `--state` with no server: the public tier's `status.json` through its release transform, or the whole document.
- Agent memory is historied (ADR-0037 PR 1, decisions 1 to 6, decision 6 amended). `MemoryStore` keeps a per-owner append-only change log, written in the same backend transaction as the entry, so a refused batch writes neither (both backends); entries are a cache of the log (`fold`) and carry `rev` (never restarted or reused), `updated_at` and `reasserted_at`; memory stored before the log migrates once, on its owner's next write, and a legacy secret migrates as a `purged` marker only, with no value and no digest. The clock is injected (`now=`) and the log's time never goes backwards: a lag of up to 5 minutes is absorbed, and a larger one refuses the batch and pages once (`AGENT_MEMORY_CLOCK`). Every string of a batch, or of a whole report, passes the screen's secret types (`screen.SECRET_TYPES`) and the leak scanner's credential shapes (the age key, vault blob and AWS secret key shapes now live in `homelabnext.shapes` for both) before anything else; refusals name a position and a type or a schema keyword, never a value. A batch holds at most 64 updates, and a report may not write a platform scope. `history` and `as_of` are owner-scoped reads (`as_of` answers `compacted`, never a wrong value). Profile schema 1.6 (additive) adds `platform_scopes` and `memory_limits`: per-scope record caps (defaults 10,000, and 100,000 for a platform scope) with a refusal that pages once per crossing through the notifier (`AGENT_MEMORY_CAP`, keyed per owner and scope); in a platform scope `keep_last` folds older records into one range record, a retired key folds to one tombstone, and tombstones past `memory_limits.tombstones` (default a tenth of the cap) age out under a high-water `rev` (only a retired key's tombstone ages, and a key's next `rev` is above both its own and the high-water `rev`), so the scope stays bounded for every key a `keep_last` pattern or retirement covers. Every record is `sources: []`, `checked: false`, `tlp: red` until PR 2.
- CI runs the lab-in-a-box's LLM gateway profile on every pull request (and push to main) that can affect the lab: the new `lab-gateway` job, gated by the same `tools/lab_paths.py` decision as `lab-in-a-box` and summed up by the same required `lab-ok` check, brings up the gateway and what it depends on (`--profile gateway up --wait llm-gateway`: LiteLLM v1.106.2 on postgres 18.6 in front of the fake inference server), then runs the `llm-gateway-keys` one-shot in the foreground (`run --rm`, so its exit code fails the job; `up --wait` returns while a one-shot still runs), 1280 MB of `mem_limit` in all. It runs the `inference` suite against `targets/inference-gateway.yml` (12 executed, 5 skipped in a local run), fails unless `gateway-rejects-bad-key`, `gateway-limit-throttles` and `gateway-models-match-roles` all passed (a skipped or missing one fails it), and always collects the logs, tears down with the volumes and uploads the report. Every step runs under `bash -eo pipefail`. Until now the profile had only been rehearsed by hand (#232, #238); docs/llm-gateway.md documents the same sequence. A job of its own rather than a step of `lab-in-a-box`, which stays the nightly's job step for step and inside its 4 GB budget; no secret, the keys are `.env.example`'s throwaway values. `tests/compose` holds the job to the documented path and runs its verdict and conformance steps against passing and failing results; `tools/tests/test_lab_paths.py` holds `lab-ok` to both jobs, and `lab-ok` now refuses a `lab-paths` decision that is neither `true` nor `false`. Key scoping at runtime is not checked: no script for it exists, and the lab target does not declare `tlp-ceiling`.
- ADR-0037 (accepted): agent memory is sourced, historied and retractable. Every applied memory update appends a change record in the same transaction as the entry (`rev` never restarts, entries are derived from the log, the clock is injected, per-scope record caps with compaction for platform scopes, keys and values pass the secret screen first); `history` and `as_of` are owner-scoped reads; an update may name `sources`, accepted only when they equal an identifier the pack assembler wrote for something the pack rendered (`report_result` 1.2, additive, with list limits enforced in code), and a report is applied only with its pack; a record's label is the strictest of the pack's label (pregate sections `red`), its sources and the entry's current label, unchecked platform writes are `red`, and reads withhold entries above the profile's ceiling as a count; sources accumulate across revisions, and each pack's exposure (what it rendered, through memory too) is kept beside what was cited; `mem:` sources name only revisions a pack's `memory` block showed; an operator-only `MemoryAdmin` (never a broker action) shows dependents as a dry run, retracts under every platform-issued alias with a deny list written first and re-checked inside each write's transaction, lifts and purges; a model's `verify` only reasserts a fact; and a replay of fixture reports with golden answers runs in `make check`. It amends ADR-0035 (decision 17 lists what changes). Docs only; five implementation PRs follow it.
- CI runs the real lab-in-a-box stack on every pull request (and push to main) that can affect it, Dependabot's compose, Dockerfile, constraints and action bumps included: until now only the nightly started it, so an image bump showed green without the stack ever coming up. `ci.yml`'s `lab-paths` job decides from the change (`tools/lab_paths.py`: the stack's directory, every `COPY` source of the images it builds, which for the broker image is the whole package, the conformance suites, schemas, profiles and examples, `pyproject.toml` and `constraints/`, the runner's `python-deps` install and the jobs that run it; renames count both sides), `lab-in-a-box` runs the nightly's steps unchanged, and `lab-ok` is the one stable check name for branch protection, passing when the stack passed or when nothing it reads changed. The smoke needs no secret (`.env.example`'s throwaway values), so a Dependabot run with its read-only token and no Actions secrets runs it as is. `tools/tests/test_lab_paths.py` re-derives what the stack reads from the compose file (build contexts, `COPY`/`ADD` sources, bind mounts, `env_file`, target adapters) and fails on anything the list misses, and `tests/compose/test_lab_in_a_box.py` holds the PR job's steps equal to the nightly's. The step-level inputs no file declares are listed by hand (`setup.py`, which the editable install reads; `capabilities.yml` and `registry/`, which `hnx conform report` reads), changed paths are read NUL-separated so a non-ASCII or quoted name is still classified, and the derivation follows continued and JSON-form `COPY`/`ADD` and fails on a Dockerfile line it cannot read (a heredoc, a variable source, a `RUN --mount` of the build context). Most pull requests run the stack, by design: the broker image copies nearly all of `homelabnext/`, `schemas/` and `examples/`, so do not narrow the list to save runner time. Add `lab-ok` to the branch protection's required checks.
- `tools/pin_freshness.py` and the weekly `pin-freshness` workflow: the pins dependabot cannot parse (role `*_version`/`*_image` defaults, CI `*_VERSION` env with the checksum beside it, `GO_VERSION`, the Terraform provider constraint the compute generator writes, and the unpinned uv and trivy) are compared with their upstream's GitHub releases, PyPI or the Go toolchain proxy, past the same 14-day cooldown (younger releases are listed as held), and the report is kept in one tracking issue the workflow finds by a marker. Being behind never fails a check; the workflow runs only on a schedule or by hand from the default branch, with `contents: read` and `issues: write`, and is the only job that asks upstream for versions. `inventory --check` (offline, also a unit test) fails on a pin with no upstream source. The token goes to the GitHub API only (a redirect to another host drops it, one away from https is refused), an answer over 20 MB is refused, and an upstream error reaches the issue as its type and HTTP status or a capped, inert code span. CONTRIBUTING gains "Keeping pins current".
- `tools/tests/test_pin_consistency.py` fails a one-sided bump of a version written in more than one place: ruff (`.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `constraints/ci-tools.in`, `constraints/ci.txt`), the lab's NATS image and CI's `NATS_SERVER_VERSION`, any image repository pinned twice across the lab compose file, its Dockerfiles and role defaults and argument specs (one tag and digest), the open_webui image in docs/model-serving.md, CI's `CADDY_VERSION` and every example registry's `impl: caddy` version (and docs/systems/edge-proxy.md), the zizmor version and the pinned tailscale/github-action named in docs/private-overlay.md, and every action (one full SHA and one version comment across this repository's and the example overlay's workflows and composite actions; a commented-out step is not read). Image pins are read with a registry port, a digest-only pin and Dockerfile `FROM` flags; every `impl: caddy` service of a registry is compared, not the last.
- Behavioural novelty baselines (ADR-0036, proposed; docs/novelty.md): `homelabnext.novelty` learns per-entity first-seen baselines from the lake, recomputed for an injected `as_of` with no model call and no state of its own. A spec (draft schema `platform/novelty_spec` 1.0; `examples/novelty/`: a spec, a site and a fixture lake) declares profiles (a table, the entity's `entity_kind`, an entity column path, value column paths, typed equality filters and the parameters); support decays by UTC day with a half-life, an entity with no learned support is `unlearned` (never scored 0), one with too many values in either window is found by a cardinality read of each window, left out of the per-value reads and reported once as `high_cardinality` (noisy while learning, not a signal) or `burst` (noisy only in the scoring window, a signal), `max_findings` caps a profile's rows and reports what it dropped, and a profile whose table holds less than `min_history_days` (default `learn_days`, read over a day-aligned lookback of twice the learning window) is `warming` and writes nothing, so a fresh lake gives each profile that much silence by design. Findings go to the draft lake table `novelty_finding` (1.0, keyed by `(source, uid)`), always `source_kind: heuristic`, stamped at the start of the behaviour window, labelled with the strictest contributing row, written idempotently per profile under batch ids that carry the writer's source mark (a write naming a source now replays only a batch committed under exactly that source and otherwise raises `BatchSourceMismatch`, while a write naming none is not judged; `write_batch` takes `expect_source`, so the dual-write queue's mark-less re-send of a parked batch is judged too and a squatted id stays parked as a `replay_failed` divergence (DualWriter forwards `expect_source` to both components and keeps it with a parked batch; a write naming two different sources is refused), and `write_findings` refuses rows, marks and ids of other sources); `corroborate` returns only the `(entity_kind, entity)` pairs that two distinct sources flagged. The analyzer reads as `analyzer:novelty` (a new reader kind in `tlp_readers`) through the new `CeilingReader.count_by` (a bounded grouped read by entity, values, UTC day and label, with column paths checked against the schema, the ceiling filter in the query, every group's label checked again, keys typed by their columns and an error, never a truncation, past its limit) and `CeilingReader.cardinality`, and writes through a table-scoped insert grant: an `analyzer:` reader's `writes: [<table>]` (declared analyzer-output tables only, below `red` only) renders `SELECT` and `INSERT` on those tables only, in the core's namespace, in `lake-access.json`, which also grants no `CALL` procedure to anyone (ADR-0036 amends ADR-0033 decision 6). Each lake adapter declares its SQL dialect for the struct field and day expressions. `hnx novelty run|corroborate`.
- ADR-0035 (proposed): the coordinator profile remembers the fleet as a ledger in its own `MemoryStore` scope, folded each cycle by code from outputs (projects change feed, `agent.job.finished`, reports, broker audit) with cursors in the same all-or-nothing update; only constrained fields, never report free text; served to the model in levels within a size budget filled by priority; retired entries kept in capped warm and cold tiers, removed lowest retention class first (`drop`, `count`, `keep`), routine work rolled up into counts; lessons from recurring patterns proposed through the records inbox and approved by a human; no cross-agent memory reads (M-09 unchanged) and no agent memory kept in model state. Nothing is built yet.
- ADR links: optional `Amends` / `Amended-by` / `Depends-on` header fields (ADR ids only); `tools/tests/test_adr_log.py` refuses a one-sided amendment pair, an `Amended:` line naming an ADR missing from `Amended-by`, an unknown or self id, and an accepted ADR that depends on one that is not accepted. ADR-0013, 0014, 0015, 0017, 0019 and 0033 carry their amendment pairs; the template and docs/adr/README.md describe the fields.
- Generated docs facts: `hnx docs facts generate|check` (`homelabnext/sop/facts.py`) fills `fact:NAME` spans of the `hnx:gen` family in authored pages from their sources: the core and extended capability names and counts (`homelabnext.conform.registry`), the tool classes with the reader and broker rules (`homelabnext/agent/tool_classes.yml`, also a generated table in docs/agent.md) and a table of every live schema's `$id` version (docs/interfaces.md section 5). The glossary, docs/interfaces.md, docs/agent.md, docs/mcp-catalog.md and docs/conformance.md now state those facts through spans; `make gates` and CI check them, so a new class, capability or schema bump cannot leave a page behind.
- `hnx site query SITE FROM TO PROTO [PORT]` answers "can FROM reach TO" from the policy before deployment: the first-match verdict and the deciding rule (`homelabnext.network.ir.evaluate`, both families; FROM and TO are addresses or hosts the site names). `tests/network/test_adapter_reach_parity.py` checks, for every pair of probe addresses, protocol and named port of the example sites (IPv4 and IPv6), that the verdict read back from the Linux router's rendered forward chain and from the controller's rendered rule sets equals the IR's, so an adapter rendering bug is caught where IR-level invariants cannot see it (docs/network-model.md).
- `provider_for(slot, instance)`, the consumer API of ADR-0027 decision 9 (`homelabnext.modules.providers`): the module serving a selected slot instance with its product, version and licence, `None` for `none` or `pass-through`, and a refusal for a slot or instance the selection does not name or an ambiguous lookup. `hnx modules providers --site FILE` lists every selected instance with its licence kind and SPDX id (ADR-0011: a provider records its product's licence), and `hnx modules swap-check` reports the outgoing and incoming providers' licences (`licence` in `--json`). `hnx modules check` now refuses a core registry row whose `impl:` is not the product or an alias of a provider the site selects for its capability, or whose capability the site selects `none` or `pass-through` for; with the repository's example registry it is a note. Comparing the baselines' `role` bindings and generating the registry from the selection are not done.
- Events carry a TLP label (ADR-0033 decisions 2 and 4; event envelope 1.2, additive): the optional extension attributes `tlp` (one of the five canonical TLP 2.0 spellings) and `derivedfrom` (the ids of the events or records the event was derived from). `build_event(..., label=, derived_from=)` writes them (a misspelt label raises), `homelabnext.events.envelope.event_label` reads them, an absent or unreadable label as `red`, and the publisher delivers them unchanged. Every event type's schema took its next minor to refer to envelope 1.2 (and `status/orchestration_export` 1.2 to `agent.job.finished` 1.2). No producer labels its events yet, so the bus stays a "not yet" egress point (`docs/modules.md`).
- `tools/licence_check.py` (`make licence-check`, CI's schemas job) and its reviewed register `tools/licences.yml`: every package `constraints/*.txt` pins and every image a `compose/` stack runs has an SPDX licence and a kind (`linked`, `tool` or `service`, a reason for the last two), seeded from the pinned versions' PyPI metadata; a new pin or image without an entry, an entry nothing uses, or a copyleft (GPL, AGPL, SSPL) `linked` package fails, so ADR-0011 decision 4 is checked against what is pinned and deployed and not only against a pull request's dependency diff.
- The registry's group-to-role table (platform service schema 2.7, additive): an `auth: idp` service's `roles` maps each of its roles to the IdP groups that hold it (lower case and `-`, never the groups separator); `homelabnext.generators.builtin.realm_grants` and `registry_grants` let a test prove the rendered realm grants exactly that table.
- `tools/experimental.yml`, the one reviewed list of experimental and frozen components (status, date, why, until, docs, tests, gates, invariants), and `tools/experimental.py` (`run`, `list`, `validate`), which `make experimental` and CI's non-blocking `experimental` job run. `tests/experimental_marker.py` marks every test under a listed path `experimental` from the list (never by hand), and `tools/tests/test_experimental.py` fails when a listed gate is back in `make check` or a required CI job, when the `experimental` job is missing, blocking or needed by another job, when the Makefile's `EXPERIMENTAL_INVARIANTS` is not the list's criteria, or when a listed page does not say so.
- Checklist rows for ADR-0033: S-08 (lake rows carry a TLP label, readers read at their ceiling) and O-12 (egress only at or below the receiver's ceiling), each with an invariants criterion; S-09 (rows stored before the label are backfilled before data is copied) as a SERVER row.
- SSH user certificates, signed by the secrets store after an OIDC login to the identity provider, as the way off the standing deploy SSH key (ADR-0021; docs/private-overlay.md, "SSH certificates (one login through the identity provider)"). Role `ssh_hardening` gains `ssh_hardening_trusted_user_ca_keys` (`TrustedUserCAKeys`; public key lines only, each file parsed by `ssh-keygen -l` before it replaces the live one), `ssh_hardening_authorized_principals` (per account, `AuthorizedPrincipalsFile`; a file for an account no longer listed is removed) and `ssh_hardening_revoked_keys` (`RevokedKeys`), and checks through `sshd -T` that the effective configuration reads those files (config and health). The lockout guard now counts only unrevoked authorized keys (an options prefix such as `from=` is read through), and accepts a CA and a principal as the kept account's only way in just with `ssh_hardening_keep_user_cert_only: true`, since a CA key that parses may still be the wrong one. `hnx ssh-ca check|render SPEC` turns `lab/ssh-ca.yml` (template in `examples/private-overlay/lab/`, owned in the skeleton's CODEOWNERS) into the store's writes: an ACL policy per role that may only sign with it, a user-certificate-only SSH role (principals, TTLs capped at 1 h for the deploy and 24 h for a person, extensions with no forwarding or user rc for the deploy, optional `source-address`), and an OIDC login role bound to the groups claim with a 10-minute token; `--format sh` pipes each write's JSON to the store's CLI. The docs give the person's `login` and `sign` commands, the deploy job's per-job key and certificate through its AppRole, and the order that makes `DEPLOY_SSH_KEY` retirable; the skeleton's `deploy.yml` still loads the key. `sssd_ldap` (`central-auth`) is marked superseded for SSH sign-on, not removed.
- A module's `requires`/`prefers` capability entry may name `hosts`, the module's own host roles that reach the slot (draft module schema; the loader refuses a role the module does not have); the egress policy then lets only those roles reach it, every role when absent. A slot reached by requirement still needs no egress grant, privileged or not: its gate is the review tier (human for a privileged slot), as the schema, the trust table and the egress section now all say (docs/modules.md, "Egress and publishing").
- Model roles, model requests, agent profiles and MCP servers carry TLP ceilings and labels (ADR-0033 decisions 10, 11 and 12; docs/modules.md, "Model-role ceilings"; docs/llm-gateway.md, "TLP labels on model requests"). `platform/model_roles` 1.4 adds a role's `tlp_ceiling` (`clear` or `green`) and a binding's `provider_terms` (`retention: none`, `training: none`, `ref`); `homelabnext.modules.rules.role_ceiling` is a role's ceiling: `red` for a role that stays in the lab, `clear` for one whose prompts can leave it (as `cloud_roles` reads it, fallback chain included), `green` only when every such role on the chain is raised and every `leaves_lab` binding on it records the terms, never `amber` or above. `rules.role_ceiling_problems` refuses `amber` and above, a ceiling on a local role, `green` without the terms and terms on a local binding, in the site rules and the gateway generator; the role table stays human-approve in both review tiers, and the swap gate reads both fields as a trust change. The ceiling is in addition to `grants.cloud_models`. `homelabnext.registry.model_tlp.admit(roles, role, claimed, sender=...)` is the gateway-side rule: for a role that leaves the lab a request is judged at the higher of its claimed label and the sender's ceiling (`send_ceiling`), an unlabelled one is `red`, and one above the role's ceiling is refused with a reason and a record the caller logs. The LiteLLM gateway has no admission code, so `hnx gen gateway render` gives every key a sending ceiling (a profile's or spec key's `tlp_ceiling`, else what its `data_tier` allows), refuses a key scoped to a role whose ceiling is below it (reading the table's `leaves_lab` as well as the spec's cloud backends), writes `metadata.tlp_ceiling` into `litellm/keys.json`, and `--check` audits it (missing or edited reads `red`). The `inference` suite gains feature `tlp-ceiling` with `gateway-refuses-role-above-key-ceiling` (17 checks) and the `ceiling-ignored` mutant; the gateway adapter takes `over_ceiling_key`, `over_ceiling_model` and `over_ceiling_served`. `agent/profile` 1.5 and `platform/agent_profile` 1.2 add `tlp_ceiling`, attested by the ceiling policy beside `data_tier` (and attesting the tier it derives without one); the compiler writes it, and a profile must state the attested one. `platform/mcp_server` 1.3 adds `tlp`, the label of what a server returns. Validation refuses a `data_tier` that disagrees with a TLP value (`local-only` is above `clear`, `cloud-allowed` is `clear`), and a profile is granted a server only when its `tlp` is at or below the profile's ceiling. Migration: a record or policy entry without a TLP value reads from its `data_tier` (a server: `clear` for `cloud-allowed`, `red` for `local-only`; a profile receives what its tier already allowed and sends at `clear` only when `cloud-allowed`), named in one note by `hnx mcp catalog check` and `CeilingPolicy.tlp_notes()`. New examples and negatives for the four schemas; the example ceiling policy and reader profile declare `red`, and the keyring, gateway configs and capability pages are regenerated.
- The status hub's views and exports declare TLP ceilings, and its public view serves only what a release transform keeps (ADR-0033 decisions 6 to 9; docs/status.md, "TLP ceilings and release transforms"). Views 1.10 (additive, frozen) adds `tlp` on every menu entry and `exports:` (`status.json`, `activity.csv`, `activity.jsonl`); `hnx status views check`, `render` and `serve` refuse an entry or export without a canonical ceiling, a public one that is not `clear`, a private one that is not `red`, and a public export or view that no release transform serves; where the hub is reachable from never raises a ceiling, and the front door's grant shows only the private group. The release transforms are declared in `homelabnext/status/release_transforms.yml` (`homelabnext.status.release`; governance, owned in CODEOWNERS with the code that applies them): each names what it serves, its input and output label, the fields it keeps and those it withdraws with the reason. Each kept path declares the kind of value it may hold (`slug`, `title`, `time`, `count`, `bool`, `const:`, `enum:` over closed sets), and every kept value is checked against it and against the egress content checks (no hostname-like dotted name, IP address, path, or TLP marker, read by `homelabnext.tlp_egress.markers`): a value that fails leaves as `withheld` (`null` for a count or a flag), never passed. The release is an allow-list whose output separate code re-checks against the keep list, so a transform passing an unlisted field fails and the route answers `500 withdrawn`; a kept leaf is a scalar only. The day-one migration keeps counts and health badges in `/status.json`, the static `status.json` and the page, and withdraws whatever names which lab system is in which state, plus `act_via`, menu `href`/`service`, live work, the whole attention list, a module's name, service, capability, sources, links and items, the whole source table, `observations`, and `host`, `project` and `model` from agent sessions, the Agents, Activity and analytics pages and `/activity.csv`, `/activity.jsonl`. Every page that renders inputs of its own, with no release transform yet, is withheld: strategy, requirements, objectives, rfis, projects, orchestration, baselines, the four maps and the four directory pages may sit only in the `private` group at `red` (the example moves them there); `serve` answers them only through the trusted front door, judged before any site works, access-logged, and the same `403` otherwise whether or not the site is configured; the public agent pages render from the released document; and `hnx status render` writes the maps and directory pages only with `--private-out DIR`, neither inside `--out` nor holding it. The publishing module gains `audience` (`public`, `community`) and `access_control`: a selection whose `tlp_allowed` is anything but `clear` is refused by the loader's site rules unless it sets `audience: community` and names its access control (`green` is not public); anything above `green` is refused outright, and a placeholder access control (`none`, `n/a`, `x`, ...) does not count.
- The egress check and the TLP backfill plan (ADR-0033 decisions 13 and 5; docs/modules.md, "Egress points"; homelabnext/lake/README.md). `homelabnext.tlp_egress` is the shared check each egress point is to apply (no egress point calls it yet): `egress_label` is the strictest of the claimed label, the recorded one (a required argument, `NO_RECORD` for none) and every TLP marker in the body, read after folding look-alike and hidden characters, bytes as UTF-8, mappings and lists leaf by leaf, an unscannable body `red`; any spelling of `amber+strict` but the canonical one, an unknown word or a dangling `+` is `red`. `egress_check` allows an item only at or below the destination's ceiling. `job_label` labels an enrichment lookup by the records that cause it (`red` with none), raised to the submitter's ceiling, and `analyzers_for` names only analyzers whose declared canonical maximum admits it. `homelabnext.lake.backfill.plan` and `hnx modules tlp-backfill` print the one-off backfill: add the column, label a row by its recorded source (red where the source's own item labels were never stored), every other row the strictest of the listed sources, only rows still unlabelled, then two checks (none left unlabelled; the non-canonical rows listed for a human); the table name is checked, a source value may hold no control character, `--map` and `--source-column` go together, and the output opens with a reminder that the routes must be every source feeding the table. The modules page enumerates the egress points with their ceilings and what grants each today.
- Lake readers read at their TLP ceiling (ADR-0033 decision 6; docs/modules.md, "Site selection"): the draft module selection gains `tlp_readers` (`{reader, ceiling, write?, why}`; a reader is `profile:`, `module:`, `user:` or `group:`) and `lake_catalog`, required with it.
