- The Code Mode golden suites (CM-6 of `docs/codemode.md`, ADR-0031 decision 9): `codemode-triage` and `codemode-reader` (`homelabnext/golden/examples/codemode/`) pose every task in classic MCP mode (`broker_actions`, `broker_call`, one broker action per turn) and in Code Mode (`codemode_search`, `codemode_describe`, `codemode_execute`: one program through the reference runner) to the same model against the same read-only broker world (`homelabnext.golden.agentic`), over a plain-text turn protocol, every tool result fenced as untrusted data by the new prompt template `agent-tool-result` (also a new G-02 placement: the injection eval is now 53 x 7 = 371 runs). Trace scorers join the golden registry (`TRACE_SCORERS`: `broker_calls`, `denied_calls`, `turns`, and `ungranted_actions`, applied to every tool-using task: an action outside the suite's grant or a tool the mode does not offer fails the task even when the broker refused it, the attempts derived from the execution result's `calls`; a program's request the relay refused before the broker is seen only through the fake's tap, and live needs the runner to list relay-refused requests). The G-02 pass places every corpus case in a fixture result the agent reads, in both modes, and fails on a follow (a canary, a call-shaped or quoted-literal forbidden tool in a reply, program code included, or that tool attempted). The `Backend` contract a live backend keeps is explicit (`seed(fixtures)` for the fixtures and the G-02 payload, `BrokerError` refusals with `Denied` and `InvalidArguments` counted denied, `describe` raising `ValueError`, `execute` returning the result whose `calls` the harness reads); pending approvals and per-run setup and teardown are documented live-only gaps. The text the harness shows the model (each mode's tools, the system message, the protocol replies) is a pinned input of the report, and a tool result is one line of JSON (NEL, LS and PS escaped). CI runs them with a scripted, recorded fake model (`homelabnext.golden.fakes.ScriptedAgentModel`, per task and mode its canned turns) behind the fake inference server and a fake broker world (the fake broker, the reference runner, the forking test executor; the world serves only the scripted model, runs per task and agent only the programs that model can write there, and reads suites and scripts, trusted CI-run code, from the examples directory only); a deliberately naive control, which follows every injection it sees, must be caught in both modes. `hnx golden codemode [--check REPORT | --out REPORT] [--fake fence|naive]` and the committed report `homelabnext/golden/examples/codemode/report.json` (accuracy, turns, broker calls, denied calls, ungranted attempts and injection follows per suite and mode, and ADR-0031's comparison), checked in `make gates` and CI; `make codemode-golden-report` regenerates it. `schemas/agent/golden_task` 1.1 (additive, frozen beside 1.0): `modes`, `grant`, `max_turns`, and a task's `fixtures`, `inject` and `checks`. The reader's Code Mode half is hypothetical: `codemode.execute` is granted to `profile:reader` by the fake world only (`homelabnext.golden.fakes.CODEMODE_SUITES`, read-tier actions only; G-03 keeps a reader off it live), marked in the report, outside its totals and with no adoption decision (`codemode_adoptable: null`). Each injected run records whether the payload `reached` the model, and the naive control must follow every reached run in every suite and mode; a program that reduces its results away is a property of that recorded program, not a Code Mode defence. `denied_calls` (max 0) applies to every task unless it sets another max, a per-mode `max` names both modes (schema and loader), and `codemode-triage` gains `queue-recovery`, a task Code Mode loses (a failed program rewritten), so the comparison counts the tasks Code Mode wins, ties and loses. This proves the harness, the scorers and the report, not a model; CM-9 swaps the completer and the backend.
- The Code Mode runner and the `codemode-runner` capability, the eighteenth (CM-5b of `docs/codemode.md`, ADR-0031 decisions 2, 3, 6, 7 and 10). `homelabnext.codemode.runner.Runner` is `codemode.execute`'s handler: it checks the execution request, asks the broker for the run's token as the bound runner (`issue_token`, the actor's granted actions, never `codemode.*`), serves the run's endpoint on the CM-4 wire and hands the program, the endpoint and the token to a `CodeExecutor`, and returns a `schemas/codemode/execution_result` held to its schema (status `ok`, `paused`, `denied`, `error` or `limit`; calls in step order with the broker's outcome, reason and request ids; `pending`; single-line `logs`; the value and `truncated`), spending the token `paused` or `finished`. Every program call is a broker request with the token, so `call` is `token_call`, `submit` is `token_submit`, the paused step on a resume `token_run`; over the socket the runner speaks as its `codemode.runners` peer and its relay as a `codemode.sandboxes` peer with the bearer line (`SocketRunnerBroker`, `SocketSandboxBroker`), and a call presenting another token is refused at the relay. An exec envelope is unwrapped to `output` (JSON when it is JSON, else text); a truncated output (`output-truncated`), a non-zero exit (`handler-failed`, the broker's), an output that does not match the action's result schema from the search index (`result-schema-mismatch`) and a result over the journal's cap (`result-too-large`) reach the program as `broker.Error`, while the record keeps the broker's outcome. A broker refusal is a denied call the program catches as `broker.Denied` with the broker's reason, with the request ids when the broker built the request and without them (never journalled) for a token refusal; a transport failure is `broker-unreachable`. The step journal (`homelabnext/codemode/journal.py`, `schemas/codemode/journal_step`) is local, append-only and durable: one directory per execution keyed per actor, one exclusively created `0600` file per record, a keyed fingerprint of each call (the request digest being the broker's), at most 200 steps, swept 7 days after an execution finished and 30 after it paused. A submit, or a call needing approval, pauses the run with `pending` naming it; a resume reads `token_status`'s `paused_by_state` (never polling `token_run`, which spends the cap) and, while the request waits, ends `paused` again unissued and having sent nothing; once it may run, journalled steps are answered from the journal (`replayed`) and never re-sent, the paused request runs by its id, only the later calls are new, and a call that differs from its journalled step is refused `replay-mismatch` and ends the run `error`. A call the broker refuses `call-cap-reached` ends the run `limit` (`calls`). The program's view gains `broker.submit(action, args)` and a `target=` keyword (the CM-4 wire's optional `op` and `target`; the forking test executor sends them). Contract `conformance/protocols/codemode_runner.py`, subjects in `conformance/fakes/codemode_runner.py` (the reference runner over the fake broker, or over the reference service on `broker-reference`, with the forking test executor), suite `conformance/suites/codemode_runner.py` with six MUST checks (`result-matches-the-schema`, `denied-call-is-a-value`, `every-call-is-journalled`, `submit-pauses-the-run`, `resume-without-replay`, `call-cap-ends-the-run`) and six mutants, each caught by its own check only; capability pages regenerated; counts in docs and tests. `codemode_search` and `codemode_describe` (`hnx codemode mcp --index DIR --socket PATH`, `homelabnext/codemode/mcp_tools.py`) read the generator's index for the profile the broker socket maps the process to, narrowed to the broker's current grant; catalog record `hnx-codemode` (hnx entry `codemode-mcp`, path flag `--index`); execute stays `broker_call` of `codemode.execute` on `hnx-broker`. New tool class `codemode` (reader-forbidden, not broker-only: its tools only read, and running code is the broker action, already `exec`), `platform/mcp_server` 1.2 (additive: the class `codemode`; baseline frozen; example and negative); `cap:codemode-runner` classed `exec` and never a module's `shared_config` target; keyring rebuilt; client renders regenerated. Hardened after review: a request takes a step, and reaches the broker, only once the relay has accepted it (the run's token in constant time, an action and a `svc:` target matched whole, at most 200 steps), so a stray or co-tenant line cannot shift a resume's steps and re-send a settled call; the relay's own refusals are bounded (32, then the run ends `error`), it serves connections concurrently (8 at once, a 5 s read timeout or the run's wall budget), can check the peer's uid (`relay_uids`), and stops accepting before the result is built. The journal writes an intent before every call and its answer after, refusals without request ids included, so a resume answers every step the same way; a step of unknown outcome (an intent with no answer) is never sent again and ends the resume `error` (`step-outcome-unknown`), the paused step's being checked against `paused_by_state` instead; an answer that cannot be written stops the run, which ends `error` with its token spent `finished` and never resumes; an intent that cannot be written is never sent; a journal failure anywhere in `handle` is an `error` result; a crashed run is marked `interrupted` and never resumes; a resume is claimed by an atomic check-and-set (`Journal.update(expect=)`), so of two concurrent resumes one runs; records are validated against the schema and `meta.json` when read; directories are tightened to `0700`; results are bounded per execution (8 MiB) as well as per result. An exec envelope is unwrapped only for an action the index says is `exec`; an ok call whose result reached the program as `broker.Error` carries `delivered_as` (`schemas/codemode/execution_result`, draft: example and negative). The runner suite gains `resume-refuses-a-different-call` and `denied-pause-is-a-value` (eight MUST checks, mutants `replay-by-step-only` and `denied-pause-waits`), and the executor suite `submit-and-target-relayed` (mutant `bare-wire`). The schemas' `x-draft` notes, the package doc and ADR-0031 (the read tools on their own `hnx-codemode` server) are brought up to date. Round 3: any failure in the runner after a call was sent (a result with `NaN`, a bug) stops the run, reports the call and ends `error`; a result that is not JSON is `result-not-json`; lines without the run's token never count against the run (answered `token-invalid`, past 64 dropped), only the program's own malformed calls do, and nothing after a pause changes a paused run's outcome; each connection has a deadline for its whole request, a newcomer cuts off the oldest connection still reading when every slot is taken, the uid check runs before a slot is taken and reads only an ESTABLISHED row for the accepted connection's own address; a resume's claim binds to the version of `meta.json` it read, so a resume overtaken by another backs off untouched; a pending call of a stopped run keeps its outcome and says why (`delivered_as`, now an enum, `pending` allowed with it in a run that is not paused); a refused `token_run` without request ids is journalled; intents, refusals and `meta.json` are validated when read, existing per-execution directories tightened, and a crashed run's owner is told by its runner instance's lock in the state directory, in any pid namespace. Round 4: a settled refusal of a pending step is what every later resume replays (it used to fall back to the pending record and fail the result's schema); evicting a connection and starting to serve it are one check-and-set, so a call the program was told failed never ran; a fault of the runner after a send is `runner-fault`; `delivered_as` is paired with its outcome in the schema (example and negative); a refusal's reason is checked on load; the sweep prunes the lock files of runner instances gone; no test binds a wildcard address.
- Code Mode capability tokens, held and enforced by the broker (CM-5a of `docs/codemode.md`, ADR-0031 decisions 2, 6 and 7; `docs/broker.md` "Code Mode capability tokens"). `codemode.execute`'s params are checked as an execution request, and while its handler (the runner) runs the request is in flight, bound to its runner: only then, and only for that runner (manifest 1.7 `codemode.runners`, or in process `register_runner`), does `issue_token` mint one token for that run, its claims (`schemas/codemode/token`) taken from the request (id, authenticated actor, execution id, code digest), its actions each admitted for the actor by `decide()` (narrowing, never widening; never `codemode.*`), its lifetime within the manifest's `codemode` caps and the request's budget, at most a few live per actor. The call cap is per execution: a resume's token carries what is left of it. Execution ids are scoped per actor. The secret (256 random bits) is returned once; the broker keeps its sha256 with the claims, the runner, the state and the call count in `<state>/codemode/` (`homelabnext/broker/tokens.py`, a re-entrant lock over one `flock`). Every `token_call`/`token_submit`/`token_run` runs as the token's actor after, in order, `token-invalid` (one answer for an unknown and a wrong secret), `token-sandbox-mismatch` (both ways: a dedicated sandbox's token only from it, a pool only tokens bound to no dedicated sandbox), `token-expired`, `token-spent`, `token-paused`, `token-action-not-allowed` and `call-cap-reached` (`TOKEN_REFUSALS`); each call past those checks counts before `decide()`, so refused ones count too. Answers, and refusals raised once a request exists, name it (`request_id`, `request_digest`). A call that needs approval, and every submit, stores its request and pauses the token in one locked step (audit `token-pause`); `spend_token(..., outcome)` ends a token `finished` or `paused`, and the end of the execute spends a live one; only a paused execution resumes, and `token_run` runs the request it paused on once approved; `token_status` reports that request's state. A start-up sweep ends a run whose broker is gone as `crashed`, drops expired live entries and removes ended executions after `codemode.retention_days` (default 7). Socket peers `runner` (`token-issue`, `token-spend`, `token-status`; exempt from the refusal limit) and `sandbox` (`token-call`, `token-submit`, `token-run`, the token sent as a bearer line ahead of the JSON body and never in it; only pre-admission refusals count, keyed per presented token, with a ten-times-higher per-uid ceiling for unknown tokens whose audit records are coalesced); `BrokerClient` methods for each. With several manifest runners, `codemode.execute`'s action names its `runner`. A paused request an approver denied is refused `request-denied`, naming the request. The sandbox socket framing is specified (`docs/broker.md`, "Sandbox socket framing"), and a `token-issue` answer carries the secret beside the result, never in it. Sandbox transport refusals with no token read are coalesced per peer like unknown secrets, and a coalescing window that ends with refusals held back is flushed (a timer, the token sweep) with its count, judged on the monotonic clock. `token_run` counts against the cap past its binding check, refused or not, so polling is bounded; `paused_by_state` reports `expired` for an approval that expired unused (the request waits for a fresh one). New test hook `approval-control` (`decide_request`) and check `token-run-names-a-denied-request` with its mutant. Schemas: broker manifest 1.7 (`codemode`), audit 1.3 (`token-issue`, `token-pause`, `token-spend`, `token_id`, `execution_id`), both frozen; `schemas/codemode` drafts: the request-less reasons are `TOKEN_REFUSALS` plus `broker-unreachable` and `rate-limited`, and the cap is per execution. Contract: the broker's `CodemodeTokens` (feature `codemode-tokens`) with the reference fake, and its checks as the extended suite `broker-tokens` (the broker suite would pass the 24-check ceiling): thirteen MUST checks, each of its fourteen mutants caught by its own check alone (now asserted for `broker-tokens` and `codemode-executor` in `tests/conform/test_conform_mutants.py`); the reference service passes it (`conformance/targets/broker-reference.yml`).
- Context packs can carry levels (ADR-0032, `docs/agent.md` "Linked knowledge"): an `include:` rule takes an optional `detail: full | summary | outline` (`agent/profile` 1.4, additive; baseline frozen, with an example and a negative). `full` is the default and changes nothing: it is never written to the normalised rule, so existing profile digests and packs are byte-identical. `summary` puts a node's id, kind, title, path and level-0 summary in the pack instead of its body (a node with no summary is its title line and `(no summary)`, recorded as `summary_source: none`, never the full text); `outline` adds the heading outline with slugs, paths and sizes for the MCP `section` tool. The pack's knowledge record keeps the digest of the full content and adds `detail`, `summary_source` and `content_chars` to a non-full node; `max_chars` caps what is emitted. The generated capability and system pages (`hnx docs capabilities generate`, `hnx docs systems generate`) now carry a derived front-matter `summary:` (a machine-owned key, outside the review digest), marked `summary_generated: true` so the MCP tools, the pack and `hnx registers summaries` report it as `generated`, not `authored`. A pack at `outline` prints `outline_digest:` (the bare value MCP `section` takes), reads files as the context store does, stops an oversized outline at `max_chars`, and takes `max_chars` only from the rules at the winning detail. For a file the context store serves differently (undecodable bytes, empty, over its size cap) a `summary`/`outline` node's `digest` is that of the served text and the record adds `file_digest` of the file's own bytes; a node a `full` rule cannot read is excluded whatever other rules say.
- Authored Markdown served in levels (ADR-0032, proposed; `docs/mcp.md` "Documents in levels"): homelab-next does not adopt OpenViking or another context database; agent context stays authored Markdown/YAML in git and `hnx mcp serve` serves it progressively. Two new read-only tools, scoped exactly like `context_for` (mandatory `allowed`, narrowed by `--scope-from-profile`; a document outside the scope is refused like a missing one, and a legacy record code resolves without a warning that would signal existence): `outline(id_or_path, allowed)` (title, summary with `summary_source` `authored`, `first-paragraph`, `synthesized` or `none`, chars, digest, and every ATX heading with level, visible-text title, slug, heading path and section size; `parse_truncated`/`parsed_chars` past the bounds) and `section(id_or_path, heading, allowed, outline_digest=None, offset=0, limit=None)` (exact match on slug or full heading path only; an unknown or ambiguous heading is a tool error listing the slugs; a changed document refused when `outline_digest` is given; paged by `offset`/`limit` with `truncated` and `next_offset`, `chars` and `digest` of the whole section). The parser (`homelabnext/context/levels.py`) is linear and bounded (1 MiB, 100,000 lines, 2,048 characters of a title line, 64 KiB of front-matter; memoised per digest), splits lines on `\n` only, skips a BOM, front-matter (any parse failure is no front-matter), fenced code, HTML comments and raw pre/script/style/details blocks, does not treat setext text as headings, drops a sole leading H1 from paths, qualifies a repeated slug by its parent (`unreleased/added`) before counting (`added~2`), and cleans every title, slug, path and derived summary of control and invisible format characters (Unicode Cc, Cf, Zl, Zp, Cs). The first-paragraph fallback skips quotes, lists, tables, HTML and images, prefers prose before the first H2, then an ADR's Context, reduces links and emphasis to text and cuts at a sentence end, else a word with `…`. The `hnx-catalog` record lists both tools. A front-matter `summary:` (one line, at most 160 characters, no control or invisible format character, part of the review digest) is accepted on lessons (`registers/lesson` 2.1), runbooks (`registers/runbook` 2.2) and docs pages; the record validation refuses a malformed one, and `hnx registers summaries PATH... [--missing]` (in `make registers` and CI) fails on one and counts records and pages without a summary as a warning. The example lessons and runbooks carry summaries, with fresh fictional reviews of the new digests.
- Handler documents may declare a `result_schema` (`schemas/broker/handler` 1.2, additive; baseline frozen): a JSON Schema object describing the handler's `output` once decoded as JSON. The broker validates it when loading the document (a malformed one is refused, naming the file) but does not enforce it at call time; runtime checking belongs to the CM-5 runner. `hnx codemode gen` renders the method's return type from it with the params type mapping (an interface/`TypedDict` or an inline type), puts it in `search.json`'s `result`, and applies the same string checks as for params schemas; absent, the result stays `unknown`/`Any`. The example `service.status` and `service.restart` handlers declare one and the goldens are regenerated.
- The `codemode-executor` capability, the seventeenth (ADR-0031 decision 10, `docs/codemode.md` CM-4): the contract for the sandbox that runs one Code Mode program (`CodeExecutor.run(program, *, language, broker, limits)` returning status `ok`, `denied`, `error` or `limit`, the cap or error kind it names, a value cut to a `truncated` prefix past `result_bytes`, and the printed output), a reference fake that forks each program from a warm Python process that never runs one itself (the warm-pool shape of ADR-0031 decision 5), with an environment built from nothing, a scratch directory per run and an audit hook (test-only, explicitly not an isolation boundary), and a suite of nine MUST checks and one SHOULD run as real programs against a stand-in broker on loopback: a decoy listener that must see no connection, a canary in the harness's environment and files that must not reach a result, CPU, memory, wall-clock and output caps, a marker a second run must not find, and a value that must come back truncated. Five mutants, one per ADR check (`open-egress`, `env-leak`, `no-timeout`, `reused-state`, `unbounded-result`), each caught by its check. Wired as a core capability: registry, `capabilities.yml`, compute model, tool classes (`exec`, so broker-only), keyring rebuilt, capability pages regenerated, counts in docs and tests. Aligned with the draft `schemas/codemode` (PR #64): statuses add `paused` (a pending broker reply raises `broker.Paused`, a `BaseException`, and the run ends paused with the call pending however the program catches it); errors are `{kind, message}` with kinds syntax, runtime, sandbox, token and broker and a single-line message of at most 2000 characters (a raised `MemoryError` and a non-zero `sys.exit` are runtime errors); denials carry the broker's reason and error replies raise a catchable `broker.Error`; `Limits.max_calls` is enforced by the broker client (`call-cap-reached`, the run goes on); memory is resident memory; the value's JSON serialization is pinned; programs over 65536 characters are refused (`ProgramTooLarge`); the executor declares `broker_bind`, where the harness serves the broker. Each check holds its programs per language, and a declared language without one is untested. New checks `denial-reason-reaches-the-program`, `pending-pauses-the-run`, `broker-error-is-an-error`, `call-cap-enforced` and `oversized-program-refused`; new mutants `no-call-cap` and `pause-catchable`. Fake fixes: output cut on a character boundary within its bytes, the program passed as a file (a megabyte program raised), a stopped run's process killed directly as well as by group. `cap:codemode-executor` is never a module's `shared_config` target. A program that is not UTF-8 text (a lone surrogate) is refused (`UnencodableProgram`, new check `unencodable-program-refused`); the result header from the sandbox is validated (an unknown status or a malformed error or pending entry is a `sandbox` error, never an exception); error messages and pending strings also lose zero-width, bidirectional-mark, byte-order-mark and surrogate characters; printed output cut by the cap drops a split character instead of returning U+FFFD; the client-side pause and call cap are documented as behaviour, the broker's refusal on the token (CM-5) being the enforcement.
- Code Mode schemas, step CM-2 of `docs/codemode.md` (ADR-0031, proposed): four draft schemas under `schemas/codemode/` (`x-draft`, not frozen): `execution_request` (the params of `codemode.execute`: language, program, a capped budget and `resume` of the same execution id; no actor, which is the enclosing broker request's), `execution_result` (status `ok`/`paused`/`denied`/`error`/`limit` held consistent with the calls, every broker call with its request id and digest and a reason from the broker's vocabulary or the sandbox client's own, pending calls by step and digest with no free text, logs as single lines), `token` (claims bound to each run's request, the code digest and the sandbox; issuer and audience `svc:broker`; never `codemode.*` actions) and `journal_step` (bound to the actor and code digest; ok steps carry their result, pending steps the request a resume runs once approved), with examples and negatives each refused for its stated reason.
- Code Mode generator (CM-3, ADR-0031 decisions 3 and 4; `docs/codemode.md`): `hnx codemode gen [--check]` writes, for every profile the broker manifest grants actions to, synchronous typed namespaces for a program (`types.d.ts` and `stubs.pyi` from one model, one namespace per action prefix, each method's parameters typed from the action's `params_schema` or lane: required versus optional, enums as literals, open objects' value types, a lane write's result as `LaneWrite`; a call that needs approval pauses the run) and the index `codemode_search` and `codemode_describe` will read (`search.json`: name, call, summary, tier, `needs_approval`, kind, the exact params schema, the result, a signature per language). Which actions a profile sees is asked of `homelabnext.broker.decide` itself (a test holds it equal to `BrokerService.actions_for`), so an ungranted action, one above the grant's tier, one without a handler and any `codemode.*` action never appear. The goldens for the example manifest live in `examples/codemode/<profile>/`; `--check` fails on a hand edit, on a manifest or handler change and on a profile no longer granted, and runs in `make gates` and CI (an ADR-0028 decision 6 row). Refused (exit 2): a name with a trailing newline or control character, a parameter that is not an identifier or is `self` (or `params` beside `**params`), a `required` name the spec does not declare, a description or pattern with a control, bidi or zero-width character, `*/` or `"""`, and a symlinked profile directory or file under `--out`; writes go through a temporary file and `os.replace`, and the stale sweep deletes only marked `<slug>/<file>` it owns. Every string anywhere in an exec action's params schema (a nested title, a default, an enum value, a key) is held to the same characters, since the index carries the whole schema.
- Code Mode, proposed and not built (ADR-0031, `docs/codemode.md`): agents may compose broker actions as short programs in a sandbox with no credentials and no egress but the broker; every call stays an ordinary broker request, `codemode.execute` is itself a broker action, the namespaces are generated per profile from the MCP catalog and broker manifest, and approvals come from humans or reviewed standing rules, never a model. The build plan orders phase 1 as nine steps (schemas, generator, executor and runner contracts with mutants, golden suites against MCP mode, then a sandbox spike, the real executor and one role live on the lab) and records the sandbox overhead measured for the decision.
- Holds are released by liveness (ADR-0029, proposed; `docs/orchestration-controller.md`): the `orchestration` contract now says, additively, that a slot, lease or token/KV reservation exists only while its worker is alive and is released on success, failure, cancel, deadline, worker death and restart (a TTL is only a backstop); `cancel` of a running run stops its worker within `cancel_grace_s`; a restart leaves no run `running` without a worker while queued runs and idempotency keys survive; an optional `capacity() -> (limit, in_flight, holders)` (feature `admission`), a per-run `deadline_s` (feature `deadline`) and `kill`/`restart` test hooks. Five MUST checks (`cancel-running-run-frees-capacity`, `killed-worker-fails-run-and-frees-capacity`, `deadline-expiry-frees-capacity`, `restart-reconciles-runs-and-capacity`, `capacity-returns-to-zero-after-churn`; the suite goes from 11 to 16) and six mutants (`leak-on-cancel`, `leak-on-kill`, `no-restart-reconcile`, `ttl-release`, `lying-capacity`, `restart-reruns-live-run`). The checks also require that a live run keeps its hold for two graces, that a stopped worker's late result never lands, and, where a limit is reported, that freed capacity admits new runs; `cancel_grace_s` is capped at `MAX_CANCEL_GRACE_S` (10 s); a restart must end a run that was executing, never re-run it. The in-memory fake runs each run on its own worker in a bounded number of slots; the controller stand-in models the in-flight route (`fixes: [backend-inflight]`), the process kill on cancel and restart reconciliation; the controller adapter maps `capacity()` to `GET /admin/backend-inflight` and declares the fault hook only on the stand-in.
- A run between attempts is live (`orchestration` clause a): after a failed attempt a retrying run stays `running` and keeps its holds, and liveness is the orchestrator's record of the run's worker, never whether an attempt's process exists at that moment. New MUST check `retrying-run-keeps-its-hold` (`admission`) and mutant `attempt-scoped-hold`. A watcher that failed every running job with no live attempt process failed live runs waiting between attempts, which then finished and read as failures with a blank reason.
- Go tooling, ahead of the orchestrator module (`orchestrator/`) itself: `tools/go_modules.py` discovers the Go modules from the tracked `go.mod` files (`<module>/tools/go.mod` is that module's pinned linters, by an explicit rule) and fails on Go source or a go.sum in no module, Go source in a linters' module (which no job runs), a symlinked go.mod, a linters' directory without its go.mod and any `go.work`; CI's `go` job runs once per discovered module (a matrix, so a second or nested module is checked without being listed; skipped as a no-op while there is none, with `go-ok` as the one stable check): `actions/setup-go` pinned by commit SHA from the module's go.mod, `go mod verify`, gofmt, `go vet`, `go build`, staticcheck and govulncheck from `tools/go.mod`, `GOFLAGS=-mod=readonly`, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` and `GOWORK=off`, the root-only tests under sudo with each test binary's exit status recorded, and `go test -race -shuffle=on -json` as the runner user under `setpriv --no-new-privs` in a loopback-only network namespace, vet, build and the linters under it too and after the verdict (streams, exit statuses and the privileged binaries in a fresh `mktemp` directory in root-owned `/run`, judged by `/usr/bin/sudo /usr/bin/python3 -I` on a copy of the policy and of PyYAML, checked against its RECORD hashes, taken before any module code runs, the job's shell `bash` for pipefail: a guard against accidents and casual tampering, not hostile pull-request code, for which branch protection and review are the boundary), judged by `tools/go_test_policy.py` (the Go side of `tests/suite_policy.py`: failing tests, a failure sticky across `-count` runs, a parent's own failure next to quarantined subtests, packages that did not build or failed beyond their quarantined tests (a hang, a race after the tests, a `TestMain` crash), tests and packages with no verdict in a cut-off stream, go's own exit status (`--go-exit`), every package `go list ./...` names (`--expect-packages`), a test event from every package whose test files declare a Test, Fuzz or output-checked Example (`--expect-tests`), the root run's failed packages and non-zero binary exits (`--privileged-exit`), skips whose reason (the last line before `--- SKIP`) matches no allowed pattern, `SkipNow`, `Skipf` and any `Skip` that is not one non-blank one-line literal, in every `.go` file of the module (`testdata/` and `_*` included) and through any selector, by a Go tokenizer (`--check-sources`), an empty skip message read as the reason it is, malformed streams; the module's `skips.yml` entries name exactly one package, resolved against go.mod's module path, quarantines with the 90-day maximum and privileged entries accepted only when the root run passes them; what the stream cannot show is documented in CONTRIBUTING.md). `tools/fix_has_test.py --prove` proves Go regression tests (the added or changed test functions, by go test's naming rule with `TestMain` a helper, must fail without the fix; `testdata/` is a test path, staged on the parent with the tests, and the package holding the outermost `testdata/` runs whole; no toolchain or a setup error is "cannot prove", never a pass, and `go: downloading` is not one), with Go (the workflow's pinned `GO_VERSION`) installed in the `regression-tests` job once a module exists; the 3.12 `test` leg installs Go too and sets `HNX_REQUIRE_GO=1`, so the Go tool tests fail there instead of skipping; `tools/check_spdx.py` covers `.go` files, generated ones (`// Code generated ... DO NOT EDIT.`) exempt; `tools/leak_scan.py` reads go.sum `h1:` digests (in go.sum files), Go test function names and numeric conversions as what they are, judges each escape-separated segment of a Go string literal and the value with its escapes taken out (other files keep a value whole), and takes Go's `:=` as a keyed-value separator, an unquoted Go expression after it or `=` being code (placed with the whole file tokenized, so a raw string's body is text) while every string literal after it on the line that is not text (words, a format string, a time layout) is judged, with one rule for `:`, `=` and `:=`, each with a negative test; `component_tests` requires a `*_test.go` in every Go package of every discovered module, its root included, and reports Go in a linters' module; the workstation-reference gate scans `orchestrator/examples/`; Go modules stay out of the sdist and wheel, a test requires a Dependabot `gomod` entry for every discovered module, and the root `.gitignore` ignores Go's test binaries (not directories named `*.test`), profiles and coverage in any module without naming a component's binaries.
- Orchestration telemetry (ADR-0025, `docs/orchestration-telemetry.md`, checklist O-11): a new event `homelabnext.agent.job.finished` 1.0, the usage record the LLM orchestrator's controller emits once per job reaching a terminal state (outcome, profile, backend, lane, wall and queue time, token counts with unknown kept apart from zero, cost, retries, the gateway tag; never the task text or the answer). `hnx orchestration consume|ingest` stores it in a SQLite telemetry store, only from the configured controller service id (`--source`, default `svc:llm-orchestrator`), idempotent by event id and run id with one gateway tag per run (a record with an impossible or out-of-order timestamp, a time before 2000 (a zero time) or more than a day ahead or a queue time older than retention before the finish, a non-finite number (which every producer here now refuses to validate or serialize, and every decode path refuses, a mapping too), a lone surrogate, an envelope extension attribute or a `subject` other than the run id (so the stored and exported envelope carries no free text either), or a taken tag is dead-lettered, never retried, while a store or environment fault, such as a missing schema library, is redelivered, and a store that cannot take a write fails the pass before anything is fetched, so an outage never spends a record's attempts; one older than retention is counted `expired`; a store an earlier build left with two jobs on one tag is refused with a fix that keeps the job received first inside retention); `hnx orchestration turns` joins the gateway's per-call records by the gateway tag through a turn-source adapter chosen by `--turn-source` (`none` is valid; a new `turn-records` feature of the `inference` capability, `TurnSource` with four conformance checks; LiteLLM's spend log, read with a viewer key by secret name over verified https, is the first adapter) in windows of at most an hour from each job's start (never its queue time, never before the retention cutoff, so a job held or running for days is stored and read only where its turns can be), settling each job as soon as its windows are read, and the store refuses any turn outside the neutral turn record (`model_role`, never a product's own field names); `hnx orchestration report` rolls up per profile, backend and lane (`homelabnext.orchestration.rollup/1`; every sum carries its own unknown count and is null when nothing is known). `hnx status serve --orchestration DB` opens the store read-only (it must exist; the writers keep a rollback journal, not WAL, so the hub can read it as another user without write access and creates no file) and adds the Orchestration page with filters and a job drill-down to its turns (views schema 1.9; a request the store cannot answer is a 500 page, never a dropped connection); jobs that did not go through the gateway show "no gateway data", never zeros. `hnx orchestration export|import` moves a store as JSON lines (`homelabnext.orchestration.export/1`: a header naming the store's sources and retention, then each job's envelope and each turn record), idempotent by the same ids and refusing what the consumer refuses, including a parser bomb or a huge integer as one refused line; `--out` is written whole and never over the store or through a symlink, an export holding records an import would refuse says how many and exits 1, and `--retention-days` is a finite 1 to 3650. `--turn-source` names code only under `homelabnext.conform.adapters` or a site package declared with `--turn-source-namespace`, and takes other adapter options as `--turn-source-option` (credentials only as `secret:` names).
- The site's network scope in the private overlay (ADR-0027 open question 13, `docs/modules.md` "The network scope"): the draft `platform/module_selection` 1.0 gains a `scope:` block (`trusted.wan`, `trusted.nets`, `trusted.tooling`, `target`, `exclude.unattended_mutation`, `exclude.offensive`), each part a list of IPv4/IPv6 addresses or CIDR networks, non-empty except an exclusion (`[]` means nothing excluded; absent is never read as empty); `trusted.wan` may also name a dynamic source (`{source: <name>}`), specified and refused by the loader until a reader exists. One pattern (`homelabnext.modules.scope.ENTRY_PATTERN`) decides an entry's text in both the schema, whose `scope` object is generated from `SCOPE_PARTS`, and the loader (no zone id, no IPv4 inside or embedded in IPv6, no leading zeros, no IPv4 host bits); the loader also refuses `/0`, special-use blocks, a trusted network broader than /8 or /32, IPv6 host bits and a network listed twice. `trusted.wan` also refuses private, shared and unique-local space. A module declares the parts it reads with `requires.scope` in the draft `platform/module` 1.0 (the external data collector requires `trusted.wan` and both exclusions); the loader refuses a selection that enables it with a declared part missing, empty or malformed, and a module that requires any address part (`target` or a `trusted.*` part) without `exclude.offensive`, or, unless every contribution kind it uses is inert (docs, detections, lake tables, status sources, notify pairs), without `exclude.unattended_mutation`. `scope_networks(site, module, part)` refuses an undeclared part and fails closed (`ScopeMissing`, `ScopeUndeclared`); the API is cooperative, so code review, not the reader, keeps a module from reading `site["scope"]` directly. `effective_targets(site, module)` is target minus the exclusions and raises `ScopeMissing` when nothing is left. A module acting on hostnames is not covered (specified, not implemented). A selection's string-valued config value now refuses a site address (`address_literals`: IPv6, hex, zero-padded, an IPv4 address or `inet_aton` short form with a port, prefix length or scheme, and a plain private or shared dotted quad or integer; bind, loopback, documentation and other special addresses, labels, plain global dotted quads and versions are kept; integer, duration and boolean keys are not scanned; an external endpoint is a hostname or `svc:` reference): **before upgrading, an overlay's `lab/modules.yml` must move any address-valued config value to the scope block or a hostname, and a selection that enables the external data collector must carry `scope.exclude.offensive` and `scope.exclude.unattended_mutation` (each may be `[]`), or the loader refuses it**. `tools/private_leak_check.py public` places the rule by location: a site scope block may sit only in the example site profiles and the generated selection examples and negatives, which are parsed strictly after a size and depth bound (`scope-address`, `scope-shape`, `scope-unparsed`); in every file the scanner reads, any suffix, with CRLF and CR line ends normalised, two rules run as a union and an address-like token outside the documentation ranges fails (`scope-address`): a parse pass judges every scalar under any key that resolves to `scope` in a file that parses as JSON, TOML or YAML, and a raw-text rule tokenises what every `scope` key holds, including in files that do not parse (Markdown prose and fences). An `inet_aton` short form under a scope key is refused only when it cannot be a version or a decimal (a hex part, a part over 255) or carries a prefix length, port or scheme (a CIDR short form); `1.0`, `24.04` and `0.05` are ignored. Markdown key spellings (`` `scope`: ``, `**scope**:`, table rows) and Jupyter notebook cells and outputs are read; a call's `scope=` keyword argument, an HTML `scope=` attribute and an attribute access or call (`scope.get(`) are not keys, while an assignment to a Python subscript (`site["scope"]["target"]`) is; an indented `scope = x` holds only the lines deeper than itself. Loopback, unspecified and link-local addresses name no site and are let through, and an IPv6 form that embeds an IPv4 address in its last 32 bits is judged by that address (so `::` in a Python slice is no address). The raw rule is tested to grow linearly (a CPU-time ratio over ten shapes). The gate defends against an accidental commit; deliberate obfuscation (hostnames such as sslip.io that spell an address, percent-encoded or ideographic dots, numbers in words) is listed as a non-goal and left to review. The documentation ranges include `3fff::/20` (RFC 9637), as in `tools/leak_scan.py`. New negatives: an empty part, a malformed CIDR, an unknown part (selection) and an unknown required part (manifest). Both schemas stay draft 1.0 (`x-draft`: a draft changes without a version bump until ADR-0027 is accepted).
- Feature, provider and core modules, a draft for review (ADR-0027, proposed; `docs/modules.md`): the module manifest schema `platform/module` 1.0, a **draft** (`x-draft`, not frozen until a loader and the pilot exist) with a `manifest_version` field. A provider fills slots with one product, the only place a product name may appear; a feature is an optional, removable mission naming slots, model roles, interfaces and host roles; a core module holds the staff agents and core consumers so a swap's dependants re-check sees them. Specified around it: a slot registry (capability descriptor 1.1: stage, formats, stateful and export formats, provisional, required in the core, privileged, pass-through), interfaces with one owner, overlay grants for egress (the only way to reach static hosting), cross-module secret reads, public routes and privileged configuration, a trust-tier table mapping each contribution class to its existing gate and approver, names bound to the module id, code limited to the module's own package and shared adapters, module origin with tree digests and overlay-held reviews, swap record 1.2, `platform/module_selection` 1.0 with lane-to-instance bindings and footprints over cpu, memory, disk and GPU. A draft `platform/lake_table` 1.0 record. Fictional examples in `examples/modules/` (sixteen modules, a slot registry draft, three site profiles) with contract tests that apply the loader rules to them and validate every contributed record (fleet_units specs, criteria, agent specs against a ceiling policy, Sigma rules, MCP records, service rows, prompts, lake tables); no loader or runtime code yet. The schema, its examples and its negatives are generated by `python -m homelabnext.schemas` (`make schemas` and CI's `schemas` job check them). A draft `platform/module_selection` 1.0 schema validates the site profiles, so a misspelt grant kind is refused. The contract-test rules bind `origin: in-tree` and `kind: core` to the public modules tree, keep the shadow lane out of the real lane's prefix, key interface ownership on the id, give a name to the longest module id it is named under, compare names across modules (roles, units, rows, MCP and det ids, identifiers, write prefixes), check datasets' `via` and fallback data, service upstreams, egress grants (internet, authority services, external hosts), public-route grants, site config secrets against `grants.secrets`, lane bindings, core placement, and GPU exclusivity with role counts. Egress grants name their targets (`grants.egress.<module>.<role>: [internet|external|svc:...]`), so a manifest cannot widen a granted role, and a `svc:` target must exist (with or without the rows); every grant kind (egress, internet_facing, public_routes, publish, secrets, units, shared_config) for an unselected module is refused, and so is one naming what does not exist for it (a role, a public row, an egress target, a unit, a shared_config target, a credential no other selected module holds, or its own secret) ("remove the grant"); an unused secrets grant is not detectable from the manifest; the loader rules live in `homelabnext.modules.rules`; an internet-facing role inside the lab needs `grants.internet_facing`; a row's status sources equal the listed ones; the rules' default in-tree root is `modules/` alone (the tests pass `examples/modules`). Defaults shorten the common manifest (`upgrade`, `exposure`, `production`, `display`, `egress`, `purpose`). The docs and the ADR mark what is specified but not implemented (capability 1.1, swap record 1.2, interface 1.0, several trust-tier grants, the supply-chain lock, the egress generator, import validation, footprint limits) with a follow-up list.
- `tools/schema_check.py`: a schema marked `x-draft` (with the reason, naming its ADR) has no frozen baseline and `--freeze` skips it. Only a schema that was never frozen, or a new major, may be a draft: `x-draft` on a major with any frozen baseline is an error. Baselines are found by the `$id` name, not the file name, two files may not carry one `$id`, and a frozen name no live schema carries is an error. With `--base REF` (CI: the pull request's base) `schemas/_frozen/` is append-only: every file there at REF must still exist, byte-identical. So `x-draft` cannot lift the additivity gate off a released schema while its baselines are kept, and the append-only check is what keeps them; `--ci-base` picks the base in CI: an unreadable pull request base is an error, a push whose previous tip is zero or gone (a new branch, a force-push) is compared with `HEAD^`, and any other event or a shallow checkout with no base is an error; the step prints the base it used, and the register-ids and regression-tests steps follow the same rule. Without a base a deleted baseline is not detected; a symlink under `schemas/_frozen/` is always refused.
- MCP catalog (ADR-0024, `docs/mcp-catalog.md`, P-14): one reviewed record per MCP server, and every client config generated from it (`hnx mcp catalog check|list|classes|render|import`, `make mcp-check`). A record names its pinned origin with the package's reviewed `bins`, its program (an allowlisted `hnx` entry, or a first-party path rendered absolute under `checkout_root`) and the server's own arguments. Render generates the launch with fixed flags (`npx --prefix=<absolute npm_prefix>`, `uvx --no-config`), so the client's working directory cannot replace the pin, and settings, env and parameter keys may not configure the launcher; a third party's hosted server is an https `remote` URL. Check refuses literal secrets, unpinned launches and unreviewed records, and no message prints a value. A hosted server is an https URL naming a public DNS host (never an IPv4 address in any notation) and is `cloud-allowed`; a local-only profile is granted an internet-reaching server only by a named `internet_mcp` exception in the ceiling policy; no record sets a variable that loads code or redirects trust in the launcher (OpenSSL and TLS, proxies, glibc, shell and interpreter options); render refuses an `npm_prefix` that is the root, a symlink or not empty, and a symlinked `checkout_root`. Render runs the whole record check, needs the review policy, and accepts only profiles `hnx agent verify` accepts against a ceiling policy, which attests each profile's data tier and model role. Import drafts records from existing client configs and fails closed on credentials. The grant compiler, verify and lint judge grants against the catalog.
- The event loop between the bus, StackStorm, the broker and the agent controller (docs/stackstorm.md, docs/broker.md; checklist R-09, P-11, P-12, P-13). `hnx broker relay` publishes the broker's outbox to NATS JetStream in emit order (a persisted counter), at least once and deduplicated by event id, as its own least-privileged user that only reads the outbox; it validates each event and its `svc:broker` source before publishing, quarantines malformed files, sets aside events the bus permanently refuses (`--skip`, `--since`, retention) and never blocks or fails a decision. `hnx workflows st2 bridge` relays bound events from durable JetStream consumers to StackStorm's webhook: validated and source-pinned, acknowledged only after a 2xx, poison and oversized bodies dead-lettered, a lease longer than a POST, per-subject backoff, streams provisioned with limits (never created by the bridge) and its own bus user. A03 runs a request StackStorm submitted, once, after a human approves it (agent approvals now say `tier_used: agent`); redelivered events are dropped by id and the runtime's datastore keys are HMAC-keyed. A01 and A02 speak the controller's job contract (source, failure classes, one resubmit, idempotency per failed run) and the submit handler sends the controller's jobs token. The lab-in-a-box bus requires a user per client, only the broker may publish decisions and proposals, and the nightly checks both. The NATS password goes only over TLS or to loopback.
- Model serving with one store and few engines (ADR-0023, `docs/model-serving.md`, checklist A-08 to A-13): a model-store manifest (`platform/model_store` 1.0) lists each (source, revision, quant) once, pinned to a commit with a sha256 and size per file, and `hnx models store validate|check|list` verifies the bytes on disk (symlinks must resolve inside their entry); a serving plan (`platform/model_serving` 1.0, `hnx models serving render|check`) turns the store and the role table into vLLM instances whose served name and context length come from the roles, with priority scheduling, unique ports and GPUs, a stated exposure and engine args from a typed allowlist only. Roles `model_store` (fetch by pinned revision into staging, verify, then promote; engines read only), `vllm_server` (one sandboxed unit per instance with only its own GPU device nodes, the store read-only, a pinned vLLM version and a verify-before-start step; health checks `/health` and `/v1/models` against the plan) and `open_webui` (pinned image, the gateway as its only backend through the registry, endpoint settings reserved, persisted UI config off). The LLM gateway generator can read the plan (`serving:`) and refuses a port or host that disagrees with the registry or a loopback-only backend. Hardware checks for the GPU host are listed in the docs.
- StackStorm as the "when" engine for workflow packs (`docs/stackstorm.md`, checklist R-09): `hnx workflows st2 render|check` renders pinned packs and a binding file (`workflows/stackstorm.yml`) into a committed StackStorm pack (webhook and CronTimer rules whose criteria are the pack trigger, one Orquesta workflow per pack, secrets as datastore references only) and fails on drift. A runtime shim admits and coalesces events and builds every broker request with the offline runner's own function, so digests match; risky actions are submitted to the broker and wait for approval, and StackStorm holds no credentials. Rules start in shadow (recorded, never submitted) and go live only with a promotion entry after a shadow window with parity evidence. First automations: A01 validates each filed proposal once, A02 resubmits a failed agent job once for capacity, stall, timeout or backend-down failures, capped and coalesced per agent. New broker action `agent.job.submit` and event `homelabnext.schedule.fired`.
- Role timers report their runs (checklist O-10): a shared `last_run` role installs a writer that each timer's service calls before and after every run (failures, timeouts and kills included) to keep an atomic last-run record per job (`status/last_run` 1.0); a `job-last-run` mapping for `poll-file` (service schema 2.6) turns those records into observations per host, failed runs fail and overdue runs warn from the timer's own calendar, and the Schedule page shows last run, hosts and problems. A role timer that does not write its record is reported on the Schedule page.
- Orchestration adapter for the agent-session controller (`docs/orchestration-controller.md`, checklist R-08): a stdlib `orchestration` adapter for the LLM orchestrator controller (profiles written and reloaded, jobs submitted, waited on and cancelled through its HTTP API, a stand-in `hermes` calling back to an in-process handler host) with live and recorded targets; the recorded stand-in cites the controller's source for every API shape. Against the controller as it stands three MUST checks fail (unknown job, bounded retries, retry until success); the controller changes that close them are made in the controller's own repository. The doc maps the controller's backends onto the gateway's role aliases and proposes additive contract fields (priority, deadline, shadow, constraints, local-only, list-runs).
- UPS state from Network UPS Tools (`docs/status.md` section 8, checklist F-20): a stdlib `poll-nut` collector (upsd protocol, STARTTLS by default with certificate verification, password by secret name, plaintext credentials refused off loopback unless allowed); NUT status flags mapped to ok/warn/fail, battery charge, runtime and load judged by limits that default to the UPS's own low marks; one source generated per `power.ups` entry with `monitored_by: nut` and a `nut_name`; going on battery pages once through the state-diffed notifier and names the guests on that UPS; the Topology map's UPS cards show the live state. Registry service schema 2.5. Tests replay transcripts recorded from upsd 2.8.1.
- Links, Schedule and Agent roster pages on the status hub (checklist O-09), script-free and built from repository data: every registry service by zone with its alias link, exposure and sign-in; every role and profile timer, CI cron, workflow pack and status collector with its cadence in plain words, the scripts it runs and, where a source reports it, its last and next run; every agent profile with owner, runtime, grants, MCP servers, model backends, broker actions, writable lanes and last activity, and the gaps where a profile has no roster entry or no compiled profile. Views schema 1.7, roster schema `status/roster` 1.0.
- The CI deploy tag's tailnet access is generated (checklist F-22): a site `tailnet.deploy` block (site schema 1.2) names the zones the deploy may reach and its tag owners; the `tailscale-acl` target renders the tag owner, one SSH (22) rule to those zones, listed exceptions with a reason, and policy tests that the tag reaches exactly those zones on 22 and is refused elsewhere. Wildcards, unknown zones and non-SSH ports without a reason are refused.
- Runbooks link lessons by uid (runbook schema 2.1): `lessons` items are lesson uids, validated, linked in the generated Links block, shown on the register page and graphed as edges; a 2.0 legacy code stays readable by the schema but is refused by record validation with a hint to run `hnx records assign-uids`, which rewrites it.
- LLM gateway (ADR-0022, proposed; `docs/llm-gateway.md`): `hnx gen gateway render|provision` generates a LiteLLM proxy config (role names as model aliases, per-agent virtual keys with request and token limits) and a Bifrost equivalent from one gateway spec over the model-role table, secrets by name only; it refuses a local-only role whose bindings or fallbacks reach a cloud provider and an agent role whose fallback crosses providers, treats a backend as local only when it is a registry service in a lab zone or a host under the lab's service domain (never under a model provider's API domain), refuses keys that share a secret or its variable, never renders a limit looser than declared, rotates keys by alias, gives Bifrost's admin API a login with its config file authoritative and LiteLLM its own Postgres role, and `--check` re-audits the rendered files (`make gateway-check`). The `inference` conformance suite gains three gateway checks (a bad key is refused with 401, a configured limit throttles with 429, `/v1/models` lists exactly the declared roles) with a stdlib gateway adapter; the lab-in-a-box gains optional, digest-pinned `gateway` (LiteLLM on the lab's Postgres) and `gateway-bifrost` profiles outside the default memory budget. An evidence run put LiteLLM with Postgres at 12/12 and Bifrost at 9/12, with Bifrost serving a fallback to a key outside its scope; the ADR records the table.
- Public and private repositories (ADR-0021, `docs/private-overlay.md`): a documented split where the public repository holds code, schemas, fictional examples and CI, and a private overlay holds the real lab data, loaded through the data-dir mechanism; secrets are never in git (named `secret:` references resolved from OpenBao, or ansible-vault / SOPS+age files), and keys are offline. `examples/private-overlay/` is a fictional skeleton of the private repository: marker file, `.sops.yaml`, `ansible.cfg` with a vault-password client that reads an injected variable or OpenBao by name, a checks workflow and a deploy workflow that runs only on manual dispatch from `main` into a protected `production` Environment, joining the tailnet with an ephemeral tagged key or running on a self-hosted runner (actions pinned to commits; the plan matrix for Free, Pro/Team and Enterprise is documented). SOPS-encrypted data files are decrypted in memory through the `sops` binary. `tools/private_leak_check.py`: in the private repository it fails on plaintext secret values, unsealed files `.sops.yaml` requires sealed, vault files without the vault header and committed key files; in the public repository it refuses any private-overlay path. The leak scan now also catches age secret keys. A first-deploy runbook and checklist F-21.
- Project management, the sixteenth capability (ADR-0020, `docs/projects.md`): a `projects` contract (projects, steps and tasks with statuses, dependencies, parent/child, links to strategy records by uid labelled supports/serves/measures/answers, optimistic versions, a change feed) with a conformance suite whose MUST checks include the board rules (a step card belongs to an open step, unique ids, a project whose steps are all done closes) and catch every mutant. A Markdown reference store (one `<uid>.md` per item, conflict-free writes), `hnx projects new|list|show|move|close|link|check|feed|doctor|sync-strategy`, a Projects hub page and a `homelabnext.project.changed` event. An OpenProject adapter with a stdlib client: `doctor` checks the one-time admin setup by name, `sync-strategy` mirrors strategy records one way into a read-only Strategy project keyed by uid (repo wins, edits in OpenProject are reverted and reported, nothing is auto-closed), and the change feed polls `updatedAt` with an HMAC-checked webhook as a trigger, deduplicated by work-package version; finished work may feed MOPs, never MOEs. Checklist P-08 and P-09.
- Records are identified by UUIDs, numbers are computed (ADR-0019): every lesson, runbook and strategy record (SO, OO, TO, TT, MOP, MOE, CCIR, FFIR, PIR, RFI) carries a permanent UUIDv7 `uid`, is stored as `<uid>.md` and links by uid only; display numbers such as `PIR 2.1` are computed from type, parent and sibling `order` wherever records are shown (register and strategy pages, Obsidian `[[uid|number title]]` links, the knowledge graph, MCP), so inserting, reordering or re-parenting changes no record or link. `hnx records assign-uids|numbers|uid`; the migration is idempotent and keeps old codes resolvable through an `aliases.yml` map with a warning. Fatal validation: valid, unique uids, files named by uid, every link resolves, parents of the right type per the doctrine, no cycles, unique sibling order. The `XX-NEW` allocation, lock and placeholder checks are retired (nothing is allocated any more); `check-lost` now guards uids and `check-uids` is new. Lesson, runbook and strategy record schemas 2.0. Checklist M-16 and M-17 reworded.
- Generated status-hub maps (ADR-0018): a "Maps" menu group with Topology, Dataflow, Architecture and Roadmap, built only from repository data. Topology and Dataflow are interactive pages modelled on a working lab's generator: the uplink chain, hypervisors and physical hosts on top, one collapsible lane per zone split into capability stacks, host cards with resources and badges (services, vmid, not IaC, planned, retired, UPS), a detail panel (descriptor, what it does, services, flows in and out), toggled flow and power lines, filter, pan and zoom, light and dark. One packaged script served under a per-route CSP (inlined and hash-pinned in static renders); data in escaped JSON, the page built with textContent only, a server-rendered fallback without JavaScript, and a browser smoke test. Dataflow grades every producer-to-consumer flow as live, planned, gap or sample. Roadmap shows each build phase's checklist criteria with live invariants results (a new `Checklist` column in docs/build-order.md; every checklist row belongs to a phase). Guest schema 1.4 (`status`, `description`, `power` for hosts), service schema 2.4 (`description`), views schema 1.5, and a fictional 17-guest example in `examples/lab-topology/`.
- Power and uplink chain in the site model (site schema 1.1): `power.ups` (id, model, monitored_by, nut_name, serial, mac), `power.default` and an ordered `edge` chain (internet, modem, router, ...). Every hypervisor, physical host, router and switch names the UPS that feeds it or `none` with a reason; guests inherit their node's power; a UPS that feeds nothing is an error. Checklist F-20.
- Products for every baseline control (23): chrony, unattended_upgrades, needrestart (list-only `restart-required` control, state-diffed alerts by mail or notify), ssh_hardening (refuses a config that would lock out the automation account), sssd_ldap (keeps a break-glass account), nftables_host (host firewall and egress filter from the guest model's own rules), os_hardening, rsyslog_forward, auditd, lynis (scheduled host assessment plus a `dpkg --verify` package-integrity sweep), trivy (package-scan; apt key pinned), aide, rkhunter, clamav, disk_encryption_check and config_leak_scan (check only), restore_drill (central) and etckeeper. New controls host-audit, host-assessment, package-scan, file-integrity, rootkit-check, malware-scan and restart-required, and a `container-host` baseline. Baseline set schema 1.2 adds per-control settings (`{id, with}`) validated against each role's own argument specs and merged through `extends`. Every config is checked by its daemon before going live, and a failed reload stays owed. Vuls is documented as an agentless alternative for package-scan.
- Broker follow-ups: the hub menu lists the Approvals page (views schema 1.4, private, shown only when approvals are configured); approval-form tokens are keyed from the secret store by name (`approvals.csrf_key`, agents schema 1.2), so they survive restarts and work across hub instances. The lab-in-a-box nightly runs the broker end to end: a broker, the hub and an edge as distinct users on one socket, a real MCP client writing to its lane and filing a proposal, unmapped and self-approving callers refused, a front-door approval through the hub with its CSRF token, a single run and the audit trail (`make lab-broker`, check `broker`).
- Broker service, stage 2b (ADR-0017 "Stage 2b"): people approve broker requests through the front door. The status hub's Approvals page (`/approvals`) lists pending requests and recent decisions behind the private tier's protection (trusted proxy, front-door secret, Authentik identity); viewers may read it, only a separate approver group may decide. `POST /approvals/decide` is the hub's only write: same-origin check, a per-request HMAC token bound to the request, its digest and the approver, and a refusal when the request changed since it was shown; the approver sent to the broker is the configured issuer plus the front-door username, never a form field; every attempt is in the access log. The hub relays decisions over the broker socket (agents config schema 1.1, `approvals:`). `decision.requested` links to the page (`--approvals-url`), and ntfy notifications carry it as a click action. `hnx broker import-proposals` files an existing directory of proposal notes into the broker's proposal lane (dry run unless `--apply`), and `docs/broker.md` maps a vault proposal queue onto lanes, approvals and the records lifecycle.
- Broker service, stage 2a (ADR-0017 "Stage 2a", `docs/broker.md`): `hnx broker serve` serves the broker on a Unix socket and authenticates every caller by its peer credentials (`SO_PEERCRED`), mapped to a profile through the manifest's `callers` (manifest schema 1.3); a request that names its own actor, an unmapped peer, an oversized or slow request is refused and audited (audit schema 1.1, `via`). Approvals over the socket come only from listed `approval_relays`, carrying the human identity the relay authenticated, under the same approver rules; the CLI's `--as` works only when a manifest sets `dev_identity: true`. `homelabnext/broker/client.py` is the client for the CLI (`--socket`), the MCP tools and the coming hub Approvals page. `hnx broker mcp` gives agents broker tools over stdio (`broker_actions`, `broker_call`, `broker_submit`, `broker_status`, `broker_run`, `broker_cancel`, `broker_pending`, `memory_write`, `finding_write`, `proposal_file`), registered as `hnx-broker` (classes `record-write`, `exec`); the agent's identity is its own process user, never a parameter. Linux only.
- Broker service, stage 1 (ADR-0017, `docs/broker.md`): `homelabnext.broker.service.BrokerService` implements the `broker` capability on the reference decision evaluator and passes its conformance suite. Durable, conflict-free state (one exclusively created file per request, claim, result, approval, consumed marker, audit record and outbox event; audit schema `broker/audit` 1.0), so an approval is consumed and a request runs exactly once across processes. Write lanes give agents limited writes to their own memory and findings directories and file proposals into the records inbox, with path containment, size caps, schema validation and a machine reason for every refusal. Exec handlers run digest-pinned files without a shell, with params as JSON on stdin, a scrubbed environment, a process-group timeout, capped output and secrets by name. Approval-gated actions emit `decision.requested`, are approved or denied with `hnx broker approve|deny`, and run once. `hnx broker validate|submit|status|run|cancel|approve|deny|pending|audit`; manifest schema 1.2, handler schema 1.1; a walkthrough in `examples/broker/`. Stage 2 (a transport and MCP tool server, SSO approvals, a hub approvals page) is planned.
- First products bound to baseline controls (ADR-0016 decision 7): `siem-agent` -> Wazuh agent (`wazuh_agent`), `remote-access` -> Tailscale (`tailscale_node`, tags from the guest's zone), `network-sensor` -> Suricata (`suricata_enroll`) and `vuln-scan` -> Greenbone (`greenbone_target`, internal assessment targets). Baseline set schema 1.1 adds `apply: guest|central` and `group`: a central control renders one play on the product's host with the list of guests that need it. New inventory groups `cap_sensor` and `cap_scanner`, a `tailscale-acl` render target that generates the tailnet policy from the zone model and the site's zone-to-zone flows, and an `hnx_secret` lookup so roles take secrets by name only. Known limits are documented per role; the per-guest roles support Debian-family systems.
- Guest baselines and defense layers (ADR-0016, `docs/compute-model.md`): a product-neutral baseline set (`_baselines.yml`, schema `platform/baseline_set` 1.0) defines defense controls in six layers (identity, host, network, detection, data, recovery) and named baselines that extend one another; guest schema 1.3 adds `baseline` (one or more names, per-guest `add`/`remove` with a reason, or a documented `exemption`). When a set exists, every vm, lxc and host must declare a baseline or an exemption. Each control's product is bound in one place, its `role`; until then it renders as an explicit unbound gap. The inventory gains `baseline_<name>` and `control_<id>` groups, a new `baseline-playbook` target applies each bound role, `hnx guests baselines` prints a guest-by-layer coverage matrix (`--check`, `--require-bound`), the status hub has a Baselines page, and checklist F-19 runs in the invariants register. Example baselines: base, server, exposed, sensitive, scratch.
- Strategy to task (ADR-0015, `docs/strategy.md`): a discipline-neutral strategy layer as linked Markdown records. A strategy (mission, intent, end state, priorities, assumptions, constraints) with its objective cascade SO → OO → TO → TT; MOPs and MOEs carrying the seven doctrine fields and bound to a source (invariant, status source, records lane, manual or none) and computed by `hnx strategy measure` into append-only readings with red/amber/green rollups; requirements in three types, CCIR, FFIR and PIR, each supporting strategy objectives, with FFIR → CCIR and PIR → CCIR/FFIR links and nothing else allowed; RFIs raised from gaps, triaged to collection or production, expiring at their LTIOV. The doctrine rules are a data-driven policy (`examples/strategy/doctrine.yml`). Status hub pages: Strategy, Requirements, Objectives and RFIs. The example is fictional; a real strategy stays in a private repository and loads through the same loader.
- Status hub, stage 2: product collectors (`homelabnext/status/products.py`, registry service schema 2.3 with per-source `auth` by secret name): `poll-prometheus` (instant queries with thresholds, and firing alerts by severity), `poll-uptime-kuma` (public status-page API), `poll-stackstorm` (failed and running executions), `poll-nifi` (controller status and bulletins), and built-in mappings for the LLM orchestrator's queue summary and Agentmetry's audit status. API shapes come from each product's source and recorded responses; the lab-in-a-box now runs Prometheus and a provisioned Uptime Kuma, polled through the real collectors in the smoke test (StackStorm and NiFi are too heavy for the 4 GB lab and are tested against recorded responses).
- Status hub, stage 1b: agent activity from any runtime, local or cloud (`homelabnext/status/agents/`, `docs/status.md` section 6). A summary tier (`status/agent_activity` 1.0: session, runtime, model, tool name, counts, outcome; also the bus event `homelabnext.agent.activity`) feeds the Agents, Activity (with CSV/JSONL export) and Agent analytics pages, and stuck or errored agents appear on the main page. A detail tier (`status/agent_activity_detail` 1.0: prompts, commands, tool input and output, approvals) is redacted by the screener before storage, size-capped and purged by retention, never put on the bus, and served only through the front door: trusted proxy, a shared-secret header the generated Caddy site adds, an Authentik identity and the viewer group, with every view in an access log. Collectors: a Claude Code hook (`hooks/claude_code_activity.py`), an authenticated push listener (`hnx status ingest`, one token per sender), an Agentmetry JSONL reader and a file spool.
- Conflict-free writes (ADR-0014, batch 13): machine state of lessons, runbooks and docs/systems pages (reviews, recurrences, verifications, lifecycle transitions) lives in append-only sidecar event files (`<record>.events/`, one exclusively created file per event, `register_event` schema 1.0); `reviews`, `recurrences`, `last_verified` and the current status are derived on load, so the human-written record file has a single kind of writer. `hnx registers events migrate|add`. Agents change a status by filing a `register-transition` intent in the records inbox; `hnx registers decide` applies it under a lock against the review policy (two conflicting intents: exactly one applies). `homelabnext.runs.run_dir` gives each run a private output directory and `tools/run_output_lint.py` fails scripts that write scratch files elsewhere. `write_record`/`write_event` validate before an atomic write.
- Status hub, stage 1 (ADR-0013, `docs/status.md`): a read-only primary console that does not depend on agents. Services declare `status_sources` in the registry (service schema 2.1: poll-http-json, poll-file, push-event; targets by registry name, interval, staleness, timeout, a declarative or built-in mapping), `hnx status sources` and `docs/status/sources.md` are the menu, collectors normalise everything to `status/observation` 1.0 records and `status.observed` events, each source runs with its own timeout (a hung source goes stale without stalling the rest), sources that stop reporting go stale, and transitions page once through the state-diffed notifier. `examples/status/views.yml` defines the shell (a main page and a left menu of built-in panels or optional external apps such as Agentmetry and Grafana); `hnx status render|serve` give a self-contained, script-free page and a loopback-only, read-only server that refuses foreign Host and Origin headers (DNS rebinding) and sends a strict CSP. Status sources are graph nodes. Checklist O-05, O-06 and O-07 are PUBLIC-NOW.
- `context`, the fifteenth capability (batch 9): scoped retrieval for agents (`conformance/protocols/context.py`: `index`, `query`, `get`, `delete`), with a mandatory `allowed` scope, 13 MUST checks (the scope is never exceeded, hidden documents look missing and are never related through, inactive records are excluded, deterministic and budgeted ranking, re-index and delete leave no stale copy, hostile query text is refused safely), six mutants each caught, a built-in fake and a reference implementation (`homelabnext/context`) that indexes the repository's knowledge graph. A product such as OpenViking joins as an adapter.
- Read-only MCP server (batch 10): `hnx mcp serve` (stdio only; extra `homelabnext[mcp]`) exposes `list_capabilities`, `get_contract`, `get_schema`, `find_services`, `related`, `context_for`, `lessons_due` and `invariants_status` as deterministic, size-capped JSON built from the same code as the docs. `context_for` requires an `allowed` scope, `--scope-from-profile` derives it from a verified profile's grants, and a call can only narrow it. Registered as `hnx-catalog` (class `read`); see `docs/mcp.md`.
- Linked knowledge in context packs (batch 8): agent specs take `include:` rules (`from: capabilities|services|mcp|task`, `kinds`, `depth`, `budget`, `require_reviews`; profile schema 1.2) that pull the content of related lessons, runbooks, checks, schemas and docs from the knowledge graph into the pack's volatile section. Only nodes wholly about things the profile is granted enter, seeds must be granted (`IncludeViolation`, re-checked at verify and pack time), inactive records never enter, and the pack records included ids with digests and every exclusion with its reason (`hnx agent pack --explain`). Knowledge never adds a grant.
- Knowledge as records and a graph (batch 7). Lessons and runbooks are one Markdown file per record (`examples/registers/lessons/LL-NNN.md`, `runbooks/RB-NNN.md`) with schema-checked front-matter, a generated Links block and the same fatal validation, read through `homelabnext.registers.load_register`; `hnx registers split|join` converted the old files losslessly, and ids (`LL-NEW`, check-lost, check-dupes) work across the directories. Lessons are `pitfall` (graduates into a mechanism: `retired_by` an invariant, test or gate) or `practice` (graduates into a procedure: `incorporated_into` a runbook step or SOP section); inactive records stay as files but drop out of every query, and `hnx registers review` lists what is due (review, retire, incorporate, supersede, update, keep). A shared review model (`homelabnext/reviews.py`, `examples/review-policy.yml`) gives lessons, runbooks and docs/systems pages a `reviews:` list with agent `check`, agent `audit` (a second, independent agent; first named `challenge`) and human `approve` tiers: no self-review, the audit reviewer differs from every checker, no agent in a human slot, and reviews go stale when the reviewed content's digest changes (`hnx reviews digest`). The knowledge graph (`homelabnext.graph`, `hnx graph build|check|related`, `docs/graph.md`) links services, capabilities, products, adapters, checks, schemas, lessons, runbooks, invariants, scripts, profiles, skills, MCP servers and docs by typed edges read only from source files; CI fails on a dangling edge or duplicate id, `related` ranks what a task should pull in, and `docs/graph/` is a local build (not committed).
- A "Scope of security testing" statement in the README and SECURITY.md: all security testing the project describes is internal and authorized, against the operator's own lab.
- Capability reference pages (L-05): `docs/capabilities/<capability>.md` for all 14 capabilities, generated from the contracts, suites, fakes, adapters, targets and registry by `hnx docs capabilities generate|check` (checked in `make check` and CI): operations with signatures and errors, data shapes as field tables with linked JSON Schemas, the MUST/SHOULD checks, every implementation with its product limits, the services using it, and how to integrate a new product. The index is a capability-by-implementation matrix for tool choice. `docs/` opens as an Obsidian vault (`docs/README.md` is its home page; wiki-links are folder-qualified), and every system page links its capability page.
