- Duplication ratchet (M-03): `tools/dup_check.py` (standard library) finds copy-pasted token runs in library and tool code; `tools/dup_check.baseline.json` records today's 35 clones (0.89% of lines), any new clone fails `make check` and CI, and the baseline can only shrink unless an increase is recorded with a reason. Two copied helpers were consolidated (`homelabnext/_yaml.py`, `lake.tables.column_diffs`).
- Real-product conformance in the lab-in-a-box (batch 5): stdlib adapters for Garage (`object-store`, with a SigV4 signer checked against AWS's published vectors), Trino plus Lakekeeper (`lake-query`, `lake-catalog`; values bound server-side through `EXECUTE IMMEDIATE ... USING`), NATS JetStream (`events`, through the new `nats` extra), OpenBao (`secrets`, KV v2 with child-token leases) and ntfy (`notify`), each wired as `compose/lab-in-a-box/targets/<capability>.yml` so the nightly run replaces the built-in fake for six capabilities. The first swap rehearsal (P9.2, `homelabnext.swap.rehearsal`, `rehearsals/swap-lake-query.sh`) swaps the fake lake for Trino through shadow, parity and flip, rolls back, and checks the record and the restored incumbent. Product limits the suites do not cover are documented in each adapter.
- Assurance loop closed (batch 4): the example invariants register now carries 70 runnable criteria (every PUBLIC-NOW row with a gate), `make invariants` and a CI job run the fast ones on every change, and pytest-selection criteria pass only when every selected test passed. New: a reader-to-actor handoff (G-04, `homelabnext.agent.handoff`, schema `agent/handoff` 1.0, `report_result` 1.1 `output`), library clock reads must be injectable (Q-11, `tools/hermetic_lint.py`), `hnx events`, and the coverage floor now also covers workflows, readers, memory, screener, handoff and model limits. The lake checkpoint now uses `homelabnext.state` (with a reader for the old format).
- Workflow packs as code (R-01, R-03, R-04, R-07): pack and deploy schemas (`schemas/workflows`), `hnx workflows validate|run|digest` checking references, deterministic-first ordering, grants, deploy pins and every pack's fixtures offline; risky actions run only under a broker approval bound to their payload digest. Three example packs in `workflows/`.
- Script catalog (M-01, M-02, M-04): an `# hnx-script:` header on every script, a generated catalog (`docs/scripts/`), `hnx scripts reality` (headers match what actually runs each script) and `hnx scripts runner-lint` (every check runs through the invariants runner).
- Decisions and models (A-04, K-08, K-13, K-14, K-15, G-10, G-11): validated model output with one retry, corroborated de-escalation (decision spec 1.3 `noise_gate`), annotate-only classification at ingest, a serving-config probe that alerts on model id or context length drift, four required safety specs (`hnx decide safety`), per-role rate/token/cost limits that throttle and alert, and pinned model versions with a golden evaluation report (`hnx golden pins`, model_roles 1.2).
- Contracts and quality (T-04, T-07, Q-05, D-04, Q-02, Q-08, F-15): event enrichment with asset owner and criticality, generated publisher and consumer contract tests for every event type and lake table, an ATT&CK coverage report counting only fully tested rules, a test-suite policy (budgets, registered skip reasons, expiring quarantine), a fixes-carry-regression-tests gate, and a `homelabnext.deploy.changed` event emitted by the release workflow.
- Repository hygiene (M-03, M-09, M-12, M-14, M-16, F-07, F-17): lints for copied shared helpers, disabled TLS verification and workstation references; controlled agent memory; a generated keyring with a drift check; a per-session access block in every pack; and a push-to-main check that no `XX-NEW` placeholder id lands.
- AI safety guardrails (G-01, G-02, G-03, A-03): a prompt-template format in which every slot declares a trust level and untrusted slots are only ever rendered inside the context pack's nonce-fenced, escaped delimiters (`hnx agent prompts lint`, schema `agent/prompt_template` 1.0, three example templates); a 45-case synthetic injection corpus run through every untrusted slot of every template (`hnx agent injection run`, golden report kept current in CI against a fence-respecting fake, a naive fake caught on every run, and `make injection-eval` for a real model before any model or prompt change); profiles declare `reads_untrusted` (fail-closed) and a data-driven capability-class table makes the compiler, `verify` and `hnx agent lint` refuse a reader holding an exec, file-write, egress or credential grant (profile schema 1.1); a screener in pack assembly redacts secrets and personal data from untrusted input with stable typed labels, a recorded opt-out and a deny-list (context manifest schema 1.1), proven on a 122-case evaluation set with 0 leaks.
- Quality gates (checklist Q-03, Q-04, Q-07, Q-11): a per-unit 80% line-coverage floor on the broker, scope guard, records, decide and agent compiler (`make coverage`, CI `coverage` job); a test-session network guard that fails any test reaching a non-loopback address unless it is marked `live`; `tools/hermetic_lint.py` for direct wall-clock and unseeded-random calls in tests; `tools/component_tests.py`, which fails a package, conformance capability, role, plugin, compose stack or tool without tests.
- Broker request isolation (P-07): requests are bound to the caller that submitted them; another caller's read or cancel is denied as `not-request-owner` and audited (broker decision schema 1.2). The broker conformance protocol gains `submit`, `status`, `run` and `cancel`, four MUST checks and two mutants.
- `hnx decide lint` (K-02, K-03, K-04): every decision spec in git must be complete and tracked, ask single-axis questions with 2-5 labels, pair comparison questions with their mirror, and keep `callers: [inline]` specs on fast tiers within the inline latency budget (decision spec schema 1.2). Runs in `make check` and CI.
- Invariants register: retired-id tombstones (`retired:`) honoured by `check-lost`, `check-dupes` and allocation; the example register's drifted F-01..F-03 are retired in favour of Q-09, L-07 and F-09, and the CI gates for F-05, F-18, D-02, D-07, Q-09, Q-10, M-17, L-06 and L-07 are now criteria that `hnx invariants run` executes.
- Veritas, the name of the project's single source of truth (ADR-0028, accepted for the name and its two rules; a naming and definition decision, nothing is renamed). A file is Veritas when it is authored and a generator or gate reads it as the standard or as a source it copies from. The public tier is this repository's contracts and gates (schemas with `schemas/_frozen/`, capability protocols, conformance suites, schema generators, `tools/` gates and their configuration), profiles, Sigma rules with their fixtures, workflow packs, prompt templates, the injection corpus and fictional examples; the private tier is a site's overlay (ADR-0021: registry, site facts, guests, records, the authored inventory, module selection, secret names; secret values only in the secret store). Every other copy is generated from Veritas or checked against it, and a hand-edited copy is drift; the ADR lists the derived artifacts and their checks, and says plainly that nothing yet compares Veritas with what runs (the loader's two-way check, ADR-0027, is specified, not implemented). The tier lists restate ADRs that are still proposed and follow them. `docs/glossary.md` defines the term; the README, `docs/systems.md`, `docs/modules.md`, `docs/private-overlay.md` and `docs/projects.md` point to it; the system pages' generated Source of truth block names the registry row as Veritas. `tools/tests/test_generated_files_have_checks.py` fails when a committed file whose marker comment says it is generated has no listed drift check.
- `tools/tests/test_constraints_bootstrap.py`: `constraints/bootstrap.txt` must be the pip block of `constraints/ci.txt`, as `make constraints` cuts it. Dependabot edits either file in place and never runs `make constraints`, so a pip bump in one alone would install one pip under one name and another under the other; `.github/dependabot.yml` no longer asks for `make constraints` after every pip update (the in-place hash update is enough).
- JSON Schemas for the conformance harness's input files, `schemas/conform/target.schema.json` (a target file: `name`, and per capability one binding: `adapter` with `config`, `features` and `waive`, or `builtin`, or `fake: true`) and `schemas/conform/capabilities.schema.json` (`capabilities.yml`), with examples, negatives and frozen baselines. `load_target` and `registry.declared_capabilities` validate every file they read against them, so a misspelt key (`confg:`, `capabilites:`) is a `ConformError` (exit 2) instead of being ignored.

### Changed

- `hnx broker serve` with a notify topic requires a declared TLP ceiling (`--notify-ceiling LABEL` or `HNX_BROKER_NOTIFY_CEILING`) and refuses to start without one, instead of defaulting to `red`, the most permissive (ADR-0033 decision 6: a reader with no declared ceiling is refused). Add `--notify-ceiling red` to a unit whose channel only the operators read.
- `hnx broker validate SERVED --decision-table TABLE` refuses a served manifest where it is less strict than a decision table (a tier changed, an approval dropped, a grant widened, agent approvers admitted): how a site's manifest is tied to `workflows/broker.manifest.json`.
- CONTRIBUTING says how to freeze a schema now that a new frozen copy must equal its live schema (run `--freeze` last; fix a missed version forward). The census names a bad MCP server name as such (it said "inventory name"), and the docs give `hnx modules render`'s overlay default for the network model.
- The event envelope is `events/envelope` 1.1, with every event type refreshed to its next minor. 1.0 was edited in place to add the optional `assetid`, `assetowner` and `assetcriticality` extensions (a published contract changed without a bump). 1.1 carries them and is frozen; the 1.0 copy stays as published; each event type's schema (and `status/orchestration_export`, which refers to `agent.job.finished`) now refers to 1.1 and has its own next minor and frozen copy. `agent.job.finished`'s `dataschema` const stays the 1.0 id (changing it would refuse a 1.0 producer).
- Documentation corrections, no behaviour change: the module follow-ups list says which items are implemented (heading now "The follow-ups: each one's status"), the footprint section names `hnx modules render`, the trust-tier approver row, grant kinds and example directories match the code, ADR-0028 decision 8 and the deferred core criteria no longer say the loader does not exist, ADR-0030 names `hnx broker mcp`, README lists X-01..X-11, and ADR-0027 and ADR-0008 state what is not done.
- The `linux` router's IPv6 input narrows what it takes from the WAN: a router solicitation only from a zone (the router advertises to no one on the WAN), an MLD query only from a link-local source at hop limit 1 (the only form a querier sends), and echo on two budgets, 10 a second from the zones and 5 from the WAN, so a ping flood from outside no longer starves the zones' diagnostics.
- `hnx site validate` warns when a networkd site sets `router.wan6` without stating `adapters.options.linux.wan_network` (and so does `hnx site render|plan --adapter linux` on a site whose own adapter is another): the drop-in then extends the assumed `10-<wan_interface>.network`, and networkd silently ignores a drop-in whose file does not exist.
- The docs no longer say `main` is unprotected: a ruleset requires the CI checks, `trusted-gates` among them, before a merge (ADR-0027, ADR-0028), though not a pull-request review. F-08's observable reads a ruleset (`rules/branches/main`) as well as classic protection, whose endpoint answers 404 for a branch a ruleset alone protects.
- `tests/roles` starts at most 8 first role runs at once (one per CPU below that); `HNX_ROLERUN_WORKERS=N` sets the number, and anything but a positive integer is refused (CONTRIBUTING.md, "Time budgets").
- A registered MCP server name is bounded and printed clipped: such a name may be a secret pasted where a name belongs, and the census checks names only for shape (`docs/modules.md`, "MCP servers, from the installed clients"). A name over 48 characters is refused on the host and by `gather`, so it is never returned or persisted; every problem and refusal that names a server keeps the owning module's id whole and shows only the first 8 characters of a rest over 16, with the name's length.
- The module census still gathers only fleet units: `docs/modules.md` ("Why the other kinds are not gathered") records, for service rows, MCP servers and tables, that no host or runner-side query reports them truthfully and in full today, and what each would need. They stay in `not_gathered`, so the loader notes each as not compared (agents and recovery pairs: see Added).
- The role tests (`tests/roles`) take about 40% less time: a test whose first role run needs nothing from another test is marked `rolerun.first_run`, and the first of a module's marked tests to run starts the first runs of every marked test of that module the session will run, one `ansible-playbook` per CPU at a time; each test then reads its own result (the `first` fixture) and goes on as before. Each run is the very command the test ran itself, in its own directory, and every assertion stays where it was. Summed over the six CI shards on a 4-CPU box the suite went from 1194 s to 700 s, so its budget drops from 1200 s to 900 s.
- The conformance sweeps are their own test suite, `tests/conform_sweeps/` (budget 75 s): the mutant sweeps (`test_conform_mutants.py`, `test_conform_protocol_mutants.py`) and X-10 end to end (`test_conform_x10_invariant.py`), moved out of `tests/conform`, whose budget drops from 150 s to 110 s. The sweeps grow with every check and mutant; their budget is now the one that moves (the follow-up docs/codemode.md named).
- The ADR log's one-line column (`docs/adr/README.md`) is generated from each ADR's `summary:` like the docs index, and checked in CI and `make check`: `hnx docs index generate|check` takes several index files, holds a table row whose first cell links a page as it holds a list entry, refuses a summary bound for a table row that holds `|` (`generate` then writes nothing), refuses a summary holding `<` or `>`, and requires exactly one span per entry. The rows lose the hand-written status prefixes ("Proposed:", "Accepted (...)"); an ADR's status is its own Status line.
- CI's `test` job runs in six shards per Python version (`pytest --shard I/N --shard-report FILE`: the collected tests, after `-m` and `--deselect`, whose node id hashes to the I-th of N parts), so the 3.12 run that took 34.5 min whole finishes in a fraction of that (six rather than four: in four, the 3.12 shards ran near-equal test counts in 491-593 s of pytest, a spread of per-test cost that more shards cut, as the slowest shard sets the wall time, more than timing-weighted balancing would, without a timing table to maintain); each job's timeout is 25 min. The new `test-ok` job merges the shard reports (`python -m tests.shard_report`, standard library only) and fails unless every shard passed, each ran exactly the tests it kept, together they ran each collected test exactly once (count and digest of the node ids), and each suite's time summed over the shards is within its `suite_budgets` budget (a shared fixture counts once per shard that uses it); each shard still holds the budgets on its own share. **Branch protection:** the shard checks are named `test (3.12, 1/6)` and so on; require `test-ok`, the one stable name, instead of the old `test (...)` checks. `tools/tests/test_ci_runs_tests.py` (Q-01) accepts the shard flags only with a matrix running shards 1..N, each job its own, and a `test-ok` that needs `test`, always runs, and whose unconditional merge step checks every Python version of the matrix and fails unless every shard passed.
- **Behaviour change:** the MCP tool `context_for` takes `detail: "summary" | "full"` and defaults to `"summary"` (ADR-0032): each hit is id, kind, title, path, score, digest, `summary` and `summary_source`, with no content and no snippet. Pass `"detail": "full"` for the previous answer (content capped as before, `content_truncated`/`content_chars`), which now also carries the two summary fields. No caller in this repository read the content (the context-pack assembler selects from the graph, not through this tool), and none was found in the sibling agent repositories.
- Broker: `validate_manifest` (`hnx broker validate`, `serve`, every manifest load) refuses a name holding a control character: `manifest_id`, an action's `action`, `handler` and `lane`, a grant's `profile` and actions, a lane's `name` and `root`, approvers, callers and approval relays, and a handler document's `action` and `handler`. The schemas' patterns end in `$`, which also matches before a trailing newline, so `profile:reader\n` passed them; the problem names the field, never the value (`tests/broker/test_broker_name_controls.py`).
- Two example agent names are generic role names (`comms-lead`, `assessment-lead`) in the status fixtures, the reviews docstring and `docs/registers.md`. `registers/register_event` 1.3 uses the new name in its writer description, edited in place (a description is an annotation, so the schema validates exactly what it did); the frozen 1.0 to 1.3 baselines keep the old name because `_frozen/` is append-only.
- `model_store` stats and hashes an entry's files in one module call (`library/model_store_files.py`) instead of one round trip per file, which matters over SSH for models with many shards.
- Customization links resolve by uid: `links.lesson` and `links.runbook` take uids, and a legacy `LL-NNN`/`RB-NNN` code resolves through `aliases.yml` with one warning naming the uid (`hnx custom check` CU017); a code not in the map, a retired code or a uid of the wrong record kind is an error (CU016) and a dangling graph edge. The register shows the computed number and uid, and `hnx records assign-uids` rewrites customization links too.
- ADR-0019 gains a Scope section: invariant criteria keep their checklist row ids and system and capability pages keep their registry and capability names (neither is allocated or positional, and both are cited verbatim elsewhere); `hnx records assign-uids` refuses those families by name.
- Names follow use: the second, independent agent review is an **audit** (an independent agent audit that looks for what is wrong, missing or unproven). The review role `challenge` is now `audit` everywhere: `homelabnext.reviews` (`ROLES`, "agent audit" messages), `examples/review-policy.yml` (reviewer `agent:example-auditor`), the graph's `reviewed` tier, a profile's `include.require_reviews`, docs and tests; the example review events are renamed `*-review-agent-example-auditor.yml`. Additive and compatible: `register_event` 1.2 and agent `profile` 1.3 add `audit` and keep `challenge`, which `homelabnext.reviews.normalize_role` reads as `audit` with a `DeprecatedReviewRole` warning (never a failure), and a test fails when `challenge` reappears in `examples/` or `docs/`. A new **Terms and scope** context (`homelabnext/agent/terms.md`, generated into `docs/terms.md` and checked by `hnx agent terms --check` in `make check`) defines auditing, audit review, auditing tool, internal assessment / validation / engagement and the authorized scope (the operator's own lab), and keeps threat vocabulary for external threats; every session pack (`start_session`) carries it as the reserved stable section `terms` (a manifest cannot define, replace or drop it; it is in the pack digest), and the MCP server has a read-only `terms()` tool. README and SECURITY scope sections, architecture row 10 and ADR-0011 now say auditing tools and internal assessment.
- Neutral wording for internal security testing: architecture row 10, checklist L-04 and ADR-0011 describe authorized internal testing (now internal assessment, see the audit entry above).
- Releases are published to GitHub only for now; the PyPI publish job in `release.yml` is skipped unless the repository variable `PUBLISH_TO_PYPI` is `true`.
- Conformance suites may hold 6 to 24 checks (was 6 to 16), one per clause of the Protocol docstring: the broker's `audit-redacts-secret-args` no longer also asserts the deferred run's success, its audit shape and the refusal count, so a failure named for redaction means redaction (`run-audited-once`, `every-submit-audited` and `submit-keeps-its-own-copy` hold the rest). **Compatibility:** the Protocols of six capabilities were tightened, so a third-party adapter that passed before can now fail: broker (every submit audited, submit keeps a copy, the value of an argument the schema does not name is redacted, a run audited once, a handler failure contained on `run`), events (a stale `ack`/`nack`/`term` settles nothing, a new consumer does not replay history), records (a name taken in any lane, `FileNotFoundError` for deciding a decided note, decisions atomic), secrets (versions are ints from 1, `0`/`True`/`1.0`/`"2"` name none; delete is for good; `lease` refuses `nan`/`inf` and returns a copy), idp (scopes are non-empty whitespace-free tokens compared exactly and case-sensitively, `issue` refuses `nan`/`inf`, a scoped check of a revoked token is invalid, at most 1s of expiry leeway) and object-store (`head` of a missing key raises, a bucket is its own namespace, `a//b`, `a/./b`, `a+b`, `a%2Fb` and `Report` are keys of their own, a deleted key is not listed); and, after the verify pass, events (an unsettled delivery counts against `max_deliver`, dead letters survive a reopen, durables independent after an ack), secrets (every read a copy, per lease holder) and idp (scope-tokens are printable ASCII without space; a bare string for the scope list is refused).

### Removed

- The Bifrost LLM gateway adapter (operator decision, 2026-10-31; ADR-0022, amended): its renderer and audit in `homelabnext/generators/llm_gateway.py`, the generated `bifrost/` configs, the lab's `gateway-bifrost` compose profile (`LAB_GATEWAY_BIFROST_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, `LAB_PORT_GATEWAY_BIFROST`), `targets/inference-gateway-bifrost.yml` and their tests. LiteLLM is the only gateway; `hnx gen gateway render` writes `litellm/` only, and a gateway spec with a `bifrost:` block is refused as an unknown key. The two shapes refused only because Bifrost could not render them (two cloud backends of one provider, a role bound twice on one backend) now render as separate LiteLLM deployments. The gateway spec and the `gateway` conformance feature stay product-neutral, and ADR-0022 names Bifrost as the documented exit. The lab's fake gateway keys drop the `sk-bf-` prefix. Remove a `bifrost:` block and any rendered `bifrost/` directory from an overlay's gateway spec.
- OpenFGA (`authz`) from the lab-in-a-box: it backed no capability or conformance suite, and authorisation in this design is the identity provider plus the broker's allowlists and bound approvals.
- `homelabnext.compute.load_zones_file`: a second site reader that kept the last of two equal keys and that nothing called; `open_site` reads the site with the network model's loader.

### Fixed
- The events publisher no longer parks an event as permanently `rejected` when the forwarder answers 401, 403, 404 or 421 (a wrong URL or credential, `MisconfiguredError`): the event stays `pending` and a rerun, `flush` or `hnx events flush` delivers it after the fix. `hnx events` gains `--auth secret:<name>` (on `deploy-changed` and `flush`), `flush` and `requeue` (makes `rejected` events pending again), and a `rejected` record is removed after the retention like a `sent` one. `write_temp` unlinks its temporary file when the write or fsync fails (ENOSPC no longer leaves dot-temps no quota counts), and the token and journal renames unlink theirs when the rename fails; file modes are unchanged. `--auth` accepts only a canonical secret reference (`SECRET_REF` of `homelabnext.mcp.credentials`, nested names such as `secret:events/broker` included), an Authorization value with a control character is refused without being printed, a secret file that cannot be read or decoded is a misconfiguration (the event stays pending, only the error's class is reported), and `requeue` is one locked rewrite that takes no `--retain-hours`; `requeue` works only within the retention of the refusal. **Breaking for overlay sites:** a forwarder answer of 401/403/404/421 no longer ends in `rejected` (it stays `pending`), and `rejected` records are now pruned after `--retain-hours` (they were kept forever); the `--secrets-dir` lookup of `secret_lookup` (status agents, `hnx events`) refuses a name that escapes the directory: an absolute name (it was read from that absolute path), or a file or symlink that resolves outside the directory (a symlink was followed); the file is opened one component at a time with `O_NOFOLLOW`, so a symlink swapped in after the check fails the read; nested names stay subdirectories, a symlink that stays inside is followed, and a `..` part stays refused. The Ansible `hnx_secret` lookup still follows symlinks.
- Golden task `expect` blocks are type-checked at load (review finding F32): `one_of.values`, `contains.all`/`none` must be lists of strings, `regex.pattern` must compile, `max_length.max_chars` a non-negative integer, `json_fields` bounds numbers and `one_of` a list, `json_schema.schema` a valid schema, `strip`/`case_sensitive` booleans. The error names the task id. Before, a scalar `values: accept` scored a wrong answer 1.0 (the string was iterated as characters) and a bad regex raised mid-run. **Breaking for overlay sites:** a golden suite with such a malformed `expect` now fails to load. `run_suite` also scores inside the per-task try, so a scorer exception marks that one task errored instead of aborting the run, and `_parse_json` treats output nested deeply enough to raise `RecursionError` as invalid JSON (score 0). `json_schema` fails closed (score 0) on deep output or an unresolvable `$ref`, `agentic.score_trace` survives a scorer fault, `json_fields` bounds must be finite with `min` <= `max`, and a scorer-error row keeps the model output. **Breaking for overlay sites:** `one_of.values` and `contains.all`/`none` must be lists of *strings* (a numeric list such as `[1, 2]` no longer loads; quote the values).
- A broker `records-inbox` lane refuses (`lane-exists`) a note whose name a decided lane already holds, as the records library always did: the decider moving it would have collided with the decided note of the same name. A `records-inbox` lane with `overwrite: true` is now a manifest problem (a note is never overwritten).
- The example registry no longer declares metrics endpoints its products do not serve. `lake-catalog` (Lakekeeper 0.14.1) declared `/metrics` on the catalog port 8181, but Lakekeeper serves metrics only on its own plain-HTTP listener (default 9000, configurable via `LAKEKEEPER__METRICS_PORT`); the block is removed, not moved, until a metrics block can name its own scheme (#250), and because 9000 is the object store's port on `lake-core` in this example. `lake-query` (Trino 484) declared port 9091, where Trino never listens; it serves `/metrics` on its own port 8443 behind its authentication, so the block now names that port, and the generated job, which sends no credentials, gets 401 until scrape auth exists (#251). `agent-runtime` (the orchestrator at the pinned `a52a1c4`) serves no Prometheus endpoint at that pin, so its block is removed. `schemas/platform/examples/service.valid.yml` is copied from the registry; `service.scope-out.yml`, an older example of its own (Lakekeeper 0.5.2, Trino 450), and the lake-query port of the `service.*` schema negatives are edited by hand to match. The generator goldens, the system pages and the example keyring (its `registry` digest) are regenerated. `tools/tests/test_pin_consistency.py` holds each example registry's metrics blocks for these three products to what the listed versions serve (`METRICS_SERVED`), fails on a metrics block for a version not listed there, on a lab image or orchestrator pin it does not list, and on two listeners (service or metrics ports) sharing one host and port.
- The JetStream adapter's `parse_ack_reply` refuses a 10-token `$JS.ACK` reply subject, which is neither ADR-15 form (v1 has exactly 9 tokens, v2 at least 11), instead of reading it as v2 with the wrong stream and consumer.
- The lab-in-a-box `llm-gateway-keys` one-shot (profile `gateway`) joins the `lab` network; it had no `networks` and landed on compose's default network, where `llm-gateway.svc.example.internal` does not resolve, so `provision` failed (`Name or service not known`) and no virtual key was created. `tests/compose` now requires every service to join `lab` or have no network at all.
- The S3 object-store adapter signs SigV4's canonical URI (`UriEncode` of the decoded path) instead of the path as sent, which differ only for a dot-segment key the adapter sends as `%2E`. Garage v2 rebuilds the canonical URI that way (as MinIO does), so against the lab's Garage v2.5.0 every request for a key such as `../x` was refused `SignatureDoesNotMatch` and `keys-never-escape-the-bucket` failed. `tests/conform/fake_s3.py` checks signatures the Garage v2 way by default (`canonical_uri="raw"` keeps v1's).
- The example registry (`examples/services.yml`) has the `llm-gateway` service every model consumer calls (docs/llm-gateway.md), in front of `inference` (a neutral `impl`: the product stays in the gateway spec, as the product-name lint requires), and the example agent runtime reaches the gateway instead of the serving engine; the system and capability pages and the generator goldens are regenerated. `tools/tests/test_doc_facts.py` holds every "the `X` capability" in the docs to a capability the example lab provides or a conformance capability.
- docs/build-order.md: the P8 row named its home as "arrives with the swap kit" although `homelabnext/swap/`, `parity/` and `decommission/` ship; S3 now runs the TLP label backfill (S-09) before the data copy and S6 exits only when every model role and agent profile has a TLP ceiling (O-12), as docs/checklist.md requires (`tools/tests/test_doc_facts.py`).
- docs/mcp-catalog.md listed seven tool classes (no `codemode`) and docs/interfaces.md section 5 gave schema ids as `urn:homelabnext:schema:<dir>/<name>:<major>` and six of the thirteen schema directories; both are generated now.
- `tools/fix_has_test.py` counts `from pkg import mod` failing on the parent as a missing name the fix adds when the fix's own diff creates that module. A squash of a pull request that renamed or added a module and imported it that way was refused on the push to main ("cannot prove the fix ... because of <module>, which the fix does not add") although every commit of the pull request had been proven.
- The capability-class table (`homelabnext/agent/tool_classes.yml`) classes every capability the example registries use: `cap:apt-cache`, `cap:network-sensor` and `cap:ntp` read, `cap:ups-monitor` read and exec, `cap:dns-secondary`, `cap:dns-upstream` and `cap:detonation` exec, `cap:dfir` and `cap:router-console` exec and credential, `cap:password-manager` credential. They were unclassified, so refused to every profile, and an overlay would have hand-patched the table or granted a broader class. The coverage test (`tests/agent/test_agent_broker_only.py`) now reads every example registry, not only `examples/services.yml`; the example keyring is rebuilt (its `classes` digest).
- An ICMP allow to or from `any` in a guest's `firewall_rules` now allows ICMPv6 as well as ICMP: the `firewall-rules` render writes a `-p ipv6-icmp` line after the `-p icmp` one, and `nftables_host` writes `meta l4proto { icmp, ipv6-icmp }` instead of `ip protocol icmp`, which matched IPv4 only and left ICMPv6 echo dropped on a dual-stack guest (docs/compute-model.md). Ping from anywhere therefore now includes ICMPv6 echo.
- The broker manifest schema describes `refusal_limit` for the peers the socket server applies it to: callers, operators and unknown peers alike; approval relays and the Code Mode runner exempt; a sandbox limited per presented token, with requests presenting no known token counted against a per-uid ceiling ten times the limit (description only, as `docs/broker.md` already said).
- `RoleTable.load` validates the role table against `schemas/platform/model_roles.json` before reading it, so a value the schema refuses (a `tier` outside the enum or missing, a negative `priority`, a `model_id` with a space, a non-boolean `logprobs`, a `temperature` above 2, a fractional `max_requests`, a missing `version`) is a `RoleTableError` naming its path instead of being accepted or coerced. The schema's `digest` description now says what the pin is: the model store's `sha256:` of the entry's `sha256sum` listing (ADR-0023, `hnx models store list`), not a hash of one weights file (description only).
- `hnx scripts reality` sees a CI job that runs a script from a worktree of another commit (`"$RUNNER_TEMP/base/tools/x.py"`, trusted-gates' checker and regression-tests' judge) as calling that script, so the headers of `schema_check.py`, `product_name_lint.py`, `dup_check.py` and `register_ids.py` name `ci:ci/trusted-gates` and `fix_has_test.py`'s `ci:ci/regression-tests` still resolves.
- CI's test shards run the suite as the runner user in a network namespace with only loopback (`unshare --net`, as the go job's tests), so "tests do not use the network" holds for the subprocesses tests start (git, curl, installed tools) and not only for Python's own sockets, which `tests/network_guard.py` still refuses; `tools/tests/test_ci_runs_tests.py` fails if the full run loses the wrapper.
- The coverage floor selects tests as every blocking job does (`-m "not live and not experimental"`, `tools/experimental.py`'s `BLOCKING_SELECT`), so the experimental StackStorm tests no longer run in the blocking coverage job, and the modules a component lists under its new `code:` key in `tools/experimental.yml` (the StackStorm render, bridge and runtime) are left out of their unit (`homelabnext/workflows`) (89.8% of lines without them, measured locally); `tools/tests/test_experimental.py` fails on a blocking job whose pytest selects an experimental test.
- CI runs every command `make check` runs: the schemas job gains `status catalog check`, `status views check` (views.yml carries the status hub's TLP ceilings), `agent terms --check`, the model store, serving plan and gateway checks of `make models-check` and `make gateway-check`, none of which CI ran; `tools/tests/test_make_matches_ci.py` now fails on a `make check` command no required CI job runs (one reasoned exception: `fix-check`, which CI runs as regression-tests).
- The ratchet gates run the base commit's code: CI's trusted-gates job also runs the base's `tools/schema_check.py --strict` (additive evolution, append-only `_frozen/`), `product_name_lint.py`, `dup_check.py` and `register_ids.py check-lost` against the change's tree (`python -I`, the change only as data), and regression-tests judges with the base's `tools/fix_has_test.py`, so a pull request can no longer weaken the gate that judges it; the schemas and register-ids jobs keep the change's copies as quick feedback, and `tests/gates/test_ci_trusted_gates.py` fails when a job runs a ratchet that trusted-gates does not.
- CI's base rule has one copy, `homelabnext/gitbase.py`: `python -m homelabnext.gitbase` prints the base commit (`--allow-root` lets a root commit run with none), the register-ids and regression-tests jobs call it instead of their own shell copies, and trusted-gates reads the file from the commit the event names (the pull request's base, or the pushed tip) and refuses a root commit, so a fix to the rule reaches every job.
- The TLP label grammar is one module, `homelabnext.tlp`: the marker grammar (`markers`, moved from `tlp_egress`, which re-exports it), TLP tags (`tag_label`) and STIX TLP markings (`stix_marking_label`, `STIX_TLP_MARKINGS`, moved from the log pipeline) are read there, beside the lenient readers of incoming labels and declarations (`read`, `parse`) and new canonical-only readers of stored and generated values (`read_stored`, never raises; `parse_stored`, raises), which the lake's `row_label` and row mapping, the source table loader and `analyzers_for` now call instead of their own checks. The log pipeline reads a TLP tag with the egress marker grammar, so a tag such as `TLP : RED`, `TLP-RED`, `[TLP:AMBER]` or a fullwidth look-alike is a claim on arrival (it used to count only when it started with exactly `tlp:`, so the source's looser label stood). `tests/tlp/test_tlp_corpus.py` holds every reader to one corpus of real-feed spellings.
- `TrinoRestLake` writes are idempotent on the server too: each INSERT is `INSERT INTO t SELECT * FROM (VALUES ...) AS v WHERE NOT EXISTS` a row with `v`'s content key, so a statement the coordinator ran twice (a lost reply sent again) appends its rows once, and `rows_written` is the engine's own count. Every INSERT of a write carries the Trino client tag `hnx-batch:<digest of the batch id>` (`TrinoHTTP.execute(..., client_tags=...)`). The adapter tests' stand-in engine now appends a plain `INSERT ... VALUES` without checking keys, as the engine does, where it used to deduplicate and hide the double write.
- `hnx registers decide` and `hnx strategy decide` fail closed (exit 1) without a review policy even when the inbox is empty; `hnx strategy rfis --sweep` resolves the policy before it files any expiry intent, so a refused sweep leaves nothing in the inbox; `hnx registers decide --policy FILE` refuses a missing file with the shared `NO_POLICY` message (`require_policy`), as `hnx strategy` does.
- Swap step evidence is written `0600` and atomically, like the other evidence files (a parity report carries row data); the registry flip at cutover and rollback is atomic. The decommission scan no longer skips files silently: a file that is not UTF-8 is searched with undecodable bytes replaced, and a file it cannot read, one over 1 MB, or a symbolic link is an `unscanned` finding that fails the check unless allow-listed. `hnx swap advance` and `rollback` exit 1 (refused), not 2, when the swap cannot advance or a rollback is left incomplete. Stopping the lake mirror at the rollback window makes the dual writer forward-only: a production write after it goes to the new primary alone, where it used to park a batch for the stopped old component that nothing would ever replay.
- The role-table parser refuses (as `RoleTableError`) a binding that is not a mapping, a non-integer `priority`, non-numeric `limits`, a non-text `fallback`, `params` or `probe` that is not a mapping and a non-positive `max_context_tokens`, which used to escape as tracebacks; role, `svc:`, `mdl:` and digest names (and the gateway spec's secret, slug and host names) no longer accept a trailing newline. `hnx models store validate --roles` exits 1 for an invalid role table (2 only when it cannot be read), and `hnx gen gateway render` exits 2 for a profile or registry it cannot read instead of a traceback. The vLLM plan requires every role on a vLLM backend to declare `max_context_tokens` (a role without one was ignored when another declared it). The binding `digest` comment names the store's listing digest.
- LiteLLM role limits are split over the role's deployments: each of a role's `n` bindings gets `1/n` of its per-minute `rpm`/`tpm` share (rounded down) and of its `max_budget`, so the sum is never above the declared figure; a share too small to split is refused. LiteLLM limits each deployment and moves an over-limit request to the next, so a two-binding role used to get twice its declared rate and budget, while Bifrost enforced the declared figure.
- ADR-0007 names the gateway capability `llm-gateway` (what the registry, Open WebUI and the conformance targets use) instead of per-role `*.svc` endpoints, says which host-side checks resolve a backend directly, and drops the concurrency benchmark ADR-0022 never measured; docs/llm-gateway.md no longer tells consumers to call the `inference` capability (a serving engine). ADR-0004 lists the sixth hook task set, `restore`, that `tools/role_lint.py` requires. ADR-0033 decision 11 is amended to say where the request rule is enforced: key scope at render time and the `--check` audit, with per-request `admit` for the component that assembles the context, which no component calls yet.
- `tools/fix_has_test.py --prove` reads a parent-run `TypeError` for an unexpected keyword argument as a missing name: a regression test that passes a parameter or dataclass field the fix adds (a module-level `Source(..., new_field=True)`) failed to collect on the parent and was reported "cannot prove", where an `AttributeError` for a name the fix adds already proves it. The keyword counts only for its own callee: the callee the `TypeError` names is reduced to its last segment (`pkg.mod.f` to `f`; `X.__init__` and `X.__new__` to the class `X`), and the fix's own diff must add that keyword, as a function parameter or annotated class field, to an owner whose last segment is that same name (a parameter of `X.__init__` or `X.__new__` belongs to `X`), compared against the parent's same owner. The same keyword gained by any other function or class, a keyword nobody added and any other `TypeError` still cannot prove the fix.
- `TrinoHTTP` retries the statement `POST` on `503` only, as its docstring says: a `502` or `504` from a proxy may come after the coordinator took the query, and re-sending it could run an `INSERT` twice past the content-key lookup. Reading `nextUri` still retries `502`/`503`/`504`.
- `events.schema_for_type` builds a schema path only from a type the envelope's pattern allows, so a crafted type (`homelabnext.../ids/common`) can no longer pick another schema and skip the envelope's required fields.
- `events.enrich_event` fills owner and criticality from the asset a publisher-set `assetid` names (or leaves them unset when the inventory does not know it), never from a different resolved asset (docs/events.md).
- `power.fleet_problems` no longer raises `TypeError` on a `power.ups` that is neither a string nor a list; the problem `ref_problems` reports stands.
- The `HttpForwarder` docstring no longer claims a plain `200` after a prior accept is reported as a duplicate; only a `{"duplicate": true}` body is. docs/interfaces.md lists the TLP surfaces (`tlp`, `tlp_egress`, `logpipeline`, `lake.rows`, `lake.ceiling`, `lake.backfill`, `TrinoRestLake`, `CeilingSender`) and `Message.tlp`.
- A guest's `capability` is checked against the platform's one capability vocabulary (the capability-class table `homelabnext/agent/tool_classes.yml` plus the core and extended conformance suites, `homelabnext.compute.model.guest_capabilities`, the same set the MCP catalog's `known_capabilities` uses) instead of a third hand-written copy of the eighteen core names, which no check tied to `capabilities.yml` and which refused honest roles. `KNOWN_CAPABILITIES` is now the conformance harness's own list, re-exported. The example guests say what they are: the reverse proxies `cap:edge` (were `cap:idp`, so the registry fragment gave the TLS proxy the role `idp`), the hypervisors `cap:hypervisor` (were `cap:orchestration` or `cap:events`), and the four-box lab's resolvers and egress box `cap:dns` (were `cap:context`). docs/compute-model.md and docs/architecture.md say so, and that the extended suites (`backup`, `dns`, `edge`...) run in CI beside the eighteen.
- Invariant X-06 and `detections lint-ids` are one rule with one implementation (ADR-0006 decision 2): X-06 now runs `homelabnext.detections.lint.lint_agent_ids` over `find_agent_files`, so the two gates scan the same files and agree. The scan now covers decision specs where they live (`**/decisions/**`, `**/decision_spec*`, e.g. `workflows/decisions/` and `homelabnext/decide/examples/decision_spec.yml`), `lint-ids` also fails on a `det:` id the map lacks (only X-06 checked that before), and X-06 now catches `sid:`/`signature` forms only `lint-ids` caught. A date after the word "rule" (`2026-10-15`, `2026/10/15`) is no longer read as an engine rule number.
- `detections map --check` judges the engine numbers, not only the key sets: a number shared by two rules, a live rule reusing a retired number, a non-integer number, or any other difference from what `map` would write now fails it (ADR-0028's drift check for `det_map.yml`). Before, a hand edit that duplicated or reused a number passed CI.
- `detections compile <backend>` uses `mappings/<backend>.yml` when it exists, falling back to `mappings/generic.yml` with a warning on stderr; it always used the generic map, so a backend's own field map had no effect (docs/detections.md, "Layout"). A rule a backend cannot express is named on stderr and left out of the bundle (exit 1) instead of aborting the whole bundle with a traceback.
- ATT&CK tags are checked against a fixed vocabulary (`homelabnext.detections.sigma.ATTACK_TACTICS`, the Enterprise tactics): only those count as tactics in `attack_coverage.json`, and `lint` fails on an `attack.*` tag that is not lower-case or not a tactic, technique, group (`attack.gNNNN`) or software (`attack.sNNNN`). Before, a group, a misspelt tactic or an upper-case technique tag counted as a covered tactic and passed lint.
- `detections lint` fails on a file under `sigma/` that is not `sigma/<tactic>/<slug>.yml` (a `.yaml`, nested or top-level file), which every gate silently ignored.
- `detections validate-techniques` exits 100 (could not measure), not 1 with a traceback, when the named or advertised snapshot is missing or malformed, and loads a backend's `known_ids_snapshot` itself when the backend has no `known_technique_ids()` method, as `adapters.py` and docs/detections.md say it does.
- `hnx invariants run --only ...` judges its exit code over the criteria it ran: a carried-over red result of a criterion not run no longer fails it, and a previous result of a criterion gone from the register is dropped instead of carried forever. Observables run through the injectable `Context.run`, and one killed by a signal is `error` (did not finish), not `fail`.
- The golden `json_fields` scorer counts failing fields, not failure messages (a field failing two constraints counted twice), and an expected `{}` is a value to equal, not an empty constraint that accepted anything.
- Docs: ADR-0018 decision 8 says what reads a guest's `status` and the descriptions (the maps grade planned/retired from it; it said nothing did); ADR-0028 names the real inputs of `det_map.yml` and `attack_coverage.json`; ADR-0006 marks decisions 3 (OCSF `severity_id`) and 4 (native rules) as not implemented yet; docs/graph.md, `is_active` and `hnx graph related --help` list every inactive status and that it applies to any node kind; the graph package docstring's edge rows match `vocab.EDGE_TYPES` (the test now compares whole rows); stale CLI docs in `attack.py`, `cli.py` and detections/README.md.
- Conformance verdicts (docs/conformance.md, "Running" and "The exporter and invariant X-10"): every run and report entry carries a `verdict` (`pass`, `fail`, `unmeasured`, `misconfigured`), and the report a `verdicts` count, and the exit code of `hnx conform run` and `hnx conform report` is decided by the verdicts alone (`1` if any fails, else `2` if any is misconfigured, else `100` if any could not be measured). `hnx conform report` used to exit `100` (could not measure) whenever one capability errored and no other had a failing MUST check, so a capability with too few checks, a missed mutant or no suite was filed as an outage and X-10 reported ERROR instead of FAIL. A `ValueError` from a factory (a `JSONDecodeError` from a probe) is now could-not-measure instead of exit `2`; an adapter's missing endpoint or credential (`AdapterConfigError`) is `misconfigured` for its own capability while `run all` goes on with the rest, and the report records it against a suite that exists rather than as `suite: false`; a bad waiver or `--check` selection is still refused before any subject is built (`homelabnext.conform.core.validate_selection`).
- `capabilities.yml` has one validated reader, `homelabnext.conform.registry.declared_capabilities`, used by the exporter, invariant X-04 and the graph: a misspelled `capabilities` key, an empty list, a non-string, a duplicate name or a duplicate key is misconfiguration (the report exits `2`, X-04 and X-10 report an error) instead of a report that is `ok` with zero capabilities. X-10 also refuses a report that names no capability, and X-04's fallback without the file no longer counts `suites/`, `protocols/`, `fakes/`, `targets/` and `fixtures/` as capabilities.
- The lab-in-a-box smoke counts a conformance run as "no suite" only on exit `2` with the CLI's own `suite for '<cap>' not found` or `unknown capability '<cap>'` line; a failing check whose evidence says "Not Found" or "not available" used to be skipped without failing the nightly. Its report gains `conformance.capabilities` (`subject: real|fake`, `result`), and a pass against the built-in fake is noted as `fake-only`; ADR-0005 decision 1 now says the nightly runs a suite against a real container only where a lab target ships.
- An unknown `builtin:` in a target binding (`fakes`, `incumbent`, `broken:`) is refused (exit `2`) instead of running the first deliberately broken fake.
- `hnx conform run|report --out` creates its directory (`build/` is absent in a fresh clone) and reports an unwritable path as misconfiguration (exit `2`) instead of a traceback with exit `1`.
- Conformance provenance names the code that ran: `tree_sha256` hashes every `.py` file under `conformance/suites`, `protocols` and `fakes` (the shared check code a suite imports), `dirty` records uncommitted changes beside `git_sha`, and an adapter binding records `adapter_sha256`.
- `tools/conform_coverage.py` reads the report from `hnx conform report --out build/conform-report.json`, never from stdout (an adapter can print anything), and exits `2` when the report's verdicts and the exporter's exit code disagree, as X-10 does.
- Docs: `make conform` runs the core suites (the extended ones run under pytest); the recorded orchestration target skips six capacity checks, not five.
- The access block and keyring rate `hnx-codemode` (class `codemode`) `read`; a class missing from `CLASS_LEVEL` fell to `privileged`, above the broker. Every class of `tool_classes.yml` has a level (tested), and `egress` no longer lists mail, chat and notifications, which are `send`.
- A compiled profile's `budget_mode` defaults to `enforce` (was `warn`), and docs/agent.md says the field is for the runtime and read by no code here yet.
- An IPv6 Code Mode `broker_bind` is a named executor error (the relay is IPv4 TCP only) instead of "the executor failed".
- Doc and docstring drift: docs/agent.md's class vocabulary, schema versions and `--classes` rule, docs/codemode.md's executor check count (held by a test against the suites) and shared index, docs/mcp.md's list of first-party servers, docs/mcp-catalog.md's record schema version, the `hnx codemode` CLI's generated files, the `codemode_search` description of an empty query, and the codemode-runner conformance docstring (a core capability, not extended).
- docs/agent.md, `agent/memory.py` and `agent/keyring.py` no longer call the profile digest check tamper protection (anyone holding the dict can re-digest it; `verify_profile` with a lock pins the reviewed profile), and say that the broker's `memory.write` lane is free-form notes, not the `MemoryStore` the M-09 guarantees describe.
- `hnx modules check` validates every slot registry entry against `platform/slot` and reports one that does not (left out of the registry) instead of reading it; `--deploy` refuses `--slots`, since the slot registry is the pinned checkout's own. An egress `svc:` target naming a provider's row is refused by the site rules as it was by the egress render (a provider's rows are reached by its slot or instance).
- Documentation and docstrings that contradicted the code: the census section of docs/modules.md said MCP servers and tables are never gathered (they are, where the overlay names their sources; only service rows are not gathered by name); the modules package, the swap module and the module schemas said there is no loader; the module schema called egress enforcement unimplemented; footprint limits said no cap is resized in place (a ZFS cap is; an image cap is not); `lake_access` said the most restrictive rule always applies (a principal's own entry wins over its groups, as docs/modules.md says); and docs/modules.md said the log pipeline's TLP tagging, the backfill and reader ceilings were still to come (all three exist; running the backfill and loading `lake-access.json` are the operator's steps).
- Workflows and decide (orchestration safety): a StackStorm schedule whose day-of-week range starts at Sunday no longer renders a backwards CronTimer range (`0-4` was `sun-thu`, `0-7` was Sunday only; now `sun,mon-thu` and `mon-sun`). The cascade accepts only labels the spec's own decision table produces (`DecisionSpec.answer_labels`; `raise`/`hold` for a safety spec): a noise-gate spec whose table never produces `accept` no longer lets a tier's `accept` stand without corroboration. `DecisionSpec.verdicts` (an answer label other than `escalate`, or `human`) is the one vocabulary for the cascade, `ModelTier` in `hnx golden` live mode and workflow decide fixtures, so a fixture says `human` where the cascade escalates and can no longer say `escalate`, which never happens live (`new-critical-alert`'s fixture is corrected). `risky-params` follows model text through enrich steps: an `extract-iocs` or `pick` over a prompt step's reply, and anything derived from it, is refused as a risky action's parameter (a `when` gate does not taint). `hnx workflows validate` reports a reference whose root is neither `$event` nor `$steps.<id>` (it resolved to null), names a decide step's spec only from tracked specs, and says when a named spec is untracked or does not parse. `decide.annotate` normalises a record's `impact` (case, whitespace) and marks a record with an unknown impact for a human instead of reading it as `low`. The StackStorm renderer's controller source is the orchestration telemetry's `CONTROLLER_SOURCE`, not a second literal.
- `hnx custom check`/`plan`: a schema-invalid customization no longer switches off conflict detection (`CU011`), so a conflicting pair is excluded from the resolved view whatever else is broken.
- `hnx records assign-uids` rewrites only the values of a customization's top-level `links` mapping (read structurally from the YAML/JSON node positions), never a `lesson: LL-003` written in the `reason`.
- `hnx decommission`: the `secret-names` plane is word-bounded (`app-core` matches `app_core_db_password`, never `myapp_core_token` or a sibling's `app_core_2_db_password`) and reads `--alias` too; a snapshot section without the key its plane reads is `SKIP`, never `CLEAN`; several `--root` trees with the same relative path are all scanned (each path prefixed with its root's name). The swap's decommission-check and `hnx decommission` share one word-bounded pattern.
- The scope guard decides a URL, `host:port`, `[v6]:port`, `user@host` or one-address CIDR on the host it names, and denies one whose host does not resolve even under `unknown: allow`; an out-of-scope target can no longer be allowed through such a spelling.
- `hnx secrets seed` reads a KV v2 version as deleted only once its `deletion_time` has passed, so a mount with `delete_version_after` no longer reports every live secret as deleted; a backend that fails (`bw sync`, OpenBao) exits 1 and only a usage or settings error (`BAO_ADDR`, `BAO_TOKEN`, `BW_SESSION`) exits 2. The OpenBao client uses the shared `_http.credential_opener`.
- `Decider.complete` loads and validates every related note and checks every destination before the first move, so a collision or a bad note on a later item changes nothing.
- `hnx` reports a missing optional dependency only for a third-party module; a missing `homelabnext` or `conformance` module raises with its traceback.
- `shared_helpers_lint` sees `Path.replace`/`Path.rename` as an atomic replace; the records note validator uses the shared `schema_registry`.
- `hnx strategy decide` refuses an intent whose note name is already in a `decided/` lane before anything applies, as `hnx registers decide` does (a reused name applied to one record while citing another note, then failed to move on every run). Both deciders share the check.
- `hnx registers events add` writes a review or a manual `value` event to a strategy record, validated against the whole strategy tree; it treated every record outside lessons/ and runbooks/ as a system page and refused it (docs/strategy.md, docs/registers.md).
- `hnx strategy rfis --sweep` decides only the sweep's own expiry intents; other pending intents wait for `hnx strategy decide`.
- `hnx projects check` applies the write path's rules to hand-edited files: a `blocks`/`precedes` target must be a step or task, and a parent a step or task of the same project (one implementation for both).
- `hnx registers review` marks a `make:` target or a path in a retire suggestion's `retired_by` as unverified: only an invariant is run.
- Docs: the strategy doctrine is nine MOE/MOP rules plus two cascade rules, not nine; the invariants count and the systems-page count are no longer stated; `completion_reading` is documented as a helper no measure binding calls yet; `hnx strategy check` no longer lists options it does not take.
- Broker: a handler document's `requires_approval: true` under a manifest entry saying `false` passed `hnx broker validate` and ran with no approval. `check_manifest_handlers` now refuses any disagreement on `requires_approval` (in either direction), and a service built from unvalidated documents requires approval when either asks. Manifests whose handler is stricter than their entry now fail validation: set the entry's `requires_approval: true`.
- Broker: a pending request whose action, grant, tier or handler was withdrawn could never be denied (the denial re-ran the whole decision and was refused `not-granted`), so it stayed on the Approvals page, kept paging and held its actor's `pending_per_actor` slot. A denial is judged by the approver rules alone (`homelabnext.broker.approver_refusal`: self, allowlist, agent); an approval still needs the request to be runnable.
- Broker: the socket relay and the hub accepted approval notes of 2000 characters while the approval schema allows 500, so a longer note was accepted at the door and refused with no reason and the whole note quoted back. The limit is read from `schemas/broker/approval` (`homelabnext.broker.approval_note_max`) by the service, the relay and the hub's form; an over-long note is refused `approval-invalid` without echoing it.
- Broker: `hnx broker import-proposals --apply` kept sending every remaining file after the lane's write rate, its quota or the socket's refusal limit was hit, each refused in turn. It stops at the first such refusal and reports the rest `not-attempted` (exit 1); docs/broker.md says how to size the lane for an import.
- `workflows/broker.manifest.json` pins `agent.job.submit`'s handler (`handler_docs`) and maps the StackStorm runner user in `callers`; it is documented as a decision table whose five other handlers are the site's, and a test loads every broker manifest the tree names, holding the table to failing for those handlers only.
- Docs: ADR-0017 no longer says any number of brokers may share one state directory (claims and approvals are exactly-once across processes, but quotas, page state and the `flock`s are per process or per host: one broker per state directory); the peer rule names all five roles and manifest 1.8; refusal audit records are bounded by `refusal_limit`; the rate-limit roles in docs/broker.md match the server; the `--notify-pairs-dir` help names `DIR/broker/pairs.json`.

- `tools/schema_check.check_note_filename` is `homelabnext.records.notes.check_filename` (the records lane's rule, which requires a `profile:` requester) instead of a looser copy that accepted any kind; `check_kanban_board`'s docstring says it is the kanban cross-card rules' executable form, not a generator's. `tests/schemas/test_schema_tlp_enums.py` holds every inlined TLP label enum (each enum holding `amber+strict`) equal to `homelabnext.tlp.LABELS`.
- Pages state the live schema versions: `docs/agent.md` (agent/profile 1.5, broker/manifest 1.8, broker/handler 1.3, broker/audit 1.4, agent/golden_task 1.1; each schema's description holds what each minor added), `docs/compute-model.md` (guest 1.6), `docs/mcp-catalog.md` and `schemas/platform/README.md` (model_roles 1.4, mcp_server 1.3). `tests/schemas/test_schema_doc_versions.py` fails when a page outside the ADRs states a version, as `` `name.json` (X.Y) `` or `` `dir/name` (X.Y) ``, that is not the schema's `$id`. ADR-0028 and the `homelabnext.schemas` docstring list every file the schema generator writes (the `interface` schema, its examples and negatives included).
- The draft `platform/module` and `platform/module_selection` schemas no longer claim "no loader yet": their `x-draft` says the module loader reads them at deploy time and that an overlay pins the core commit until ADR-0027 is accepted (regenerated with `python -m homelabnext.schemas`; docs/modules.md, schemas/platform/README.md and the `tools/schema_check.py` docstring agree). `tools/schema_check.py` refuses a draft whose `x-draft` says no code reads it while code under `homelabnext/` (the generator aside) names its file.
- The shared id schemas refuse a trailing newline (`schemas/ids/README.md`): every anchored pattern in `ids/logical-id` and `ids/common` ends in `$(?!\n)`, so `cap:dns\n` fails the schema as it fails `homelabnext.ids.parse` (Python's `$` matched before the newline). The guard is the same language under JSON Schema's semantics, so neither `$id` changed; `grammar.schema_pattern`/`union_pattern` carry it, and the property test's alphabet now includes newlines. The evolution test's "changed since frozen" rule is the checker's own (`same_version_problems`), which emptied its list of excused status schemas.
- An example or negative whose label holds a dot (`customization.full.maintained.json`) is validated: `tools/schema_check.py` gives each file under `examples/` and `negative/` to the longest sibling schema stem its name starts with, and a file no schema owns is an error. Invariant X-03 runs `tools/schema_check.py --strict` where the tree has it, instead of a second implementation that covered 82 of the 90 schemas (no OCSF `<class>.v1.json`, `service.json`, `model_roles.json` or `decision_spec.json`) and matched instances differently; an adopter's tree without the checker keeps the generic layout check.
- The schema additivity gate (`tools/schema_check.py`, ADR-0001) refuses tightenings it used to pass: a new `multipleOf` (a changed one is additive only when the new value divides the old), a new `contentSchema`, a new `then`/`else` beside an `if` (unless no old document can meet its condition: a branch for a new value of a closed top-level discriminator, how `records/note` and `registers/register_event` grew), a schema replacing `additionalProperties: true`, and a new pattern-property entry that constrains anything. `docs/interfaces.md` section 6 now says what the gate takes as additive (any enum growth; a new property of an open object, by policy) instead of an "open enum" rule nothing implemented.
- The baseline playbook's central-role rows carry the guest's zone `gateway` and `dhcp_range` (`[first, last]`) after `subnet`/`subnet6`, and `hnx_enroll_address_problems` refuses a row's IPv4 extra address that is the gateway or inside the DHCP pool, as guest validation does; a hand-written row without them is judged as before, and an IPv6 extra inside another zone's prefix stays the generator's refusal alone (a row names no other zone).

- `tools/schema_check.py` holds every change below `not`, `if`, a non-discriminated `oneOf` or a capped `contains` (and in a local definition such a position uses) to an unchanged subschema: a wider subschema there can make the schema accept less, which the additive check accepted (`{"not": {"enum": ["a"]}}` widened to `["a", "b"]` read as additive). A `oneOf` whose branches each require a property pinned to a distinct `const` stays monotone.
- `tools/schema_check.py` no longer counts every `$ref` to a later minor as additive: the target `$id` must resolve to a live or frozen schema, and the bump is refused under `not`, `if`, `oneOf` and a `contains` capped by `maxContains`, where a wider target makes the referencing schema accept less (`NON_MONOTONE_KEYWORDS`); the orchestration store test reads its `dataschema` from the live schema's `const` (the pinned first-published 1.0 id) instead of retyping it.
- The swap gate compared the role tables with Python `==`, where `1 == True`: a `leaves_lab` of `1` changed to `true` (which the rules read as a binding whose prompts leave the lab) read as unchanged and needed no record. It now compares them as canonical JSON, types included.
- The swap gate sees a `leaves_lab` that moves from one binding of a role to another: a role's non-pin binding keys are now compared per binding, keyed by its pin, not as a bare multiset (`homelabnext/modules/swap_evidence.py`).
- The swap gate no longer asks for a record when `leaves_lab: false` replaces an absent key (or the reverse): a binding's `leaves_lab` is read as `rules.cloud_roles` reads it (only `true` counts), so the no-op is no change.
- `hnx modules swap-gate --overlay DIR` refuses (exit 2) a `DIR` that is not the root of its git checkout, instead of checking only that it is a directory (docs/modules.md, "The overlay's gate").
- Tests for the overlay gate cover a `leaves_lab` or `fallback` change needing a record that names the role, and a push event's `--ci-base`.
- Schema versioning gaps in `tools/schema_check.py`. A current schema whose `$id` version equals a frozen baseline's but whose content differs beyond documentation (`description`, `title`, `$comment`, `examples`) and the `(?!\n)` newline guard is refused; a non-draft schema with no frozen copy at its own minor is a warning and an error under `--strict` (as a schema with none at all already was), which `platform/model_roles` 1.3 now has; a `$ref` to a later minor of the same schema counts as additive; and the registry resolves a frozen copy's `$ref` to a version only the baselines carry.
- The invariant observables F-15, P-11 and P-13 report could-not-measure (exit 100) instead of FAIL where they cannot measure. F-15's deploy-event test reads the installed version, which falls back to `0.0.0+local` on an uninstalled checkout and has no changelog section; P-11 and P-13 skip their real-bus test without `nats`, which their `none skipped` regex then reads as a failure. Each now guards on the package metadata or `import nats` first (`examples/registers/invariants.yml`).
- The running check and the core-collision check now share one definition of a module's tables. `_declared` (`homelabnext.modules.loader`) counted only `data[].table`, while `_module_tables` counted `data` and `contributes.lake_tables` records, so a table declared only through a lake table record was reported as "runs but declares no such entry" (and a record that was not running was never reported). `_declared` now uses `_module_tables`.
- The review-tier governance list now covers the code the tiers and the swap gate import. `GOVERNANCE` (`homelabnext.modules.review_tier`) named only the two `review_tier.py` files, `gitbase.py`, `schema_check.py` and `ci.yml`, so a change to `modules/loader.py` (`parse_yaml`, `contributed_files`, `read_tree_file`), `swap_evidence.py`, `swap.py`, `rules.py`, `golden/pins.py`, `models/store.py` or `registry/models.py` could rewrite what the tiers decide at owner tier. All are human-approve now, and a test derives the list from the imports and fails when one is not listed (docs/modules.md, "Trust tiers").
- The module census no longer fails the whole fleet on an MCP server name the manifest and loader accept. The census (`homelabnext.modules.census`) and the role's reader (`mcp_client_configs.py`) refused an underscore and any name over 48 characters, but `platform/mcp_server` `instance` allows `_` and 64 characters, so a legitimate `example-lake_main` raised `CensusError` for every host. Both copies now share `MCP_NAME_RE` and `MCP_NAME_MAX = 64` (a test proves they agree, in text and behaviour), the draft `platform/module_inventory` gains an `mcp_name` definition for `mcp_servers`, and names are still only ever shown clipped, never config text.
- An IPv6 allow to `any` from a zone whose prefix is not global (unique-local or documentation) no longer reaches the WAN on the Linux router: `ipv6_form` now gives it the new `intranet` destination ("anything but the internet"), rendered in nftables as an outbound match on the zones' own interfaces (named positively, so a second uplink or a tunnel is never "not the WAN"); the controller renders the `any` rule in `LANv6_IN` preceded by a `WANv6_OUT` drop for the same source, since its legacy `LANv6_IN` also sees routed LAN-to-WAN traffic.
- The controller adapter now renders the `WANv6_OUT` drop for a zone denied the internet whose prefix is unique-local or documentation: `ipv6_form` gave a deny to the internet no IPv6 form from such a prefix, leaving the controller's default to decide. An internet allow from such a prefix still has none.
- `hnx guests render --target baseline-playbook` renders a valid no-op play (`localhost`, no facts, no tasks) instead of `[]` for a fleet with no baseline set or no control bound to a role; `ansible-playbook` refuses an empty list ("A playbook must contain at least one play").
- The `hnx_address` filter's docstring claimed to hold addresses to the guest model's own rules; it now says which it does not check (a zone gateway, a DHCP pool address and another zone's IPv6 prefix, none of which a row carries), and a test pins the gap.
- The census's MCP client configuration reader refuses a YAML complex key (a sequence or mapping used as a key) as a configuration that does not parse, naming only the error's type; it ended the census with an unhandled error before.
- A firewall rule naming a zone now reaches the zone's IPv6 hosts. A zone's `subnet6` (site 1.4) was never rendered: `zone:` sources and destinations in guest `firewall_rules` expanded to the IPv4 subnet only, so under default-deny egress (or the inbound drop) the zone's IPv6 traffic was silently refused. The `.fw` file now carries the rule once per address family (the IPv6 line right after the IPv4 one, ICMP as `ipv6-icmp`), and the inventory's `hnx_firewall` a second row of the same name with the prefix, which `nftables_host` writes as an `ip6` match (ICMP as `meta l4proto ipv6-icmp`), after the metadata refusal as before; the role accepts a rule address only as `any`, an IPv4 CIDR or a well-formed IPv6 prefix (a unique-local or global-unicast /48 to /64, `hnx_rule_prefix6_problems`, the `subnet6` rule; that it is a zone's is the generator's doing), so `10/8` or a zone id is refused. Egress validation judges a `zone:` destination over both families, the IPv6 prefix as an IPv4 subnet: a unique-local or documentation prefix is the lab's, a global-unicast one reaches the internet (so a model runtime may not allow it, unless the site declares the prefix its own; see the `delegated_prefixes6` entry above), and one holding a cloud metadata address is refused. A rule naming a zone whose `subnet6` does not read is refused. A CIDR and `any` are unchanged. The example zone `mgmt` and the compute test site's `mgmt` carry a documentation-range `subnet6`.

- A broker client reported a transport error instead of the broker's answer when the broker answered without reading (`busy`, an unknown caller) and the request arrived after its drain: the close then reaches the client as a reset after the answer. A whole answer line followed by a reset is now the answer; the relay-starvation test failed intermittently on this race.
- `tests/golden` has its own 90 s budget: in six CI shards its code-mode report fixture (module scope, about 13 s) runs in each shard that draws a test of its module, so the suite's summed time reached 61.0 s on 3.12, past the 60 s default, and `test-ok` failed with no test failing.
- The knowledge graph build no longer fails as a whole on one bad link: a wiki link or Markdown link whose target the system cannot even look up (a name over the path-length limit) raised `OSError` (ENAMETOOLONG) from `is_file()`/`exists()` and took every graph-backed command and `hnx mcp` tool down with it; such a target is now a dangling link like any other missing page, while a real I/O error such as a permission problem still fails the build (`homelabnext/graph/sources.py`).
- `hnx gen gateway provision` deletes the LiteLLM keys of profiles removed from the spec: a gateway spec's new `name` is its identity, `keys.json` carries it as `managed_by` and every key as `metadata.hnx-managed`, and after provisioning the declared keys `provision` deletes each key tagged with that name (over every `/key/list` page) whose alias is no longer declared. Keys of another gateway spec on the same proxy and untagged keys are never touched (untagged ones with undeclared aliases are listed for a one-time manual cleanup); a file that declares no keys refuses to prune and one that does not parse is refused; `--dry-run` writes nothing and `--keep-undeclared` opts out. `gen gateway --check` fails a key whose tag is not the spec's name and an alias declared twice. Previously a removed profile's key kept working.
- `hnx conform`: a MUST check that needs a product feature and a test hook is skipped when the product feature is missing, instead of being scored `untested` (a failure) on a target that has neither; a target with the product but not the hook is still `untested`. `Mutant` takes the `features` its factory supports, so a mutant may name a check that requires them (it runs with them declared).
- PR #38 follow-ups: the LiteLLM spend-log reader refuses a `timeout` outside 0 < t <= 600 when it is built, so `hnx orchestration turns --turn-source-option timeout=1e308` exits 2 at startup instead of printing an `OverflowError` traceback on every pass (and `-1` exits 2 at startup instead of on every pass); it converts `timeout`, `page_size` and `max_pages` to plain numbers and checks the converted value (a bool or a non-number is refused; a subclass that overrides its comparisons is judged by its value); a stray word in `docs/orchestration-telemetry.md` is gone.
- Data correctness review: a swap replays the writes parked for a component before a route change makes it primary (and refuses the flip while it cannot), a rollback starts the old component before restoring the route, and `start_old` fails while a write stays parked; parked writes keep their order and `mirror_status` retries them; a rollback that cannot complete stops the old component again, and `SwapRunner.rollback(abandon_parked=True)` drops only the refused writes (nothing while the old component is unreachable), written at once as `abandoned-parked` evidence (parked again if that write fails); `LakeBackend(pending_path=...)` keeps the parked queue in a locked, append-only, self-compacting journal (released on `close()`, on collection or as a context manager), and at start the swap direction and the old component's state follow the registry (an unreadable registry refuses to start); OpenProject listings send the status filter "all", so closed strategy records are no longer re-created on every sync and closed work items stay loaded (the fake applies OpenProject's open-only default); a reused intent name is refused before any transition is applied; a lake batch that commits nothing no longer rewinds the checkpoint; `IcebergLake` finds a stored uid at any time; `TrinoRestLake` keeps its INSERTs and content-key lookups under `max_query_length` (default 900,000) and keeps a bounded batch ledger (`ledger_size`, default 1000); `check-lost` reports deleted sidecar events and `.events/` directories; the event publisher prunes sent records older than `retain_s` (default 24 h, `--retain-hours` / `--publish-retain-hours`, at least 1 h) under one lock; JetStream `dead_letters()` skips an original that aged out and reads on from its cursor; register records, sidecar events and records notes refuse duplicate keys (an unhashable key is a YAML error, not a crash); lake validation range-checks `long` and rejects an int beyond float range in a `double`, however long; the Markdown projects backend writes journal intents before items and recovers them after a crash.
- `hnx scripts reality` (M-02) no longer needs `external_exec` entries for the fictional module examples' fleet_units specs: a new `fleet_unit_specs_exclude` setting skips `examples/modules/**` (whose code is named, not shipped), and the module contract tests validate those specs instead. The two allowlist entries that let them through as if they were product binaries are gone. An exclude glob that skips no spec is a stale entry, like every other allowlist entry.
- The StackStorm bridge survives bus errors (per-subject backoff, reconnect), stops fetching on SIGTERM, no longer stays `warn` after a dead letter, and a durable it creates starts one day back instead of skipping the backlog.
- The broker relay treats no responders as an outage, keeps its hold and per-event attempts across restarts and `--once` runs, quarantines unreadable outbox files, and `--skip` takes the relay lock; a standing stream whose `duplicate_window` is shorter than the client's is refused.
- `events/agent.job.failed` and `workflows/stackstorm` are 1.1 (frozen); a schema whose `$id` names a frozen version must match that copy.
- The wheel ships `conformance.protocols`, which the broker, the StackStorm bridge and the NATS, OpenBao and S3 adapters import at load; an installed `hnx` could not start `hnx broker serve` or `hnx workflows st2 bridge`.
- StackStorm hardening from review: rules pass each event URL-encoded and the runtime decodes it only in Python, so no event text is ever rendered as a template (an agent's proposal title could otherwise have reached StackStorm's unsandboxed Jinja); the records-inbox lane refuses template markers; `admit` and `step` take a pack and step id and load the pinned pack, its mode and its steps from the installed pack (a hand-run action cannot go live or act as a general broker proxy); packs pin their event source and rules and admit check it; the referenced job-submit handler is in the tree and digest-checked at render. The private leak check masks `ENC[...]` only inside SOPS files, accepts only the default or marker-pinned unencrypted suffix and refuses duplicate keys in sealed files; last-run records take their host from the directory they were shipped into and ignore future-dated runs; redaction also covers hex, offset base64 and other derived encodings; the broker re-indexes recent decisions on start.
