- Action broker review: the approver allowlist matches (issuer, subject) exactly (name variants of a listed approver no longer pass), and the hub no longer lower-cases front-door usernames; the broker dates records-inbox proposals itself and tags each file name per agent, so one agent cannot claim another's names; approve and deny records are indexed separately and refused requests are rate-limited per peer (`refusal_limit`, manifest schema 1.4), so a flood cannot slow the Approvals page or fill the disk; handler output redacts JSON-escaped, percent-encoded and base64 forms of a secret; the Approvals page compares Origin in scheme, host and port against `approvals.public_origin` (agents schema 1.3). Checklist P-03, P-10.
- Private overlay review: the self-hosted deploy runner must be ephemeral and ships a job-started hook that refuses anything but `deploy.yml` dispatched from `main`, and the workflow lint fails any other workflow naming it; the private leak check pins the sealed paths, requires every sealed leaf value to be well-formed SOPS ciphertext, flags a `.sops.yaml` that narrows them, requires vault files to be header plus hex, and fails on files it cannot scan; the vault password client refuses redirects (an OpenBao token could follow one to plain http) and revokes the AppRole token it mints; the `hnx_secret` lookup reads OpenBao KV v2 by name when `BAO_ADDR` is set and refuses names that collide; the deploy uses Tailscale workload identity instead of an OAuth secret; validation messages name the path and rule, never the value; the threat model states what the deploy still holds long-lived and the skeleton gains a CODEOWNERS. Checklist F-21.
- Contradictions introduced by the record-uid, project-management and maps work, found by a review sweep (22 findings): old `XX-NNN` tombstones carry into `aliases.yml` as `retired:` entries without a uid; renames are checked before any file moves and links are never nested; an open task now holds its project open, as an open step does; read-only `hnx projects` commands no longer create a projects directory; the OpenProject change feed emits one event per work-package version and never retries a PATCH that may have landed; the `projects` conformance suite runs against the recorded OpenProject fake as target `projects-openproject` and catches ten mutants; the IPv4 rule exempts only real four-part record numbers; record lists follow the doctrine's type order; checklist M-16 runs exactly what its row names; and ADR-0014/0015 are amended for uids.
- Review of PRs #21-#26 (28 findings). Hub: the Approvals page sends `Referrer-Policy: same-origin`, so browsers' decision POSTs carry a real Origin (they were all refused as `Origin: null`); expired approvals are offered again. Broker: pending rows report `approval_state` (an expired or consumed approval no longer looks approved); `run` consumes an approval only once the run will proceed; denials with over-long names are still audited; imported proposals keep the original requester in their name and same-name clashes are a conflict; the socket directory must be owned by the broker; one deadline per request and reserved relay slots; the handler's process group is killed before reaping; a secret cut at the output cap no longer leaks a prefix; secret arguments are stored and digested as an HMAC; an index of open requests keeps listings O(open). Roles: Suricata groups are tested on a staged copy before going live and a failed reload is retried; tailnet flows are derived from the network IR (protocol defaults and partial denies now match the firewall), checked by a property test; held Wazuh and Tailscale packages can be upgraded; apt signing keys must match pinned vendor fingerprints; Tailscale uses the Ubuntu repository on Ubuntu; GMP filters quote names with spaces and GMP passwords with `%` are escaped.
- Code review of batches 13-15: agent-activity ingest decides ownership from what is stored (no forged detail on another sender's event; a detail is written once), publishes only the store's redacted, de-duplicated summaries and can now publish them to the bus (`hnx status ingest --publish-to`), and keeps `has_detail` true only when a detail is kept; the Agentmetry trail reader detects rotation by file identity and skips over-long lines, and drains in one batch per poll; the transition decider skips unrelated or malformed inbox notes per intent, finishes an intent stranded between lanes, and refuses YAML 1.1 boolean keys (the intent field is now `retired_on`, note schema 1.2); `write_record` accepts a loaded record back; review migration compares reviews as a multiset; scoped `lessons_due` hides out-of-scope ids in reasons; the run-output lint flags `os.mkdir`, `io.open` and `codecs.open`; the registry refuses two secret references sharing one environment variable and two senders sharing a token; the lab's Uptime Kuma provisioner is idempotent.
- Code review of batches 5-13: graph `reviewed` tiers and pack `require_reviews` count only current, valid reviews (one evaluation shared with the register validation); knowledge subjects resolve through products and adapters to the services that run them and fail closed; a profile-scoped MCP server bounds every catalog tool, `invariants_status` reports the page's own verdict, and `related` accepts any spelling of a path; generators prune only directories they own; the lake setup cache is keyed by catalog and table DDL and an explicit `ensure_tables()` always runs; two placeholder records are two graph nodes; S3 list keys decode form-style (AWS, MinIO); status schemas refuse a trailing newline; the conformance adapters share one endpoint and credential helper and the plaintext rule.
- Status hub: a pushed observation dated in the future is clamped to the hub's clock (and the skew recorded), so it still goes stale; only the service that declares a push source may publish for it, and only into its own module; an agent-activity sender may only write its own sessions.
- Reviews: the content digest no longer covers the record's id, so allocating a number for an `LL-NEW` record keeps its reviews current; a requirement's `min` counts distinct reviewers, not reviews.
- Invariant X-06 never checked `det:` ids: it read a top-level `det_map.yml` under a `detections:` key instead of `detections/det_map.yml` `entries:`. `detections lint-ids` now skips test fixtures and runs in `make detections` and CI; `hnx docs systems check` (L-05) now runs in CI.
- `make test` selects what CI's test job selects (`-m "not live"`, the coverage-floor test deselected), so it no longer fails a green tree on the tools/tests time budget; `make check` runs `make coverage` (the Q-07 floor) as a prerequisite instead, as CI runs its coverage job. `tools/tests/test_make_matches_ci.py` holds the two selections equal.
- `hnx broker validate` reports a manifest that is not valid YAML (or not UTF-8) in one line with exit 2, as it already did for a missing file, instead of a `yaml.parser.ParserError` traceback.
- A file that cannot be read or parsed is exit 2 (bad input), as docs/interfaces.md states, in `hnx registry` (every subcommand), `hnx site`, `hnx swap`, `hnx status views check` and `hnx models store` (malformed JSON; a missing file already was); they returned 1, the code for a failed check, which an invalid but readable document still gets. The loaders raise `RegistryUnreadable`, `SiteUnreadable`, `RecordUnreadable`, `ViewsUnreadable` and `StoreUnreadable`, subclasses of the existing errors, so library callers are unaffected. `tests/cli/test_cli_input_errors.py` holds the sibling commands to one rule.
- `hnx guests` (every subcommand) exits 2 for a site file, guest directory, guest file or policies, nodes, keys or baselines file that cannot be read or parsed; it returned 1, the code for an invalid guest set. The loaders raise `ComputeUnreadable`, a `ComputeError` subclass. `plan --exit-code` exits 3 when there are changes, as `hnx site plan --exit-code` does; it exited 2, the code a mistyped path now gets, so a script reading 2 as "changes pending" would have acted on bad input.
- `hnx models serving render|check`, `hnx invariants run` and `hnx agent` (`compile`, `verify`, `pack`) exit 2 in one line for a spec, register, store, role table, library, policy or other input document that is missing or does not parse; they printed a `yaml` or `FileNotFoundError` traceback with exit 1, so a mistyped register path read as a failed fleet. `invariants run` also exits 2 when the previous results it reads back from `--out` do not parse (a full disk or a killed run truncates them), instead of a traceback and exit 1 on every run after. `hnx agent` used to document bad input as 1; an invalid but readable document still is.
- `tools/check_changelog.py` exits 2, not 1, when `homelabnext/_version.py` or CHANGELOG.md cannot be read or names no VERSION: misconfiguration, not a missing section.
- `hnx conform run` prints `COULD NOT MEASURE` instead of `FAIL` on the line of a capability whose subject could not be built (exit 100), as docs/conformance.md describes it: neither a pass nor a failure.
- `tools/check_spdx.py` (Q-09, `make spdx`, the pre-commit hook) also checks Jinja templates (`*.j2`); the 31 Ansible role templates without an SPDX header now carry `{# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0 -#}`, a comment that renders nothing (a test compares the template tokens with and without it). README and CONTRIBUTING name the suffixes the gate checks instead of claiming every source file.
- README said "Twelve architecture decision records" (there are 28 once ADR-0028 and ADR-0029 merged), the ADR index still called 0025 reserved after ADR-0025 merged, and docs/conformance.md cited a renamed test file; all three are corrected, and `tools/tests/test_doc_facts.py` now holds README's ADR and capability counts, the index's reserved numbers and every `tests/`, `tools/`, `conformance/` or `homelabnext/` Python file the docs name to the tree.
- `tools/coverage_floor.py` (Q-07) measures each unit by its own tests, as its docstring said: units are grouped by the tests they name and each group runs in its own pytest with coverage of its own units, Python subprocesses those tests start included (coverage's `patch = subprocess`); before, one run credited every unit with lines any other unit's tests reached. It also covers `homelabnext/status/approvals.py`, `status/serve.py`, `generators/safe.py` and `tools/leak_scan.py` (19 units, all at or above 80%). On a shared 4-CPU box the floor took 552 s against main's 458 s, measured back to back: about +20%, from the ~370 tests of the four new units (the per-group runs and subprocess tracing cost is within the noise). The CI coverage job's timeout is 20 minutes, and the `test` and `dev` extras pin `coverage>=7.10`, the first release that honours `patch = subprocess`.
- X-02, X-07 and X-08 were `evidence: plugin` criteria whose plugin reports skip everywhere, so `make invariants` counted them as run (`pass 44, skip 3`, exit 0); they are now deferred `evidence: server` criteria with a `why` (no check exists yet) and a written observable, so the runner neither runs nor counts them and the register page shows them deferred. This corrects the 0.1.0-era entry that called them exercised through the runner.
- Every PUBLIC-NOW checklist row now has a criterion in `examples/registers/invariants.yml`: 23 rows had none (F-15, F-22, O-05, O-06, O-07, O-09, O-10, O-11, P-10 to P-14, R-09, Q-01, Q-02, Q-03, Q-06, A-08 to A-11, A-13), although the checklist said PUBLIC-NOW rows are the ones the runner executes. each is an `auto` criterion running the row's own CLI gate or named tests (could-not-measure when a dependency is missing, failing on any skip). F-08, branch protection, is a setting of the hosted repository that no checkout can decide, so it is now a SERVER row (its CI half is Q-01 and Q-06) with a `manual`, deferred criterion giving a `why` and the `gh api` command a maintainer runs; PUBLIC-NOW has no exception. F-05 is split: "Secrets are not in git" (tree and full history) stays PUBLIC-NOW and the on-disk half is the new SERVER row F-24. Q-07 gets a 30-minute timeout (the runner default of 120 s could never fit the coverage floor), and the slow role and model criteria get theirs. `tests/invariants/test_inv_example_register.py` holds every PUBLIC-NOW row to a runnable criterion and every SERVER row to a non-runnable one.
- The reference event bus fake settled in-flight state by message instead of by delivery, so a stale delivery (one handed out before a reopen, or settled again after its redelivery was fetched) settled the live redelivery: a crashed process's late `ack` swallowed the new process's `nack` and the message was lost. The reference identity provider fake accepted a `nan` or `inf` ttl (an immortal token), and the reference secret store accepted one for a lease (`inf` never expired, `nan` was born expired). The reference identity provider took a bare string for the scope list character by character (`issue("u", "read", ttl)` granted `r`, `e`, `a`, `d`), and the reference event bus never dead-lettered a message whose consumer kept crashing before settling it.
- The OpenBao secrets adapter passed `version=0` to KV v2, which reads it as "latest": `get(path, version=0)` returned the newest secret. Versions below 1 are now refused as absent, and so is any version that is not an int (`int()` read `True` as 1, `1.9` as 1 and `"2"` as 2, and raised a raw `ValueError`/`OverflowError` for `nan`/`inf`), before any request. `lease(path, inf)` raised a raw `OverflowError` after the lease ACL policy was written; a ttl that is not finite is now a `LeaseError` before anything is written.
- Approval notes have one limit, 500 characters, read from `schemas/broker/approval`'s `note.maxLength` (`homelabnext.broker.state.APPROVAL_NOTE_MAX`). The hub's form and the broker transport took up to 2000, so a 501-2000 character note passed both and the store refused it: the approve or deny failed as `approval-invalid`.
- `hnx status render --private-out DIR` also writes the private `index.html` that the maps' and directory pages' menu anchors link back to. Before, those links pointed at a page that was only ever written to `--out`.
- `hnx status views check` refuses a shell or menu title that the release's `title` check would publish as `withheld` (for example `Agents: activity`), instead of letting it change silently in the public view.
- ADR-0013 records its amendments: the Approvals page is the hub's one write (ADR-0017 stage 2b), and the agent summary tier is stored at `red` and shown only through the release transforms (ADR-0033). It also names `COLLECTORS` in one place. docs/status.md no longer lists `host`, `project` and `model` as going to the public pages, and it says the bus carries the summary tier unreleased. The `/agents` and `/agent-analytics` rows no longer offer a `?project=` filter or a By-model count, which the release withdraws. ADR-0033 decision 6 says how the hub's route ceilings and the `red` default of `--notify-ceiling` meet "a reader with no declared ceiling is refused".
- The `unifi` adapter's rule sets contradicted themselves: it skipped a zone's internet allow ("the controller's default allows it") while ending `LAN_IN` in a default-deny with no destination, on its own premise that `LAN_IN` sees routed LAN-to-WAN traffic, so every zone's internet egress hit the deny. An internet allow now renders as a `LAN_IN` drop of what the zone sends to any zone's network (the new `hnx-zones` / `hnx-zones6` address groups, under `groups` in `firewall-rules.json`) followed by an accept, both ahead of the default-deny. Its rule indexes are bounded: a site whose rules would reach the default-deny at 2999 is an error instead of rules silently placed behind it. A mirror port with more than one source is refused by the adapter instead of rendered as its first source, and validation applies the one-source rule to every switch the `unifi` adapter renders, whatever the switch's `model`.
- `hnx site validate` checks that `router.model`, `adapters.network`, `adapters.dns` and a switch's `model` name an adapter of the right kind (`adapters.network: dnsmasq` used to validate and render no firewall), and refuses `icmp` listed beside ports in a `zone_to_zone` rule (the IR dropped it silently). `tailnet.deploy.owners` refuses a `group:`: the rendered policy defines no groups, and Tailscale refuses a policy naming one it does not define.
- `hnx guests render --out` never removed what it no longer rendered, so a removed guest's `vm-*.tf` stayed under terraform and an old `firewall/<vmid>.fw` applied to the next guest with that vmid. It now writes through the same writer as `hnx site render` (only files that differ), names stale generated files in a warning, and `--prune` deletes them (never a hand-written file).
- Nothing compared a guest's `network.ip` with its host address in `services.yml`, though the router, DNS and DHCP read one and the hypervisor and guest the other: `hnx guests validate` now refuses a registry host whose address or zone differs from its guest's (`--registry`, default the `services.yml` next to the site file). The check found `examples/minimal` drifted: its registry now gives `bus-core` the guest file's address.
- `hnx site plan` listed no object names for a created or deleted JSON payload (it compared against `{}`, which has no `objects` list).
- `hnx guests` read (and SOPS-decrypted) the site file six times per run; it now reads it once (`homelabnext.compute.zones.site_facts`).
- The four-box example put the unreachable detonation box and the sensor in `cap_siem`, the SIEM manager's group; neither is a SIEM manager.
- `make schemas` and `make ids-check` compare with a base only when `BASE=<rev>` is given, as their comments say: the `BASE ?= origin/main` written for `modules-review-tier` is global in make, so both always compared with `origin/main` and failed on a clone without it. The review-tier and swap targets default their base through `REVIEW_BASE` (`BASE` when given, else `origin/main`).
- `tools/dup_check.py` enforces that its baseline only shrinks against the base commit (`--base REV | --ci-base`, which CI's schemas job now passes), as `tools/product_name_lint.py` does: the check took its parameters and clones from the committed baseline, so a hand edit (`min_tokens: 100000`, `clones: {}`) turned the M-03 copy-paste gate off. Unless the baseline records an increase the base's does not (`--update-baseline --allow-increase REASON`), its parameters must be the base's and no clone may be new against the base's baseline; the base's recorded increases cannot be removed.
- Role lint RL012 covers `script` and `expect`: `script` runs its `cmd` through a shell and `expect` splits its `command` like one, so a templated value there is refused unless every expression ends in `| quote` (no Jinja statement); docs/role-interface.md says so. Before, only `shell`, `raw` and a free-form `command` were checked.
- The `hnx_registry` lookup refuses a row without the `host` or `port` its field needs (and a row that is not a mapping) with an `AnsibleLookupError` naming the service, instead of a bare `KeyError`; its docstring no longer limits `scheme` to `http|https|tcp` (the example registry uses `udp`, `ldaps` and `ssh`).
- `tools/check_spdx.py` exempts only expected outputs under a `golden/` directory: `*/golden/*` also exempted the `homelabnext/golden` package (including the governance code `pins.py`) and the `tests/golden` suite, so a Python or Go file there could lose its licence header unnoticed. The pre-commit hook excludes the same set.
- The 3.12 test shards now require what they install: `HNX_REQUIRE_MODULES` (new; `duckdb,pyarrow,pyiceberg,sqlalchemy,mcp,ansible` there) turns an `importorskip` of a listed module, or a submodule of one, into a failure, and `HNX_REQUIRE_TOOLS=ansible-playbook` does the same for the playbook suites. Before, a broken extra or a missing `ansible-playbook` turned whole suites into accepted skips. The tool skips go through one path: the new `@pytest.mark.requires_tool(...)` marker calls `require_tool()` before any fixture, every `skipif(shutil.which(...))` in the suite became that marker, and a test refuses the old form.
- The egress canary's object-store list (`canary.OBJECT_STORES`) names every store the `model-runtime-object-store-request` and `-lookup` detections match: it lacked `storage.cloud.google.com`, `myqcloud.com` and `myhuaweicloud.com`, so the canary passed a model the detections would flag. `tests/golden/test_egress_canary.py` holds the two lists to each other; the pinned evaluation report is regenerated (`make models-report`).
- The `wazuh` detection stub emits case-insensitive patterns as scoped `(?i:...)` groups: an alternation of bare `(?i)` patterns (`(?:(?i)a)|(?:(?i)b)`) is refused by Python's `re`. `hnx detections run` now also runs every fixture through the stub's compiled rules (`WazuhStubBackend.evaluate`, `runner.run_compiled`; a backend with an `evaluate(artifact, event)` method is driven this way) and fails on a must_fire or must_not_fire fixture whose compiled result is not the reference evaluator's, so "swap the engine = backend change + fixture gate" (ADR-0006) is checked against what the backend compiles. `detections/attack_coverage.json` gains `backends`, each such backend's own count from its compiled rules (`rules_counted`, `techniques_covered`, `must_fire` fired of total, `uncounted` naming a rule it cannot express); docs/detections.md.
- The realm sign-in test (`tests/generators/test_gen_golden.py`) also holds the flag-gated bindings: a `registrationFlow` or `resetCredentialsFlow` the render binds must be a rendered flow with a password and an OTP step on every path, so turning `registrationAllowed` or `resetPasswordAllowed` back on cannot open a way in without the second factor; it gains the `registrationAllowed` mutations and says that it reads only the rendered clients, not those the IdP creates on import.

### Security
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the private-overlay template's `HNX_REF` (the homelab-next commit `checks.yml` and `deploy.yml` check out into `hnx/` and the deploy runs with every deploy secret and root on every host) is the tracked file `locks/hnx-ref` (CODEOWNERS owns `locks/`), not an Actions variable, and both workflows refuse it unless it is one full SHA on homelab-next's `main` (`git merge-base --is-ancestor` against a freshly fetched `main`, with a full-history checkout, before anything from `hnx/` is installed or run). Before, any 40-hex value was accepted, so whoever could set the variable could point the deploy at an unmerged pull request's or a fork's commit, which GitHub serves under the parent's name. `checks.yml` gets an `hnx-pin` job the other jobs need and take the commit from. ADR-0021 (new decision 7), ADR-0028 and `docs/private-overlay.md` ("The pinned homelab-next commit") now agree on where `HNX_REF` lives and how it moves. An overlay adds `locks/hnx-ref`, takes up the new workflows and deletes its `HNX_REF` variable (`TODO.md`).
- The private-overlay template's `checks.yml` validates `lab/ssh-ca.yml` (`hnx ssh-ca check`) and `lab/secrets.yml` (`hnx secrets plan --spec`) on every pull request, each when present (new `lab-specs` job), so a broken spec shows before the operator applies it. The template's deploy SSH certificate role lives 1 h (was 30 m), at least the deploy job's 60-minute timeout, as its own comment requires; a test holds the two together.
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** the records decider (`homelabnext.records.decider`) no longer renames the inbox file into a decided lane, which kept the agent's inode (its owner and mode, and any hard link or open descriptor it held), so an agent could rewrite a note after it was approved and `complete()` acted on the rewrite. Every move now reads the note once through a descriptor opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`, refuses anything that is not a regular file with exactly one link (`LaneViolation`), validates those bytes, claims the note in the decider's own `decided/.claims/`, first its name (an `O_CREAT|O_EXCL` lock, `<name>.lock`) and then its entry (an atomic rename), so of an approve and a deny raced on one note exactly one wins and the loser fails with `FileNotFoundError` having filed nothing; it then writes the validated bytes to a new file in the destination lane (created by the decider's user) and the sidecar after it, each never replacing an existing name, and only then removes the claimed entry and the lock. The read's descriptor is held open until the move ends, so a note swapped after the read is recognised even where the filesystem would reuse the inode number. A move that fails after its claim removes only the files it created and puts the note back (never over a name that has reappeared: `renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE)` on Linux, best effort elsewhere), so a move stays all-or-nothing, and a source the decider may not move (a sticky inbox it does not own) is refused before anything is filed. `inbox/` and `decided/` must be on one mount: across mounts the claim is refused (`EXDEV`, "inbox/ and decided/ must be on one mount"); a crash mid-move leaves the lock in `decided/.claims/` and that name is refused until the operator removes it (the lock records the pid, host and UTC time of its move, the refusal says whether that pid still runs on this host, and `hnx records decide` lists what `decided/.claims/` holds when it refuses). A `Decider` holds the descriptor of each note it reads until that note is moved: a re-read releases the earlier one, `Decider.close()` (or `with Decider(...)`) releases the rest, and the register and strategy deciders release them after every intent; `complete()` reads the decider's copy, and each `related:` entry must be exactly `decided/approved/<file>.md`. A decided note and its sidecar are mode 0640 less the decider's umask (`Decider(mode=)`), no longer readable by every local user: agents read `decided/` through the group of the decider's lane directories (setgid on them keeps it), so a site whose agents are not in that group loses read access. A site that put symlinks or hard-linked notes into `inbox/` or `decided/approved/` now has them refused. The register and strategy deciders (`hnx registers decide`, `hnx strategy decide`) read each intent through the new `Decider.read`, the same load, so a symlinked or hard-linked intent is an `invalid` outcome before anything is applied (it used to be applied and then stuck in `inbox/` when the move refused it), and the bytes filed under `decided/completed/` are the ones decided on. A note with CRLF line endings is parsed as before. The guarantee needs the decider to run as its own OS user, as before.
- New operator command `hnx records decide approve|deny|complete NAME --root DIR --by WHO [--reason TEXT]` (`homelabnext.records.decider.main`, routed by the new `homelabnext/records/cli.py`): the documented route for reviewing generic proposal notes when migrating a vault proposal queue (docs/broker.md) had no runnable mover. Exit 0 moved (each new path printed), 1 refused (any OS error included, such as running it as a user that may not write the lane), 2 usage (an empty `--by` included). `hnx records assign-uids|numbers|uid` are unchanged.
- **Breaking for overlay sites:** `model_store` no longer takes a fine-tune export by path, never follows a link in one, and refuses an export that holds anything but its listed files. `exports/<run>/` is written by the trainers' group, and the root-run promotion chowned and chmodded it recursively with `ansible.builtin.file`'s default `follow: true`, so a link planted beside a legitimate export (`x -> /etc/shadow`, or `-> /`) made its target store-owned and group-readable, or stripped the whole filesystem; and since a trainer can rename any name in that directory, `exports/<run>` included, even a `follow: false` chown after a check could be redirected to a link swapped in between. Promotion now takes the export by descriptor (`model_store_files` `lock: true`): it opens `exports/<run>` with `O_NOFOLLOW` and closes it to all but the store owner (`fchown`, `fchmod 0700`) before listing it, then each directory below the same way, relative to its parent; the same walk refuses a link, a special file (device, FIFO, socket), a regular file that no manifest entry for that run lists, a listed file with another hard link (checked on the opened descriptor, so it does not depend on `fs.protected_hardlinks`), or an `exports/<run>` that is itself a link. With any of these no file is touched, no link's target is ever opened, chowned or chmodded, and the directories stay closed, store-owned, for an operator to clean. Otherwise each listed file is taken through its own `O_NOFOLLOW` descriptor and only then is every file verified by sha256, so a trainer can no longer open the export for writing once the check runs (a mismatch fails with the export already store-owned: the operator removes it). Residual risk: a write descriptor a trainer process already held open survives the ownership change; the vLLM unit's `ExecStartPre` sha256 check stays the last gate. Two export entries may share one run (the key is (source, run, quant)): each one's walk allows the files of every entry for that run, as `check_disk` does. The hub entries' ownership task sets `follow: false`, and `health` and `verify` walk exports the same way. An overlay whose promoted export holds files Studio wrote but no manifest entry lists (a README, a training log) must list them with their sha256 or remove them before the next run; a refused export's directories must then be removed or cleaned as root.
- A records note written through the `Records` library is owner-only (0600), as the broker's records lane already wrote it; it used to be created 0666 less the caller's umask, so under a zero umask any local user could rewrite a pending note.
- The consumers that act on an agent profile take only a `VerifiedProfile` (`homelabnext.agent`): `start_session` and `access_block`, `MemoryStore` (`apply_updates`, `apply_report`, `read`), `select_knowledge` and `build_keyring` refuse a dict with `UnverifiedProfile`, even one whose digest was recomputed after an edit (a widened `memory_scopes` or a changed `id` used to pass). A `VerifiedProfile` is made only by `verified_profile(profile, policy=, lock=)` or `load_verified_profile(path, policy, lock)`, which run `verify_profile` against the ceiling policy and the lock of reviewed digests; it cannot be constructed directly and is read-only. `hnx agent pack --profile` now needs `--policy` and `--lock`, and a keyring sources file that lists profiles needs `policy:` and `lock:` (`examples/keyring/policy.yml` and `lock.yml` for the example; docs/agent.md).
- The authority slots have one source, `homelabnext.modules.authority` (`AUTHORITY_SLOTS`, `GRANTED_UPSTREAMS`, `NEVER_SHARED` and the derived `SHARED_CONFIG_GRANTS`): the module and selection schema generator and the loader rules read it instead of keeping their own lists. The draft slot registry (`platform/slot`, additive) gains `authority` and `granted_upstream` flags, `examples/modules/slots.yml` flags the authority slots it holds, and `hnx modules check` reports a registry whose flags disagree with the list either way. As a consequence `remote-admin` is no longer an ordinary shared component: a module's `shared_config` into it needs `grants.shared_config` like edge and idp (the selection schema's grant enum gains `cap:remote-admin`).
- docs/status.md says first-sign-in OTP enrolment is trust on first use and how to provision people (a temporary password and enrolment in person, or a pre-enrolled credential); a test proves the sign-in redirect's return target keeps a query with `&` whole.
- The generated IdP realm also binds its direct grant flow (`directGrantFlow`: `direct-grant-validate-username`, `-password` and `-otp`, all `REQUIRED`), so `grant_type=password` (the IdP's built-in `admin-cli` allows it) needs a one-time code too and a user with no OTP gets no token; before, the IdP's built-in direct grant flow ran, whose OTP step is conditional, so an un-enrolled user got a token on a password alone. The realm test walks every user sign-in binding (browser, direct grant), and requires registration and credential reset off, no identity provider, no client flow override and no client with direct access grants (ADR-0034 decision 2).
- The edge's forward auth lets in only the groups of the service's own group-to-role table, not every signed-in realm user: each public `auth: idp` site imports `hnx_forward_auth` with the sorted union of its `roles` groups (URL-encoded, `,`-joined) and the snippet asks `/oauth2/auth?allowed_groups=<them>`, which the auth proxy answers with `403` for a signed-in user in none of them. The `caddy` generator refuses a public `auth: idp` service with no `roles` (an empty `allowed_groups` admits everyone) and a group name outside lower case and `-`; the example registry's `metrics` and `uptime` gained `roles: {viewer: [lab-viewers, platform-team]}`. Only a `401` is redirected to sign-in (a `403` is passed on, never looped back to sign-in), and its `rd` return target is `{http.request.uri_escaped}`, so a query with `&` survives. `tools/caddy_lint.py` refuses an import of the generated snippet without its argument (ADR-0034 decision 3).
- The generated IdP realm (`idp_realm`) has no browser sign-in without a second factor: the OTP form is `REQUIRED` right after the password form, with no `ALTERNATIVE` beside it, and the realm's `requiredActions` enable `CONFIGURE_TOTP` as a default action, so a user with no OTP enrols one before the sign-in completes (or, with the action disabled, is refused). The flow had OTP and WebAuthn as two `ALTERNATIVE`s, which left a user with neither configured to the IdP's handling of a subflow with nothing configured; WebAuthn is dropped from the flow (ADR-0034 decision 2).
- The status hub refuses an `agents.yml` `private.identity_header` or `groups_header` other than the platform's identity-header set: the edge strips and copies only those, so a hub reading another name would have trusted a header a client can send.
- The egress marker scan reads `TLP` followed by a label word after any run of white space and up to three other separators that are not letters or digits, or none (`TLP-AMBER`, `TLP_RED`, `TLP/AMBER`, `[TLP=RED]`, `TLP  -  RED`, `TLPRED`); white space is never counted against the three, so a column-padded or line-broken header (`TLP    RED`) is read as before, so a body quoting such a header can no longer leave at a looser claimed label (ADR-0033 decision 13; docs/modules.md, "Egress points").
- The log pipeline reads item TLP labels carried as a `tlp:` tag in `tags` (a string or a tag object's `name`), the `tlp` of an OCSF `osint` entry and a STIX TLP marking in `object_marking_refs`, besides `tlp`/`labels.tlp`, so a stricter carried label is no longer lost to the looser declaration (docs/log-pipeline.md; conformance check `tlp-stricter-claim-kept` covers every carrier, with a mutant).
- `homelabnext.lake.rows.row_from_event(event, schema)` is the event-to-row mapping: the row's `tlp` is the event's `labels.hnx_tlp`, never the event's own `tlp` field (which a by-name mapping would store, bypassing the relabelling), and an event without a canonical `hnx_tlp` is refused for a labelled table (docs/log-pipeline.md, homelabnext/lake/README.md).
- An agent can no longer expire a standing requirement through the strategy decider: `to: expired` applies only when the record's dated LTIOV is before the decider's today, whoever filed the intent (the policy has no review tier for an expiry because time decides it; docs/strategy.md, "RFIs from gaps").
- The register and strategy deciders fail closed without a review policy: with no `--policy` and no `review-policy.yml` in or beside the root, `hnx registers decide`, `hnx strategy decide` and `hnx strategy rfis --sweep` stop before deciding anything, instead of waiving every tier (`--no-policy`, also spelled `--no-review-policy`, is the explicit opt-out; `hnx registers build` and `hnx strategy check` refuse too, below). `hnx strategy decide`, `check` and `rfis --sweep` gain `--policy`.
- The event write path refuses every `rekey` event, `by: hnx:migrate` included: only the uid migration creates one, so a typed rekey can no longer carry a stale review, a human approve included, onto edited content.
- `hnx docs systems check` fails a page with text outside its `hnx:gen`/`hnx:todo` blocks, an unknown, missing or duplicated block, or a changed heading: the review digest covers only the `hnx:todo` blocks, so such text would have shipped under a review that never saw it (docs/systems.md).
- Model-role clients can carry credentials and reach roles through the gateway (ADR-0007 decisions 2 and 3; docs/model-serving.md): `homelabnext.registry.models.GatewayResolver` resolves `mdl:<role>` to the `llm-gateway` capability with the role as the model, `ModelClient(..., api_key=)` sends `Authorization: Bearer`, and `probe`/`probe_all`/`check_serving` take the backend key a keyed vLLM needs (it answered 401 to every keyless probe). `hnx golden run` now goes through the gateway by default (`--key-env VAR` names the key; `--direct` resolves the backend, host-side only), and `golden pins report` and `golden canary` take `--key-env` for the backend key.
- `hnx swap advance|rollback --now` (a forged clock passes the soak and rollback-window gates in seconds) needs `--rehearsal`, and every transition it writes carries the actor `<actor>@rehearsal-clock`. The runner refuses a clock earlier than the record's last transition, and `hnx swap record` reports a record whose transition times go backwards as invalid.
- The LLM gateway generator refuses a binding the spec puts on a `cloud` backend that the role table does not mark `leaves_lab: true`, and a `leaves_lab` binding on a local backend (docs/llm-gateway.md, "TLP labels on requests"). It used to cap such a role at `clear` for key scope while `homelabnext.registry.model_tlp.admit`, which reads only the role table, judged the role as staying in the lab (ceiling `red`) and allowed an `amber` request that the gateway then sent to the cloud provider. The role table is now the one source of a role's ceiling.

- The A-03 screener no longer leaves an IPv4 address in clear after `@` (`root@`, `git@`, URL userinfo), a comparison operator (`==`, `!=`, `>=`) or a word ending in `ver`/`oid` (`server`, `driver`, `avoid`). It reused the leak scanner's version context; it now has its own, narrower one (`shapes.SCREEN_VERSION_CONTEXT_RE`: `v`, `ver`, `version` or `OID` as whole words), and the eval set has cases for each shape.
- `start_session` checks the manifest against the profile (`homelabnext.agent.session_problems`): a profile with `reads_untrusted: false` is refused a manifest with `source: input` sections, and manifest `tools` and `memory_scopes` must be within the profile's MCP grants and memory scopes. Before, an actor could be handed raw untrusted input, and a pack could list tools its own access block denied.
- `hnx agent lint --policy` applies the ceiling policy's reader pin and, for profiles the policy has an entry for, its attestations (`data_tier`, `model_role`, `tlp_ceiling`, local-only and internet servers); before, a profile the policy pinned as a reader but that declared `reads_untrusted: false` passed G-03. `profile_problems` is now the one copy of those rules (`verify_profile` and the client render call it), and the G-03 observable runs with the example ceiling policy.
- `hnx mcp catalog render` refuses a relative `profile_file` for a server that reads `{profile_file}` (`hnx-catalog`'s `--scope-from-profile`), and writes the profile's resolved path when none is given; a relative one resolved in the client's working directory, where an agent could drop a wider locked profile.
- `actors-act-via-broker` (G-13) refuses an MCP server the catalog does not hold on its own, as it does an unknown capability or tier; it left the case to the catalog rule.
- The Code Mode runner treats a result schema its validator cannot use as `result-schema-mismatch` instead of passing the value unchecked.
- `hnx codemode mcp` reads a profile's search index with the runner's loader (no symlink, a regular file no other user may write), and the runner, like the search server, takes `<slug>/search.json` only as `profile:<slug>`'s.

- Module egress: a role with `internet` egress no longer reaches plain DNS (udp and tcp 53) outside the lab, as it already did not reach DNS over TLS or QUIC (853), so it resolves through the lab's resolvers as documented (logged, rate-limited, dropped before the internet accepts; the lab's own `dns` allows come first). The internet sets also exclude `scope.trusted.wan`, the lab's own public addresses, so an `internet` role cannot reach the port forwards behind them; a dynamic WAN source is refused by the render as by the scope reader (docs/modules.md, "Egress and publishing").
- A missing review policy fails closed for the registers, the strategy tree and the system pages, as it already did for the modules loader and the MCP catalog: `hnx registers build` and `review` refuse, `hnx registers decide` (and the strategy decider and sweep) stops before deciding anything instead of applying an intent with zero reviews, `hnx strategy check` fails (it only warned) and `hnx docs systems check` exits 2. `--no-review-policy`, also spelled `--no-policy` (every one of these commands takes both; `--policy none` for the system pages, `policy=False` in the library), is the explicit opt-out. The rule and its message live once, in `homelabnext.reviews` (`require_policy`, `NO_POLICY`) (docs/registers.md, "Reviews").
- A module's `python/` tree may hold only its own package, `hnx_modules/<id_>/`: the manifest rules (`homelabnext.modules.rules.code_tree_problems`) refuse another module's `hnx_modules/<other>/`, `homelabnext/`, any other top-level module, an `hnx_modules/__init__.py` (it would end the namespace package) and a symlink anywhere in it. docs/modules.md and the schema claimed this refusal; nothing did it.
- `hnx site review-tier` rates a change to a zone's `purpose` human-approve; it was owner ("a description"), but `purpose: management` opens SSH to the router from that zone (the `linux` adapter) and makes its mirrored traffic sensitive, so an owner-tier edit could open the router's SSH to the internet-ingress zone. `site.domain` is human-approve too (it was owner with the id and timezone): the lab's DNS answers that domain locally for every client and DHCP pushes it as the search domain, so renaming it to a public domain would resolve that domain inside the lab.
- `hnx modules review-tier` rates a change to a module's `requires.hosts` or `requires.scope` human-approve; both were owner, though `requires.hosts` decides which host roles the module's roles run on as root (and their exposure: moving a role onto the internet-facing host role) and `requires.scope` the scope parts it may act on.
- Module egress: a role granted `internet` no longer reaches a lab host in a global zone prefix whose address is not listed (a guest's SLAAC address). `hnx modules render --network FILE` (and `egress.render(network=)`) adds every zone `subnet6` and `delegated_prefixes6` prefix to the never-internet set; without it the render notes that unlisted lab IPv6 is treated as the internet. The IPv6 internet accept now also requires `2000::/3`, as docs/modules.md promised.
- A change to the model role table or to a swap record is now human-approve, and the swap gate ties a record to what changed (S4). `hnx modules review-tier` rates `examples/model_roles.json` and `examples/swaps/` human-approve and `hnx site review-tier` the overlay's `lab/model_roles.json` and `lab/swaps/` (the overlay `CODEOWNERS` template names both), so dropping a binding's `leaves_lab` no longer reaches `main` without a code owner. `hnx modules swap-gate` compared roles by pin only, so a dropped `leaves_lab`, a changed `fallback` or any other non-pin field was covered by any record; it now compares each role field by field and requires a record to name every such role in the new optional `model_role_changes` of `platform/module_swap`, refusing a name whose role did not change (docs/modules.md, "Swap record 1.2").
- `hnx site validate` refuses a `zone_to_zone` allow to `any` from a zone whose `egress.internet` is `deny`. `any` includes the internet, and the `linux` adapter renders such an allow with no outbound-interface match ahead of the zone's internet rule, so the zone reached the internet while `egress.internet: deny` (and the model-runtime check that trusts it) said it could not; the `unifi` adapter kept the deny, so the two disagreed on the same plan.
- `lab_table_namespaces` is checked more strictly. The loader now refuses a namespace that is a module's even when that module was read and failed its own validation; the refusal is reported beside the module's own problem, including when the module is selected. In `hnx site review-tier` a `null` list fails closed at either end (unchanged too), whatever the schema says of it, instead of reading as empty (which made dropping every namespace look like an owner-tier removal).
- An IPv6 zone id (`%scope`) no longer passes as part of an address or a prefix. Python parses `2001:db8::5%eth0` as an address and `2001:db8:0:82::%eth0/64` as a prefix and writes both back unchanged, so the canonical-form checks let them through: a zone's `subnet6` with one was accepted on the compute path (which never runs the site schema) and held guests' IPv6 extra addresses; the sensor role's `hnx_enroll_address_problems` filter accepted one in `ip`, an extra address or `subnet6` (and the address with it compares unequal to the one without, so one address could be named twice); and the scope guard resolved `2001:db8::82%eth0` to no target, so under `unknown: allow` it reached an out-of-scope guest. `subnet6_problem`, guest validation and the filter now refuse a `%`, and the scope guard resolves the address without its zone id. `tests/roles/test_hnx_address_parity.py` runs one corpus of prefixes and addresses through the filter and the guest model and fails on any difference, so the filter's standard-library copy of the rules cannot drift looser.
- Physical model runtimes are held to default-deny egress (docs/compute-model.md, "Model runtimes"). A `kind: host` guest with `cap:inference` or `cap:codemode-executor` must resolve `egress-filter` through its baselines (the set must define it; no baseline exemption or `filtered_by` pointer stands in for it), have `firewall_rules.policy_out` DROP or REJECT, and name only lab destinations in `outbound_allow`; the metadata refusal applies as on any guest. A host may now carry `firewall_rules` when its baselines resolve `host-firewall` or `egress-filter`: they reach its own `nftables_host` ruleset through `hnx_firewall`, never a hypervisor `.fw` file. `model_store` refuses to fetch from the public hub on a default-deny host without `model_store_hf_endpoint` (an https endpoint `outbound_allow` must reach; the public hub's own hosts are refused). On any guest or host that needs `egress-filter`, `nftables_host` now holds forwarded (container) traffic to the same `outbound_allow`: the forward chain's policy is drop, with established flows, published ports (`ct status dnat`) and traffic between the bridges in `nftables_host_forward_local` accepted, and its `health` hook fails unless forward is drop too. Before this a containerised executor on such a host reached any address but the metadata ones. A host that carries `firewall_rules` is now held to its `policy_out` when another guest's `filtered_by` names it, and validation reads only DROP and REJECT as default-deny. Tests cover a fixture host (`tests/compute/physical/gpu-1.yml`) end to end, through the inventory into the role and the real `nft -c`.
- The fake OpenAI-compatible inference server (`conformance/fakes/openai_server.py`), which the Code Mode golden suites run behind, refuses to listen on a host other than loopback unless `--allow-any-host` is given (the lab-in-a-box container passes it), and answers a POST whose Content-Length is malformed (400) or over 4 MiB (413) without reading the body. It has no authentication, so it stays small and local.
- Agents act only through the broker (ADR-0030, proposed; `docs/agent.md` "Actors act only through the broker", checklist G-13). A new rule `actors-act-via-broker` beside the reader rule applies to every profile, reader or not, and to every context manifest's tools: `tool_classes.yml` lists `broker_only: [exec, file-write, send, credential]` (a `--classes` table may list more, never fewer, and may not class a packaged capability or tier without one of its packaged classes); `send` is a new class for chat, mail, notifications and HTTP requests (reading the web stays `egress`). A granted MCP server with an acting tool must be the broker's own front (`acts_via: broker` on the record that is structurally `hnx-broker`: first-party `homelabnext/broker/mcp_tools.py`, launched as `hnx broker-mcp`, exactly the tools it registers, with current reviews), or a direct exception (`acts_via: direct` with a `direct_reason`, a current human approve review, and the ceiling policy's new per-profile `direct_mcp` entry); every capability is judged failing closed (the table classes every capability the registry, the conformance contracts, `capabilities.yml` and the module slots name; an unclassed one is refused), and none acts but `cap:broker`; an `execute` or `privileged` tier only with `cap:broker`. The compiler (`BrokerViolation`), `verify`, `hnx agent lint` (`--rule`, `--policy`, one walk for both rules, a note for a `direct_mcp` entry that excepts nothing), `hnx mcp catalog check`, the client render and the module rules enforce it (a grant of a server whose record is below its class floor is refused by all of them, not only by the catalog check); a module contributes no acting MCP record, no `acts_via` and no acting grant to its own agent (ADR-0027). `platform/mcp_server` 1.1 adds `acts_via`, `direct_reason` and the class `send`; the catalog check requires `acts_via` on every active or deprecated server with an acting tool, refuses `acts_via: broker` on anything but the broker's front, refuses `approval: true` on an acting tool unless the server is that front (only the broker grants an approval; the schema no longer says a client may), and floors tool classes in code (known acting software by what it launches, its package, image, bins, repo or provider; a tool's first verb, camelCase split, when it acts or writes, and a read verb first is a read; `record-write` only on a record that fronts a capability the table classes `record-write`; a module's records the same). `tools/schema_check.py` now judges `dependentSchemas`, `dependentRequired` and a new `anyOf`/`oneOf` instead of accepting them. Catalog: `hnx-broker` is `acts_via: broker`; `ssh`, `shell`, `ansible`, `kubernetes`, `vault`, `filesystem`, `git`, `github`, `gitlab`, `slack`, `email`, `smtp`, `ntfy` and `http` are `acts_via: direct`, `approval: false`, and ship as unreviewed drafts (no example carries an approval for them); `bash`, `exec` and `terminal` are retired (replaced by `hnx-broker`); `agent-runtime` drops `submit_job` (a job is submitted only through the broker action `agent.job.submit`); `notes` is a client of the records API (`record-write` honestly); the llm-orchestrator catalog renders no kind for a direct acting server no rendered profile is granted. CODEOWNERS names the MCP catalog, the ceiling policies and the class table. The example actor profile holds `hnx-broker` instead of `shell` (keyring rebuilt). G-03 runs `hnx agent lint --rule readers-cannot-act`, G-13 `--rule actors-act-via-broker` with the ceiling policy, and G-04's observable runs the broker rule before its tests. **Upgrade:** an overlay whose profiles, ceiling policy or render specs grant a direct acting server (a shell, SSH, Ansible, Kubernetes, a secret store, a file or repository writer, a chat, mail or notification sender, a generic HTTP client), an acting capability (`cap:automation`, `cap:agent-runtime`, `cap:records-db`, `cap:dns`, `cap:notify`...) or a capability the table does not class now fails lint, verify, compile and render, and its catalog records fail `hnx mcp catalog check` until every acting server declares `acts_via`, only the broker's front says `broker` and keeps `approval: true`, and tool classes are at or above their floor; an orchestrator profile granting such a server fails the llm-orchestrator render's `check_profiles`; a lab's invariants register passes its own ceiling policy to G-13. Replace the grant with `hnx-broker`, `cap:broker` and a broker action, or make a reviewed exception (record `acts_via: direct` and `direct_reason`, a human approve review, the policy's `direct_mcp`), or class an overlay capability in a `--classes` table.
- The edge's forward-auth protection is enforced, not only generated. Every Caddy pin is 2.11.7 (2.11.2 is the first release whose `forward_auth` drops a client-sent identity header the IdP omits; the examples, schema fixtures and lab registries pinned 2.8.4). The generated `hnx_forward_auth` snippet deletes every client-sent header whose name contains `authentik` (`request_header -*authentik*`, any case and separator) where it deleted `X-Authentik-*` and `X_Authentik_*`: a CGI-style upstream folds the mixed `X-Authentik_Groups` into the real header too, and Caddy 2.11.1 passed it through both prefixes. Every generated site that imports the snippet also runs `request_header -*authentik*` first, at site level: Caddy sorts it before every `handle` and `route`, so a `handle /api/*` added beside the import, or a matcher on the auth route, no longer reaches the service with a client's identity headers (it did, live, on 2.11.7). `tools/caddy_lint.py` (F-23; CI schemas job, `make caddy-lint`, part of `make check`) reads every Caddy config `git ls-files` lists (Caddyfiles, their templates and imports, markdown fences of any kind, Caddyfiles embedded in YAML, Caddy JSON/YAML, and the `caddy` generator's output for every registry that loads) and fails when, in a config with a `forward_auth` anywhere, a site that runs it or proxies, whatever it dials, lacks that matcherless site-level strip (the IdP's own site excepted: the same app under another name or host cannot reach a client's identity headers either); when a `forward_auth` is not directly in a `route`, after a delete (site level or in the route) of each header its `copy_headers` sets in every `-`/`_` spelling, or has a matcher; when a header directive sets, adds or replaces a header named after `authentik` or a copied name, or copies one through a header placeholder (site addresses, upstreams and matchers are not judged; snippets are judged where imported); when a `request_header`/`header_up` with a `*` field rewrites headers from a placeholder value; when a FastCGI `env` sets `HTTP_X_AUTHENTIK_*`, `REMOTE_USER`, `AUTH_*` or any variable from a client-chosen value; when a `forward_auth` dial (or any proxy dial in a forward-auth site) is a request placeholder (`dial`); on what it cannot read (a heredoc, a multi-line quoted token, a brace sharing a line, `{$ENV}` or a template in directive position, a header name that is not literal, an import it cannot follow or whose arguments it cannot substitute, a global `order` option, a `forward_auth` in a file it does not read as a config); when the generator fails on a registry that loads; and when any Caddy pin is below 2.11.2 by semver (registry `impl: caddy`, `caddy_*version`/`_tag`/`_release` keys, any image whose last path segment is `caddy` behind any registry prefix, Dockerfile `FROM`, `COPY --from`, `RUN --mount=from=` and `xcaddy build` in shell or exec form with `ARG`/`ENV` defaults resolved, `caddy=` packages, `go.mod`, `.env`, a Python registry; a pin it cannot resolve fails, and an image built on Caddy needs a reviewed allow). With a `caddy` binary (`--caddy`, `$HNX_CADDY` or PATH) it also runs `caddy adapt` on every Caddyfile and judges Caddy's own JSON: every `reverse_proxy` a request can reach in a forward-auth server must be reached with the identity headers deleted on every path, and only the auth answer may set one. CI installs Caddy 2.11.7 (version and SHA-512 pinned from the release checksums file) and requires it (`HNX_CADDY_REQUIRED=1`); a local run without one says so and relies on the text rules, which do not see the handler order Caddy builds beyond the directive order they model, nor what a plugin directive does. An allowlist entry names one file (or a glob that names no Caddy config) and the rules it excuses. With the semantic check required, a Caddyfile it cannot read (a template) is a finding. The lint's threat model is accidental misconfiguration of the tree's Caddy configs: their authors are trusted and reviewed, and deliberate obfuscation, caddy-docker-proxy labels (a `caddy.*forward_auth` label is a coverage finding), the operator's environment (a `{$VAR}` is assumed to expand to one token), unknown plugins outside a forward-auth server, configs deployed from outside the tree and services published by another proxy are out of scope; that every human-facing service is reachable only through the authed front door is the separate front-door work's job (Rule 1). To hand the IdP's answer on under another header name, `copy_headers X-Authentik-Username>Remote-User` is the supported form (the lint requires every spelling of the target stripped); a `header_up` or FastCGI `env HTTP_*` copy is refused, and only a FastCGI `env` of `REMOTE_USER`, `AUTH_USER` or `REDIRECT_REMOTE_USER` may take it, after the site-level strip (an allowlist: no request header sets those three, while PHP rebuilds `PHP_AUTH_USER`, `PHP_AUTH_PW` and `PHP_AUTH_DIGEST` from the client's `Authorization` header, live under php-fpm and php-cgi, so `env PHP_AUTH_USER {...}` and every other name are refused). The env form leaves the client's own `Remote-User` reaching the app as `HTTP_REMOTE_USER`; for an app that falls back to that header when `REMOTE_USER` is empty, also strip `Remote-User` and `Remote_User`, or use `copy_headers X-Authentik-Username>Remote-User` with `env REMOTE_USER {http.request.header.Remote-User}`. Site files imported by a main Caddyfile see its snippets, in-tree glob imports are expanded (each match sees the snippets of the matches before it), and an imported file is judged where it is imported. A missing dependency exits 2, which the F-23 criterion reports as could-not-measure.
- Broker and hub hardening from review: quotas on accepted work (manifest 1.5: `quotas.pending_per_actor`, default 20, a submit past it refused `too-many-pending`; an actor's approval requests page while it has no open page and at most once per `quotas.notify_window_s` (default 300 s; a page it holds back is sent when it passes; a page is marked open only once the notifier sent it; when the paged request settles and others wait, the oldest is paged, naming every waiting id; a cancel by the owner closes its page quietly; an approval that expires unused pages again; each agent's messages draw on its own notifier budget, `Notifier.report(scope=)`); a lane's `max_files` and `max_total_bytes`, default 4096 files and 64 MiB per lane directory, refused `lane-quota-exceeded` under a directory `flock`); an action is audited before it executes (audit 1.2 outcome `started`; refused `audit-unavailable` when that record cannot be written, `audit-incomplete` when the outcome record fails after it ran; a run that can record nothing, or whose process died, reads `interrupted`, never `running` forever); every refusal counts for the socket's rate limit, `not-pending` included; `hnx status ingest` times out a request after 30 s and both hub listeners cap concurrent connections (ingest 32, console 64; more answered 503 without a thread); a `Content-Length` that is not ASCII digits is refused 411 and logged by the decide and ingest handlers (it crashed them, unlogged); agent-activity retention also runs on reads (at most once a minute; skipped, never waited for, while a writer holds the lock) and on a timer in the long-running listeners. Manifest 1.6: a shared lane gives each actor a share (`max_files_per_actor`, `max_bytes_per_actor`, default an eighth of the lane) and a lane a per-actor `max_writes_per_hour` (default 120 on a shared lane; refused `lane-rate-exceeded`), so one agent cannot lock every other out of the proposals inbox; `max_files: null` for an append-only lane (the production example's `findings`: no file cap, 1 GiB, 20 writes an hour); `hnx broker validate --data-root` and `serve` show each lane directory's usage and warn at 80%, and the first `lane-quota-exceeded` per actor and lane notifies the operator once, and "cleared" only once the directory is back under 80% of its quotas, 15 minutes apart at least; usage rows show each agent's share of a shared lane; overlapping lane roots are refused. `hnx broker serve --notify-topic` pages through ntfy, its token a `--notify-token secret:NAME` reference (without it, a lane-quota refusal is logged at WARNING once per agent and lane). A run claim names its broker instance, whose `<state>/live/<id>` lock tells a live claimant from a gone one in any PID namespace. An I/O error during a run is an error outcome (`handler-io-error`) and its result, never `running` forever, and a filed proposal whose event cannot be queued stays filed (audited `outbox-failed`). The ingest bounds a whole request (a deadline that shuts the socket) and one client address to 4 connections (`--max-per-client`). Run claims record the pid's start time, the boot id, the PID namespace and the host. The broker's own faults (`secret-unavailable`, `handler-spawn-failed`, `handler-timeout`, `handler-io-error`, `lane-io-error`) no longer count toward the refusal rate limit. The hub's listeners and the broker socket listen with a backlog of 128. The generated Caddy forward-auth snippet deletes every client-sent `X-Authentik-*` header before `forward_auth` (Caddy 2.9.0 to 2.11.1 keep one the IdP omits), and every `X_Authentik_*` one (Caddy up to 2.11.1 passes those, and CGI-style upstreams fold them into the real header). `hnx status ingest --max-per-client` below 1 is refused. What an approval page is due for is derived from state: every agent with a request awaiting approval and no open page is paged as its floor allows, at start (a restart loses no page), by a monotonic-clock timer and on every submit, settle and listing; a page that could not be sent is retried a minute later; a held lane-quota "cleared" is retried on the same path; a settled request's page is dropped from the notifier's store (`Notifier.forget`). `serve`'s notifier caps all agents together at 60 an hour as well as 20 per agent, checks its endpoint and token at start (refusing plaintext with a token), logs failed sends, and takes its topic from the environment or a file. A broker instance's lock file is locked before it can be swept.
- A `tls://` events endpoint does the TLS handshake before anything else (`tls_handshake_first`; the server needs `tls { handshake_first: true }`, nats-server 2.10.3 or later), so a server that never asks for TLS can no longer receive the bus password in plain text.
- A03 runs a request only when the broker's status says a person approved it (`approver_kind: human` on each approval); an event's `tier_used` is advisory.
- The StackStorm bridge ignores `HTTP(S)_PROXY`, creates its durables only through the filtered `CONSUMER.CREATE.<stream>.<durable>.<filter>` subject (lab ACL updated), and only the controller's user may publish `agent.job.>`; the lab's conformance account has JetStream limits.
- Conformance false passes closed: broker per-command allowlist, records completion only of approved notes, idp forgeries on JWT-shaped tokens (alg=none, empty or tampered signature) and expiry without clock control, lake-query injection payloads and verbatim round trip, secrets leak scan over the cause chain and traceback, events redelivery and object-store key traversal. A `Skip` counts only from the suite itself, a MUST needing an undeclared test hook is `untested` (a failure), `run` enforces `MIN_CHECKS`, unknown `--check` ids fail, one mutant per gap, and X-10 reads the report from `--out`. Suites load only from a verified checkout or an explicit `--root`.
- Generator injection: service schema 2.0 restricts every interpolated free-text field to one line (patterns reject a trailing newline), every renderer refuses control and format metacharacters (`homelabnext/generators/safe.py`), and the Authentik blueprint is emitted as data. fleet_units refuses control characters, hardens units by default and retires units to `/bin/false`; Terraform strings escape `${`/`%{`.
- Safer defaults: guest schema 1.2 `cloudinit.sudo` (no sudo rule by default; `NOPASSWD:ALL` is opt-in), Proxmox firewall files named by vmid with a datacenter-enable check and optional default-deny egress, libvirt refuses VLANs and NIC firewalls it cannot honour, `ansible.cfg` checks host keys, `compose_app` keeps secrets in 0600 env files with `no_log` and refuses privileged containers, docker.sock and all-interface ports unless explicitly allowed, the example unit runs sandboxed as a non-root user, and the lab-in-a-box refuses a non-loopback bind while credentials are the published placeholders.
- Broker approvals: `expires_at` and a default TTL are enforced, future-dated and replayed approvals are refused, approvers come from a manifest allowlist with issuer-qualified, normalised identities (broker manifest and decision 1.1).
- Swap records cross-check step state against the transition log, hash a state digest into each transition, and support an HMAC key and an external head anchor (swap record 1.1). Context packs fence volatile sections with a nonce; profile ceilings come from a separately owned policy and digests can be pinned in a lockfile; a service can only narrow its host's scope; the HTTP clients refuse credentials over plain http, cross-origin redirects and unbounded reads; repository tools and SQL table names are verified before use.
- Supply chain: the leak scanner scans tracked files, fails on files it cannot read, no longer lets `scrub-allow` waive secrets, detects public addresses, compressed IPv6 and keyed literals, and can scan history and commit identities; wheels and sdists contain only tracked files and no symlinks; gitleaks allowances are value-scoped; CI pins actions and hashed dependencies, drops persisted credentials, and adds DCO, dependency review, pip-audit, CodeQL, Scorecard, Dependabot and a release workflow with provenance, SBOM and trusted publishing; register ids may be committed as `XX-NEW` and are checked in CI.
- A lean agent profile (`platform/agent_profile` 1.1, which now declares `reads_untrusted`) can no longer switch off the reader rule its ceiling policy forces: `hnx mcp catalog render` refused a reader holding a shell, exec or egress server only until the file said `reads_untrusted: false`; it now refuses that flag when the policy marks the profile a reader and judges the profile as one. Typing `reads_untrusted` as a boolean (an open key in 1.0) is strictly a tightening under docs/interfaces.md §6; it ships as a minor version because `profile_problems` already refused any non-boolean value.
- Handoff action parameters (G-04): `is_constrained` no longer accepts schemas that admit free text: an array with `prefixItems` (outside `items`), an object with `patternProperties` (keys that bypass `additionalProperties: false`), any `$ref` or combinator, and a "closed" pattern whose class lists everything (`[ -~]`, `[\u0000-\U0010FFFF]`), matches whitespace or has no length bound; such fields reached broker request params from a reader. A closed pattern is now read with Python's own parser, anchors and top-level alternation included (a `(?#()` comment hid a top-level `|` from the old text scan; comments are refused): every literal and alternative (not only class members) must be an ASCII letter, digit or `-_.:/@+=,~%` (`(?:[a-z]|;|\$)` spelled a shell class), `\w` and `\d` count as wide (they match Unicode), inline flags such as `(?i:[a-z])` are refused, and so is a pattern whose match can start with `-` (an option to an argv-fed handler); a `.` inside a class is a dot. A pattern's length bound is read on the parsed tree too: without a `maxLength`, every repeat needs an upper bound of at most 4096.
- Context packs and prompt templates: a fake `instructions [stable]` header in untrusted text is escaped on every line boundary (CR, VT, FF, NEL, U+2028, U+2029, not only LF) and behind zero-width or BOM prefixes; a line is read as a reader sees it (NFKC-folded, with format characters, combining marks and blank fillers such as U+3164 and U+2800 dropped throughout), so a header behind a combining mark, with a zero-width space inside the delimiter or in full-width forms is escaped too; the template lint and the injection eval's fake model read lines the same way, and the corpus (version 2) has eight cases for it.
- The agent-activity hook (`hooks/claude_code_activity.py --post`) follows no redirect (urllib copied the bearer token to whatever host a 302 named) and sends its token only to an `https://` URL, or plain `http://` to a loopback address, never through an `http_proxy` from the environment.
- The shared screener (context packs, the activity hook's spool tier) redacts credential shapes it missed: a quoted password-style value with spaces (JSON, YAML), `DB_PASS=`/`_PW=` style keys, `Authorization:` credentials of every scheme (Basic, Digest, Token, short Bearer), `Cookie:`/`Set-Cookie:` values and IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses as one address; matching also runs on a view without zero-width and other format characters and with Unicode digits folded, and the redaction is the union of both passes (folding alone glued a value to its neighbour and missed it). Every rule is linear in the text: the key=value rules backtracked cubically over a crafted run such as `token.token...` (seconds at 2 KB, hours at the hook's 64 KB field limit). Word boundaries are ASCII, so a value glued to a CJK, Thai or Cyrillic letter is redacted (Python's Unicode `\w` and `\b` saw no boundary there); a dotted quad glued to such letters is now redacted as an address too. A value of any type overlapped by a higher-priority span keeps its own label for the rest (a spaced card number after `password=` left twelve digits in clear), screening is idempotent (only a label the screener could have written, a known type and a count, is taken as already screened), a keyed value that ends in the next key (`pw=secret: <value>`) no longer hides that key's value, and merged spans of one type keep the plain text's label.
- `hnx mcp catalog check` refuses a hosted server URL (and a lab server's `transport.path`) whose path could carry a credential (anything but an MCP path or lower-case words and versions, the importer's rule), and render refuses one too: a key in `/mcp/<key>/sse` was written verbatim into the committed client config. A host label that looks like a credential (any label, a single-label host included: `<key>.mcp.vendor.example`, `<key>.example`; an IDN `xn--` label is judged in its decoded form, and as written when it does not decode) is refused the same way, and the importer does not copy it.
- MCP catalog check and import treat `--user user:password` and `--user=user:password` (and `--username`, `--proxy-user`, an attached `-uuser:password`, and httpie's `-a user:password` with its attached forms; short flags are case-sensitive, and after `-a` a listen address on an IP or `localhost` and a path whose host is dotted, an IP, `localhost` or a drive letter are not credentials) as credentials, like `-u`; the importer kept the literal in the draft's args.
- `hnx mcp serve --scope-from-profile` requires `--policy` and `--lock` and verifies the profile against them (catalog check refuses a record that passes the profile without both); verified only against the ceiling stored in the file, a profile rewritten with a recomputed digest widened the server's scope.
- No MCP record or grant may set `SSLKEYLOGFILE` (`DENIED_ENV`): Python's ssl, curl, NSS and Node write every TLS session's keys to the file it names.
- The lab-in-a-box bind guard judges what docker compose resolves: `bind_guard.sh` refuses a `.env` (or `.env.example`) line that is not a plain `KEY=value` (compose also applies `export KEY=v`, `KEY = v`, an indented line and `KEY: v`, which the guard skipped, so it read the loopback default while compose published `0.0.0.0`), then reads `docker compose config` (every profile, the same `--env-file` arguments as `make lab-up` and `smoke.sh`) and `config --environment`: a published port off loopback or a host-network service needs the acknowledgement and credentials that differ from what compose resolves from `.env.example` alone. It exits 3, never 0, when it cannot resolve the configuration (no docker compose or python3, or config fails); `smoke.sh` passes that code on.
- `compose_app` no longer lets a non-canonical bind source past its host-root and docker-socket rule: the rule matched the text, while compose passes `/.`, `//`, `/./`, `/var//lib/docker`, `/var/lib/docker/` and `..` paths through unchanged and the kernel reads them as `/` and docker's state, so every source must now be canonical. The rule also covers the containerd, podman and CRI-O sockets and runtime directories, a rootless `/run/user/<uid>` runtime directory, `/var/lib/containerd`, `/var/lib/containers` and `/var` itself; role lint RL014 flags a containerd, podman or CRI-O socket like a docker one.
- `compose_app` refuses root however it is spelled: the explicit-root check compared the user part with `0` and `root` as text, so `user: "00"` or `"000:0"` (which the runtime reads as uid 0) passed; a user or group that is `root` or all zeros (`00`, `000:0`, `1000:0`, `root:root`) now needs `compose_app_allow_root`.
- `private_leak_check public` can no longer be switched off by the change it checks, and finds SOPS files by content: `lab/` and `ansible/inventory/production/` are pinned in the tool (the skeleton marker may add paths; one that drops a pinned path is a `private-paths` finding), where before deleting or editing `examples/private-overlay/.hnx-private-overlay.yml` in the same pull request emptied the list; and every file that reads as text is checked for SOPS ciphertext, not only `.yml`, `.json`, `.env` and `.ini` (sops writes any format under any name, e.g. `wg0.conf.enc`). `/examples/private-overlay/` has its own CODEOWNERS line.
- A plain deny-list term (the leak scanner's `--denylist`/`HNX_DENYLIST` and the context screener) matches its separator variants: `-`, `_` and `.` no longer count as part of a word, so `name` also matches `name-core`, `name_core`, `name.example` and `name2`, and the separators inside a term match any run of `-`, `_`, `.`, spaces or none (`some-name` matches `some_name`, `some name`, `somename`). A real host name built on a deny-listed word passed every pattern before.
- The `tailscale-acl` target keeps every value on one line: a `tailnet.deploy` exception reason (or any other `tailnet.deploy` value) holding a line break, NUL or Unicode line separator is refused by `deploy_problems` (so `hnx site validate` and `hnx guests render` both refuse it), and the renderer checks each `//` header line itself; a reason with a newline used to end its comment and write raw text into `tailscale-policy.hujson` ahead of the policy.
- `tools/fix_has_test.py --prove` (Q-08) no longer passes a fix it cannot prove: it counted pytest exit 2 on the parent (a collection or import error, e.g. a test module importing a name the fix adds, whatever it asserts) as "fails without the fix", and passed a fix whose only test files were fixtures, `conftest.py` or a non-test file under `tests/`. The parent run now uses `--continue-on-collection-errors` and a junit report and needs at least one failed test; the other cases are reported as "cannot prove" unless the commit carries a `No-regression-test:` trailer.
- The DCO job exempts Dependabot by the pull request GitHub records, not by the commit: the exemption keyed on an author email matching `<n>+<name>[bot]@users.noreply.github.com`, which any contributor can set with `GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL`; now only commits authored as Dependabot in a pull request whose `user.login` is `dependabot[bot]` and `user.type` is `Bot` skip the sign-off.
- The tests that call external linters and validators now run in CI: a new `external-tools` job installs actionlint v1.7.11 and nats-server v2.10.22 (the lab image's version) by `go install`, each module sum pinned and compared before install, and zizmor 1.31.1 from a sha256-pinned wheel, lints this repository's workflows and actions (actionlint; zizmor at medium and above), and runs the private-overlay template and lab-in-a-box tests with `HNX_REQUIRE_TOOLS=actionlint,zizmor,nats-server,docker`, which turns a listed tool's "not installed" skip into a failure (`tests/suite_policy.py`). Before, the deploy templates were never checked by actionlint or zizmor anywhere. dnsmasq (an apt package, not pinnable by version) and playwright for node (a browser download) are still not installed.
- `tools/fix_has_test.py` is no longer counted as a test file: its name ends in `_test.py`, so a fix that touched only the gate passed the "ships a regression test" check, and `--prove` staged the fixed gate on the parent.
- `compose_app` no longer lets docker compose interpolation reach a mount: the template writes every `$` as `$$` in every rendered value (a volume `${X:-/}:/host` became the host root, `${X:-/var/run/docker}.sock` the docker socket, and `environment: {A: "${HOME}"}` the deploy host's value, because compose interpolates the rendered file from the environment of whoever runs `compose up`), the validator refuses a volume holding `$` or a source starting with `~`, and role lint RL014 flags such a bind source in a role's defaults, vars or templates.
- `compose_app` bind sources are an allow-list: a host path must lie under `compose_app_bind_roots` (the application's own directory and data directory by default; a host-wide root such as `/etc`, `/var` or `/srv` is refused) or be listed on its own with a reason in `compose_app_bind_extra`; relative sources and named volumes need neither. The deny-list it replaces let `/etc`, `/proc`, `/dev`, `/boot`, `/root`, `/run/systemd`, the D-Bus system bus, `buildkitd.sock` and `/var/lib/rancher` through. The runtime sockets still need `compose_app_allow_docker_socket`.
- `tools/fix_has_test.py --prove` applies one rule to Python and Go: a test that fails on the parent only because it does not build, collect or import counts as failing only if every such error names a symbol, module or Go package that the fix's own diff adds (read from the parent run's junit report, or from the Go compiler's `undefined:`/`has no field or method`/missing-package errors); any other build, collection or import error, and any error that is not a failing test, is "cannot prove". Before, a Go package that did not build on the parent counted as failing whatever the error, a Python collection error never did, and an import of any name inside a test function counted. A fix whose only test changes are fixtures outside a Go package is "cannot prove" in both languages without a `No-regression-test:` trailer.
