- The `external-tools` job builds actionlint and nats-server with the workflow's one `GO_VERSION` (its own `TOOLS_GO_VERSION`, a floating end-of-life 1.24, is gone) and checks that each installed binary's build info (`go version -m`) names the pinned module, version and sum: `go install` of a package path builds whatever module provides it, so a nested module tagged later would have been built unchecked.
- One required-tools mechanism: tests ask for an external tool through `require_tool()` in `tests/suite_policy.py` (the `go_toolchain` fixture, the docker, nats-server, actionlint and zizmor checks), which returns its path, skips with "<tool> is not installed", or fails where `HNX_REQUIRE_TOOLS` (or the older `HNX_REQUIRE_GO=1`, kept) requires it. It replaces matching the wording of a skip reason afterwards; a gate test keeps the tools CI requires from being skipped any other way. The lab bind guard's first pass runs on its own (`lab_bind_guard_pass1`), so its allow path is tested again without docker, and the guard exits 3 when the local compose has no `config --environment`.
- `tools/fix_has_test.py --prove` runs a Python fix's tests on the parent the way it runs Go's: the test functions and `Test*` methods the commit added or changed (decorators included), and every test that reaches a module-level name the commit changed (a parametrize decorator reading a case list that gained a case, a changed fixture or helper, through other module-level names too), the whole module only when a module-level change names nothing or reaches every test unnamed (`pytestmark`, an autouse fixture, a `pytest_*` hook); a case list that gained a case is no longer missed when the same commit also edits another test in the module (the gate ran only that test and reported a correct fix as passing without it); a neighbouring test that already fails on the parent no longer proves the fix, and the parent run stays short now that it no longer stops at the first failure.
- Deny-list terms no longer join words across whitespace (a hyphenated agent-card name matched the ordinary English phrase, in the leak scanner and in the context screener's redaction): `-`, `_` and `.` inside a term still match any run of those or none, and whitespace matches only where the term itself has it; a term ending in a digit (an address) is not extended by further digits.
- The `regression-tests` job's timeout is 25 minutes (was 15): a push to main re-proves the whole merged range and is never cancelled.
- `compose_app` refuses the application directory itself (`.`, `compose_app_dir`) and its `secrets/` directory as a bind source or `env_file` unless listed in `compose_app_bind_extra`: any service could mount them and read every other service's 0600 env file.
- `compose_app` judges an `env_file` path by the bind-source rule (relative to the project directory, under `compose_app_bind_roots` or listed in `compose_app_bind_extra`; canonical, no `$` or leading `~`): it accepted any string, so `/etc/shadow` or another application's `secrets/<service>.env`, which compose reads on the deploy host as root, reached the container. `docs/role-interface.md` now says that compose interpolates a user `env_file`'s contents from the deploy host's environment (only `docker-compose.yml` is written with `$$`).
- `compose_app` accepts an anonymous volume again (a container path alone, `/var/lib/postgresql/data`): the bind allow-list read it as a host path outside the roots, and the backup manifest listed it as a host path to back up.
- `compose_app_allow_docker_socket` unlocks only the docker socket (`/var/run/docker.sock`, `/run/docker.sock`): it let through every source the runtime pattern matched, including host root, `/var`, `/run`, `/run/user/<uid>` and any path holding `docker.sock` (`/root/docker.sock`); another runtime's socket or state directory now also needs its own `compose_app_bind_extra` entry.
- `compose_app_allow_root_group` lets a service run in the root group alone (`user: 1000:0`, for images built to run as an arbitrary uid in group 0) without granting `compose_app_allow_root`, which still covers both.
- Conformance suites closed against protocol-violating subjects that passed them (16 found in a first review and 17 in a second, each now a registered mutant; `tests/conform/test_conform_protocol_mutants.py` keeps the record): the broker suite checks the allowlist, argument validation and secret redaction on `submit`/`run` as well as `call` (a broker that let anyone queue and run any command passed), that every submit, accepted or refused, is audited (`every-submit-audited`), that `submit` keeps its own copy of the arguments (`submit-keeps-its-own-copy`: changing the caller's mapping afterwards changed what ran, unvalidated), that a run is audited once (`run-audited-once`) and a handler failure on `run` is contained; records pins that a decision is final (`decisions-are-final`: no approving a denied note or denying an approved one), that a name taken in any lane cannot be proposed again (an inbox-only check let a namesake replace an approved note) and that a decision is atomic (`decide-is-atomic`: an approve and a deny raced on one note, under a deterministic interleaving, both succeeded on a copy-then-unlink move); secrets asks for versions 0, negative and not an int, deletes two versions and writes the path again (a tombstoned history came back), lists the parent after delete, checks a bad-version error on a path that holds the value, that `put` and `lease` keep copies (`stored-data-is-a-copy`) and refuses a `nan`/`inf` lease ttl; idp matches scopes exactly and case-sensitively (`""` and `READ` are Forbidden), refuses a scope holding whitespace (`"read admin"` granted `admin` through a space-delimited claim), rejects a revoked token when a scope is required, and refuses a `nan`/`inf` ttl; object-store raises NoSuchKey for `head` of a missing key and NoSuchBucket for `head` and `delete` on a missing bucket (deleting a missing key stays a no-op), deletes in one bucket only, keeps buckets apart when one name extends another (a flat `bucket+key` space passed), keeps `a//b`, `a/./b` and `./x` as keys of their own, and lists no deleted key; events adds `stale-delivery-settles-nothing` (for `ack`, `nack` and `term`, before and after the live delivery is settled) and `new-consumer-starts-at-creation`. A verify pass of those fixes found thirteen more, now registered: events pins that a message one durable acked still reaches the others (WorkQueue retention passed), that dead letters survive a reopen (`dead-letters-survive-reopen`) and that an unsettled delivery counts against `max_deliver` (`unsettled-deliveries-count-toward-max-deliver`: the reference fake redelivered a crash-loop message for ever); secrets that `get` of any version and each lease are the holder's own copy (a cached per-path lease passed); idp that a forgery is `InvalidToken` when a scope is required too (a scope gate on the unverified payload answered `Forbidden`) and that scopes are RFC 6749 scope-tokens matched codepoint for codepoint, with a bare string refused for the scope list (`scopes-are-opaque-tokens`); object-store that keys are opaque (form-decoded `a+b`/`a%2Fb` and case-folded keys passed: `keys-are-opaque`) and buckets are namespaces with a dotted name too (`buckets-are-namespaces`); broker that unnamed-argument redaction does not depend on the name. Clauses the second round had folded into other checks have their own (`put-after-delete-starts-fresh`, `list-omits-deleted-paths`, `deleted-key-not-listed`). Records' `decide-is-atomic` is now a `should`: it explores single-preemption schedules of the adapter's own package (every line, wherever installed), cannot see a race that needs both decisions preempted, never fails a correct decider whose lock times out, and reports "could not judge" instead of passing when it has nothing to pause at.
- Broker audit redaction is the default (the reference `BrokerService` and the reference fake): the value of an argument the command's schema does not name, a misspelt secret argument or any argument of an unregistered command, was written to the audit trail in the clear on its `invalid` or `denied` record, for `call` and `submit`; it is now recorded by name only (`unknown-arguments-redacted`), and its value is keyed with the broker-held digest key in the request digest too: the refused request's `request_digest` was an unkeyed hash of the clear value next to every other input it covers, so a wordlist recovered it. No refusal's message, attributes or cause carries such a value either.
- CI constraints: `filelock` 4.0.12 -> 4.2.4 (CVE-2026-112907, CVE-2026-112913, CVE-2026-112940) and `setuptools` 84.0.0 -> 84.2.1 (GHSA-xmj3-9gf7-h52w), which turned the `pip-audit` job red. Both are exempt from the 14-day constraints cooldown so `make constraints` keeps the fixed versions.
- The status hub's agent summary pages and exports read the activity store only through one released reader (`homelabnext.status.agents.pages.PublicActivity`, held by `SummaryPages`, which has no store), so a new page cannot skip the release (ADR-0033 decision 6). `hnx status serve` builds the public document in one place, and answers `404` for any route with no TLP ceiling declared by tier in code (`homelabnext.status.views.route_ceiling`), whatever site offers it.
- The front door's groups header is split on `|` only. A `,` also split it, so a group whose name contained a comma counted as membership of the viewer or approver group.
- The push ingest checks session and event ownership again inside the write transaction (`ActivityStore.add(owner=True)`, `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`). Two senders racing for one new session could both pass the earlier check, and the session then held both senders' events.
- A one-way zone (`egress.one_way`) could be reached and could query a resolver. `hnx site validate` accepted a `zone_to_zone` allow to `any` from a zone with internet egress, and the adapters rendered it with no destination match, so the internet-facing zone reached the sandbox (and the `tailscale-acl` flows expanded `any` into it): a site with a one-way zone now refuses every allow to `any` (name the zones), and the tailnet flows never expand `any` into a one-way zone. The `linux` router accepted DNS on its input from every VLAN, and the UniFi gateway answers DNS on every network by default, so a resolver on the router carried the sandbox's data out in queries around `egress.internet: deny`: the `linux` adapter now drops DNS (TCP and UDP 53) from a one-way zone's interface ahead of that accept, and the `unifi` adapter renders a `LAN_LOCAL` (and `LANv6_LOCAL`) drop of DNS from its network. The IR carries the one-way zones in `Policy.one_way` (omitted from its digest when empty).
- The private-overlay template's CODEOWNERS owns every path by default (`*` first), so the Ansible inventory and `ansible/known_hosts` (which pick the deploy's targets and the host keys it trusts), `lab/customizations/` (whose hooks add tasks) and any path added later need the owner's review like the workflows do; the named lines stay to say why each matters, and a test checks that every file the skeleton ships has an owner and no rule names none. `checks.yml` gains a last job, `checks`, that needs every other job, runs even when one failed (`if: always()`) and fails unless each succeeded: the docs told the operator to require a `checks` job that did not exist, so the site review tier and the swap gate could be left out of branch protection by accident. The deploy's census pins `module_census_hnx_cli` (run as root on the broker host) and `module_census_pairs_dir` on the command line like `module_census_systemctl_cli`, so the overlay's `host_vars` cannot repoint them; the docs say what is pinned and that the overlay's own `site.yml` can still change what the hosts answer. docs/private-overlay.md: the CODEOWNERS coverage is no longer a stale hand-kept list, `SOPS_AGE_KEY` is on the validation and the deploy steps (not one step only), and an OpenBao token covers one lookup (every reference it names), not one read (also ADR-0021); the template README says to lock `locks/deploy.txt`, which the skeleton does not ship and the deploy requires.
- CI's `trusted-gates` job no longer lets the pull request it checks set up the checker's interpreter: it calls `actions/setup-python` directly instead of the checkout's `python-deps` composite action, calls Python by the absolute path setup-python reports (a `$GITHUB_PATH` shim cannot stand in), and installs pip and the checker's dependencies from the base commit's `constraints/` and `pyproject.toml`. The swap-gate bootstrap fallback, which ran the change's own gate when the base's `modules/cli.py` lacked the string `"swap-gate"`, is gone: a base that cannot run a gate fails the job. `hnx modules review-tier` rates `.github/actions/python-deps/action.yml`, `constraints/bootstrap.txt`, `constraints/ci.txt`, `pyproject.toml` and the release transforms (`homelabnext/status/release_transforms.yml`, `release.py`; ADR-0033 decision 7) human-approve. CODEOWNERS, ADR-0030 and docs/agent.md now say plainly that code-owner review is advisory while `main`'s ruleset requires none, and docs/modules.md no longer states the trusted-gates guarantee as unconditional: the workflow file is still the change's.
- The release workflow signs only a commit the default branch already holds: right after a full-history checkout, before any action or script of the tagged tree, it fails unless the tagged commit is an ancestor of the default branch (whose ruleset admits only commits that passed the required CI checks). A `v*` tag on any other commit was built, attested and released before.

### Added

- Decommission audit: `hnx decommission check|plan NAME`, a plane interface (inventory, playbooks and deploy-chain imports, IaC with a vmid-reuse INFO, live references split from comments and narrative, hypervisor config/firewall/volumes/backup/HA, SIEM roster, monitoring, secret names, DNS, proxy, DHCP) read through adapters with fakes; an unreachable plane is SKIP, never clean, and `--control` runs a positive control on a live guest (`docs/decommission.md`). `tools/decommission_check.py` remains the service-swap scanner.
- Per-system SOP pages: `docs/systems/_TEMPLATE.md`, `hnx docs systems generate|check|template` rendering one prefilled page per service-registry row (human sections left as TODO blocks, preserved on regeneration) and failing on a missing page, an orphan page or a stale generated block (`docs/systems.md`).
- Agent and broker contracts and libraries: `schemas/agent/` (compiled `profile`, `context_manifest`, `pregate_output`, `report_result`, `golden_task`) and `schemas/broker/` (default-deny `manifest`, `handler`, `request`, `decision`, `approval` bound to a request digest), `homelabnext.agent` (profile compiler with ceiling-subset, budget, digest and provenance rules; pure context-pack assembler with stable-before-volatile ordering, reported truncation and pre-gate deferral; `report_result` validator), a reference broker evaluator with a table-driven decision fixture, `hnx agent compile|pack`, the `agent` extra and `docs/agent.md`.
- `homelabnext.events` (`docs/events.md`): build and validate the CloudEvents-shaped envelope against `schemas/events/`, derive ids from event identity, and an idempotent `Publisher` that writes ahead, backs off on transient failures and never duplicates (a crash between send and ack resends the same id and the forwarder reports it as a duplicate); `HttpForwarder`, an in-process `FakeForwarder` and a loopback `FakeForwarderServer`.
- `homelabnext.scope` (`docs/scope.md`): a scope guard for writer tools. The out-of-scope policy is data (`scope: in|out` on guests and services, `vmid_threshold`, `out_tags`, per-id overrides); references are resolved to one canonical logical id before deciding, unknown or ambiguous references fail closed, and a denial is a structured `Decision` and a `homelabnext.scope.denied` event (new schema `events/scope.denied` 1.0).
- Guest schema 1.1 and service schema 1.1 (additive): `scope`; guest `network.firewall_exemption` and `firewall_rules`. The default of `network.firewall` is now `enabled`.
- Firewall on by default (`docs/compute-model.md`): the Terraform renderer always writes the NIC `firewall` flag, validation rejects a flag-off NIC on a firewall-enforcing node without a documented exemption, `_nodes.yml` / `--nodes` says which nodes enforce a firewall, and the new `firewall-rules` target renders a per-guest rules file (inbound drop plus the guest's allow rules) and `firewall/EXEMPTIONS.md`.
- ATT&CK-id validation in the detections layer (P6.5): `homelabnext.detections.attack` loads a per-engine known-ids snapshot (YAML/JSON structured, or plain-text with `# key: value` header), the Wazuh adapter now implements the optional `KnownTechniques` protocol pointing at `detections/attack_snapshots/<backend>.yml`, `python -m homelabnext.detections validate-techniques [--backend wazuh] [--snapshot FILE] [--suggest-parent]` exits 0/1/100 (invariants-runner convention) and emits parent-tagging guidance for unknown sub-techniques whose parent IS known, example snapshot shipped for Wazuh, 33 synthetic tests (no real rule ids), `docs/detections.md` covers the format and the fix pattern.
- Modularity pillar: X-07 and X-08 are now in the example invariants register with their `evidence: plugin` SERVER stubs (every PUBLIC-NOW and SERVER X-criterion from `docs/checklist.md` is now exercised through the runner); invariants X-03 plugin fixed to look at `negative/` (singular, matching the repo layout and `tools/schema_check.py`), build a `referencing.Registry` so cross-schema `$ref` resolve offline, accept `/<major>.<minor>` version pinning in `$id` (the repo convention), load `.md` instances through YAML frontmatter, and exempt `x-defs-only` schemas from the example/negative requirement; regression tests cover the directory bug, the registry bug and the pin-format bug.
- Terraform generator validated against the real `bpg/proxmox` provider (version 0.116.1): `terraform init && terraform validate` are clean on the example-fleet render and a one-guest live plan/apply/destroy cycle on a scoped scratch pool succeeded; the trimmed provider schema is committed as a fixture (`tests/compute/fixtures/bpg_proxmox_schema.json`) and a CI-safe shape test (`tests/compute/test_terraform_proxmox_schema.py`) now rejects any attribute or block the real provider does not accept.
- Self-contained wheel: `homelabnext.paths` is the one place that locates `schemas/`, `profiles/` and `examples/` (env override, then packaged `homelabnext/_data`, then the source tree, with an error listing every place tried); `setup.py` copies the top-level directories into the wheel at build time, a minimal example lab ships as `examples/minimal/`, and the wheel test now installs into a clean venv and runs `hnx site|guests|registry validate` and `hnx custom list` against it.
- Conformance harness: `hnx conform list|run|mutants|report`, a suite format (named checks, severities, features, waivers) that runs against any implementation named by a target file, 14 capability suites with fakes, deliberately broken fakes that each suite must catch, three extended suites (`backup`, `dns`, `edge`), the `capabilities without a passing test` JSON export and invariant X-10 (`docs/conformance.md`).
- Lab-in-a-box (`compose/lab-in-a-box/`): a pinned Docker Compose reference environment within a 4 GB budget (object store, Iceberg REST catalog + Postgres, single-node query engine, event bus, secrets dev mode, notifications, authorisation, fake inference and case servers), `make lab-up|lab-down|lab-smoke`, a smoke script with direct per-service checks, a scheduled `nightly` workflow, and `docs/architecture.md` / `docs/build-order.md`.
- `homelabnext.notify`, `homelabnext.state` and `homelabnext.records`: state-diffed paging with registered recovery pairs and an ntfy-style sender, crash-safe versioned state files with a keyed check API, and the records lifecycle (single validated agent write path, separate operator movers).
- Customizations: schema, loader, linter, plan-only applier, invariant X-09, register view, three synthetic optional profiles and `hnx custom list|check|plan|register` (`docs/customization.md`).
- Customization schema 1.1 (additive): `maintained_upstream` exempts project-shipped profiles from `review_by` expiry; honoured only for the shipped `profiles/` directory, rejected with `CU015` in any overlay; `hnx custom check`, invariant X-09 and the register honour it.
- Schemas, logical-id library, service registry and generators, role interface and linter,
  detections as code, register generators and invariants runner (first wave of workers).
- `hnx` console script (`homelabnext/cli.py`) dispatching to registry, new, gen, detections,
  registers, invariants, ids and schema-check.
- Inference and decision building blocks: model-role resolver with `/v1/models` mismatch probe
  (`homelabnext.registry.models`), fake OpenAI-compatible server (`conformance/fakes`), golden
  tasks (`homelabnext.golden`), decision cascade with impact scan, replay gate, synthetic eval and
  `/v1/systemone` adapter (`homelabnext.decide`); `model_roles` and `decision_spec` schemas 1.1.
- `make check` now runs lint, tests, strict schema check, endpoint and role gates, detections,
  registers, SPDX, leak scan and changelog check; CI runs the same gates and a pinned gitleaks CLI.
- Pinned OCSF 1.3.0 schema subsets (`schemas/ocsf/`) and the `homelabnext.lake` library: `LakeClient` protocol, `FakeLake`, DuckDB/Iceberg/Trino adapters, cassette parity, and commit-then-checkpoint idempotent writes.
- Site/network model: `schemas/platform/site.schema.json`, `homelabnext/network/` (validation, vendor-neutral IR, `linux`, `dnsmasq`, `zonefile` and dry-run `unifi` adapters, `opnsense` deferred) and `hnx site validate|plan|render`, with golden files from a synthetic site.
- Guest (compute) model: `guest.schema.json`, `homelabnext.compute` loader, validation and renderers (Terraform for a Proxmox-style provider, libvirt, cloud-init, Ansible inventory, registry hosts, backup jobs, plan diff) and `hnx guests validate|render|plan`.
- Swap kit: `hnx swap plan|start|status|advance|rollback|record` (nine-step resumable state machine with gates, evidence and a hash-chained `swap_record` schema), `homelabnext.parity` (row, table, schema-drift, event-stream and notification parity), a dual-write helper, the registry flip with its exact inverse, and `tools/decommission_check.py` (`docs/swap.md`).

- Wave-3 integration: `events/swap.step` schema 1.1 (additive: the nine step names of the swap record; 1.0 names stay valid) with `homelabnext.swap.events.step_events`, invariant X-10 in the example register plus its `x10_conformance` entry point, `tools/conform_coverage.py` behind `make conform` / `make conform-check` (part of `make check` and CI) and a nightly `hnx conform run all`, and tests that run a swap between conformance fakes and run `hnx conform list` / `hnx swap plan` from an installed wheel.

### Changed

- Rendered Terraform now states `firewall = true|false` on every guest NIC (previously omitted unless set); the example-fleet goldens were regenerated on purpose (`ct-idp-core.tf`, `vm-infer-core.tf` gain `firewall = true`, `vm-bus-core.tf` gains an exempted `firewall = false`).
- Wave-3 integration: the blanket `compose/**` endpoint allowance is replaced by four file-specific entries, the `swap` extra is PyYAML + jsonschema (what the kit imports), the swap record schema is located through `homelabnext.paths`, and `.gitignore` no longer hides `conformance/secrets/`.
- Wave-2 integration: `lake` extra pins (`duckdb>=1.5`, `pyarrow>=26`, `pyiceberg[sql-sqlite,pyiceberg-core]>=0.12`), package data for example specs, the X-09 invariants entry point, CI matrix with and without the lake extras, `hnx custom check` gate, `role_lint` expands a directory of roles, guests read zones through the network loader, registry merge keeps extra registry fields, schema contract test covers every schema, cross-package integration tests and a wheel-install smoke test.
- Core install is dependency-free; PyYAML, jsonschema and friends are extras.
- Leak scanner accepts exactly the `Claude-Session:` commit trailer shape.

## [0.1.0] - 2026-10-15

### Added

- Repository skeleton: licence and policy files, CI, pre-commit configuration, Makefile.
- Architecture decision log with twelve ADRs (`docs/adr/`).
- Interface index for parallel work (`docs/interfaces.md`) and glossary.
- Generic public-repo leak scanner (`tools/leak_scan.py`) with fixture tests.
